# Cele 697 de capabilități Hermes — status cod

[Statusul sprintului](../HERMES_STATUS.md) · [Reguli și livrări](HERMES_SPRINT.md)

ID-urile H001–H697 sunt poziții în inventarul înghețat, validate prin hash. Folosește căutarea paginii pentru nume/ID sau comanda `show` pentru cerința originală completă. «Audit 07.09» înseamnă moștenit, nu verificat din nou. Dovezile legate sunt fișiere inspectate; simpla lor existență nu dovedește întregul flux și nu afirmă că testele au fost rulate în această livrare.

| <a id="h002"></a>H002 | Run one scripted agent turn from a pipe and get a machine-readable cost report | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Two parts of the row are open. (1) THE SPEND HALF IS A SCHEMA OF NULLS. The hub cannot attribute one turn's cost. \`AgentConfig.model\` defaults to \`google/gemma-4-31b-a4b\` and none of the 18 agents in \`agents/_system/agents.yaml\` declares a model, so \`cost_tracker\` meters every turn at the default price row and reports $0.00; an id it does not know is priced from the \`default\` row with no \`priced\` flag. \`estimated_cost_usd\`, the token counts, \`api_calls\`, \`model\` and \`provider\` are therefore \`null\` with \`cost_basis: "unavailable"\` rather than measured zeros, and a test pins that they never become numbers. (2) A STOP THAT QUEUES NOTHING IS STILL JUDGED BY TEXT, AND ON A LIVE HUB THAT TEXT IS USUALLY REWRITTEN. The hub reports ids only for approvals queued through the gated Tool-RPC path. The tool loop's own stops are recognised only by containment over the reply. A turn routed to a specialist is not handed back verbatim: the orchestrator sends it through synthesis (\`was_synthesized = len(responses) \> 1 or "jarvis" not in responses\`). With a \`jarvis\` agent and a router loaded, \`jarvis.synthesize\` rewrites it through a model; only the fallbacks (no \`jarvis\` agent, no router, a failed synthesis call) produce the \`\[athena\]: …\` join that containment survives. So a specialist's tool-loop stop can come back reworded and exit 0. The table also has no entry for \`THINKING_EXHAUSTED_REPLY\` (agents.core.llm.base): a comment in the table names it, but no row follows. A reasoning model that spent its whole budget thinking is therefore printed as the answer with exit 0. Also open: \`POST /chat\` returns no trace, invocation or session id, so \`session_id\` is echoed only when the caller supplied one; \`-z\` does not silence hub-side logging; there is no \`--output-format\` beyond the existing \`--json\`; a piped body is read whole up to the bound rather than streamed; and \`os.replace\` onto a symlinked \`--usage-file\` path replaces the link with a regular file instead of writing through it. [d1](../agents/cli/nerva.py) [d2](../tests/test_nerva_oneshot.py) [d3](../tests/test_nerva_cli.py) |

| <a id="h042"></a>H042 | Start, check and stop the web UI / headless backend | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Hermes's row is start, check \*\*and stop\*\*, and the stop half is entirely absent: no \`stop\` verb, no pidfile, no ready file. The row cites \`hermes dashboard --status/--stop\` plus backend ready sentinels and a ready file (06-cli-f.md, 16 entries); Nerva has \`nerva status\`, which reads the HTTP surface and so cannot report a hub that is starting or wedged before the port opens, and Ctrl-C. Hermes also signals the conflict as a machine-readable \`BACKEND_PORT_IN_USE\`, where this slice signals it as an exit code and an English sentence — enough for a supervisor, not enough for a caller parsing a status stream. Not delivered either: \`--isolated\`, \`--skip-build\`, \`--no-open\`, the SSH session-token file and owner-nonce, or the interactive auth-setup prompt. \*\*One known limit, in the safe direction:\*\* the probe picks a single address family from the host string while uvicorn binds every address \`getaddrinfo\` returns, so a dual-stack name like \`localhost\` is probed on v4 only and can miss a conflict held on the v6 address — it misses silently, and uvicorn then reports it exactly as it does today. The Windows half of the \`SO_REUSEADDR\` choice is read off asyncio's \`reuse_address = os.name == "posix"\` line and has never been executed here; that test skips off POSIX and the docstring says so. [d1](../serve.py) [d2](../tests/test_h2311_operability.py) [d3](../docs/test-manual/01-environment-and-boot.md) |

Nerva își construiește propriile drivere, deci nu există binar de adus — dar nu există nici instalarea dependențelor opționale dintr-o comandă (pywinauto/uiautomation/pyobjc/PyGObject rămân în sarcina owner-ului), nici un \`doctor\` dedicat operatorului, nici o suprafață în HUD pentru aceeași cale. NEVERIFICAT pe hardware: containerul e headless, deci calea a fost probată doar pe sonde sintetice — niciun panou de sistem real nu a fost deschis și niciun grant real nu a fost observat schimbându-se. [d1](../agents/core/desktop_setup.py) [d2](../agents/cli/nerva.py) [d3](../tests/test_desktop_setup.py) |

Memoria nu e costată: e per sesiune și variabilă, nu cost fix, iar a o raporta ca „fix" ar fi exact tipul de cifră greșită pe care livrarea asta o evită. Schemele de unelte sunt raportate doar când i se dau: verbul nu bootează un registru, iar pe o cutie fără hub secțiunea lipsește și raportul \*\*spune\*\* că lipsește, în loc s-o raporteze ca zero. Cost per toolset (grupat) nu e implementat, doar per unealtă. Neverificat pe serviciu real: nicio cerere nu a fost trimisă — cifrele sunt estimări comparabile cu bugetul intern, nu contorul unui provider. | <a id="h060"></a>H060 | Talking to a busy agent: queue / steer / interrupt, \`/stop\`, and the ack that says what happened | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | No busy-input semantics. A mid-turn message cannot redirect or interrupt the running turn. There is no explicit FIFO \`/queue\`, no \`/steer\` into the running turn and no configurable mode between these with downgrades. \`/stop\` is not a hard kill: it engages the e-stop but does not cancel the running turn or unlock the session. It is also dispatched inside \`handle_input\` (agents/core/orchestrator.py:1901), so it waits behind the same lease. While a turn holds the lease past the wait bound, \`/stop\` itself is answered with \`TURN_BUSY_REPLY\` (named at agents/core/orchestrator.py:3478). There is one busy string, not a set of acks saying whether a message was queued, steered or interrupted. None of the protective demotions exist (running subagent, in-flight compression, photo burst, very fast follow-up merged as the same intent). The lease is the only slot reservation; there is no pending-agent sentinel. \`SlashCommand\` (agents/core/commands.py:61) carries no \`busy_policy\`. [d1](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) [d2](../agents/core/commands.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |
| <a id="h061"></a>H061 | Turn serialization and per-session runtime state (lease, run generations, slot claim, concurrency cap, stale/reaped eviction, warm agent cache) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | No run generation lets a late callback from an abandoned turn detect that it no longer owns the session. The only compare-and-set is the checkpoint-clock commit of a compaction (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3792), and it guards a summary, not turn ownership. There is no active-session slot claimed before the first await and no \`max_concurrent_sessions\` cap: turns on different sessions run concurrently without bound (tests/test_turn_lease.py:88). No watchdog evicts a session whose turn is wedged: its lease stays held until the turn ends, and later messages only get the busy reply. There is no eviction of an in-memory slot whose durable routing row ended, and no single per-session \`SessionState\` object with explicit lifetimes. There is no per-session warm agent cache and no memory-pressure eviction. Agents are process-wide: every session shares \`orchestrator.agents\`, which the compaction-boundary persona refresh walks as a whole (agents/core/orchestrator.py:412). [d1](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) [d2](../agents/core/commands.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |

Reset policy: idle-minutes and daily-at-hour resets, overridable per session type (dm/group/thread) and per channel, applied by rotating the channel session to a fresh id while keeping the old transcript. 2. Reset triggers: configurable texts (e.g. /new, /reset) intercepted in channel_handler before a turn runs; they start a fresh session and acknowledge it. 3. Expiry watcher: a scheduled job that applies the idle/daily policies and drops stale _channel_sessions entries. 4. Stall watcher: track inbound receipt against last turn progress per session and notify the owner once when a message has been pending past a threshold with no progress. 5. Tests for each. Slack and Discord replies are not streamed until one edit stream can be approved as a single \`channel.reply\` decision (H104). The missing typing indicator on an ordinary text turn is counted once, under H683's acknowledge-at-ingest gap. Documented limitation, not solved: taint cannot be tracked through the model; what travels is the coarse fact that the turn read untrusted material, and the channel's \`inbound\` origin is what keeps an action derived from a photo in approval. \`mark_turn_recall_tainted()\` is not called here because it would be a no-op (escalate-only over an already untrusted origin). UNVERIFIED on real services: the suite is hermetic; no real Telegram message received, no local VLM actually called, \`getFile\` and the download exercised only against a fake client. | <a id="h117"></a>H117 | Message batching and debounce (treat a burst as one turn) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A burst of messages is one turn. agents/core/channels/batching.py holds the rules. Coalescer (agents/core/channels/batching.py:57) holds turn pieces per (conversation, sender) and merges them in arrival order, joined by newlines, into one turn. Each piece extends the batch window of WINDOW_SECONDS 0.35 (Hermes' _busy_text_debounce_seconds), never past HARD_CAP_SECONDS 1.0 from the first piece (Hermes' hard cap; deadline at agents/core/channels/batching.py:84). A batch of MAX_PARTS 32 pieces flushes at once. JARVIS_INBOUND_BATCH_MS and JARVIS_INBOUND_BATCH_MAX_MS set the window and cap for every channel (configured(), agents/core/channels/batching.py:114); a window of 0 turns batching off. Telegram's poll loop (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:417) hands each update to _handle_update (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:447). It queues what the update reads as: a text fragment, or what an attachment was read as (a photo's description, a voice note's transcript, its caption). So a client-split long message, an album (one update per photo) and a photo followed by its question each become one turn (_queue_turn, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:526). While pieces are held, the loop polls getUpdates with timeout 0 instead of the 25 s long poll (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:851), so the batch flushes when its window closes. A button tap or an observed group message first flushes what was said before it, and stopping flushes what is held (agents/core/channels/telegram.py:442). Discord and Slack, whose messages arrive as events, use AsyncBatcher (agents/core/channels/batching.py:124). It flushes each batch from its own timer and delivers batches for one key one at a time, under a per-key lock, in order. Discord keys by channel and author (agents/core/channels/discord.py:88); Slack keys by channel, member and thread, and with batching off still returns the handler's answer (agents/core/channels/slack.py:363). A stopping Discord or Slack channel discards held pieces and cancels their timers, as Slack's shutdown already discards its undelivered event queue. The merged text is what the gateway receives, so its prompt-injection scan runs on the whole message. The scan now collapses whitespace first (agents/core/channels/gateway.py:175), so a payload split across pieces, or across lines, reads as the sentence it is. Per-chat order is kept: a flush hands the merged turn over before anything later from that chat is processed. tests/test_h117_message_batching.py (47 cases) covers the coalescer (defaults, order, empty pieces, window, cap, wait on the earliest batch, full batch, off) and the Telegram loop: one poll, across polls within the window, after the window, the hard cap, senders and chats kept apart, a photo and its question, an album, a button tap, an observed message, stop, a full batch, batching off, the voice mark, the environment knobs, and the getUpdates timeout. It also covers AsyncBatcher (window, cap, one at a time, off with its answer, full batch with its timer cancelled, an empty batch, drain and discard, a failing delivery logged), Discord and Slack bursts, their stop, and a split injection payload caught in the merged turn. The per-message tests of the Telegram, Discord and Slack channels run with batching off, since they test each message on its own. There are 61 mutants: 59 killed and 2 equivalent (returning or breaking on a batch that is already gone; a delay of exactly zero). [d1](../agents/core/channels/batching.py) [d2](../agents/core/channels/telegram.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/discord.py) |

| <a id="h130"></a>H130 | Serve the UI under a reverse-proxy sub-path | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Același bundle SPA funcționează la rădăcină sau sub un JARVIS_ROOT_PATH validat, cu reverse proxy care elimină prefixul. Bootstrap-ul serverului, navigarea și istoricul, API/SSE/streaming, chunk-urile lazy, fonturile, manifestul și service worker-ul folosesc prefixul; cache-urile PWA sunt separate per prefix. Paginile /map și /mission-control legate din SPA sunt adaptate. Teste prin aplicația reală și proxy HTTP verifică reload, shortcut-uri, cache offline, auth pe POST și lipsa cererilor la rădăcina greșită; coliziunile /v2 și /api au regresii. Sub prefix, /metrics păstrează eticheta de rută (middleware-ul de prefix întoarce scope\["route"\] către golden-signals, la fiecare mesaj trimis și la eroare), iar căile nerutate rămân \<unmatched\>. Contractul este pentru browser cu proxy prefix-stripping: wrapper-ul nativ cere prefix gol, /v1 și proxy-urile care păstrează prefixul nu sunt incluse. Fixture-ul dezactivează lifespan/provider startup; nu se pretinde deployment sau probă cu provider live. | <a id="h135"></a>H135 | Appearance preference follows the owner across devices | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Browser HUD, ambient and responsive phone layouts synchronize fixed appearance preferences through a narrow user-guarded Settings DB document. Boot reads never write defaults; stale known values coerce to supported choices. Prefix-scoped immutable edit journals preserve offline changes across tabs, server revision CAS prevents stale writes without Web Locks, and acknowledgements remove exact edits even after unmount. Motion defaults to the OS System preference. Two independent real browser contexts recover saved choices after cache loss. Five fixed local UI font choices now share that normalization, revision and offline journal contract. The HUD applies the body/display override at its root; code and explicitly monospace elements retain their independent font stack. Real root/prefix browser tests verify computed families, persistence in a fresh browser, look independence, offline replay and code font invariance. The separate native application now reads the same owner Settings DB document at boot and foreground entry. Its connection-scoped finite cache, explicit-save hydration epochs and serialized current-record writes prevent stale credentials or appearance from returning. All 19 native consumers react without resetting local component state; code fonts remain independent. The accepted cross-client preference contract is implemented; native authoring and a full curated font catalog are not required by this row. Scope remains one configured owner instance. Unsupported native fonts have explicit platform fallbacks; unavailable storage cannot preserve offline choices across process restarts. Native rendering is covered with real React source and host mocks, not device GUI proof. | <a id="h145"></a>H145 | Read the system logs from the UI | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub's own log is readable from the UI. GET /api/admin/logs (agents/core/routers/admin.py:401) is admin-only and read-only; it reads off the event loop and answers a refused filter with 400, its reason and no-store. Behind it, agents/core/log_tail.py reads the file the hub's own setup_logging actually opened, or reports the error it met (FILE_LOG_STATE, agents/core/log.py:19; _writer_state, agents/core/log_tail.py:95), falling back to the configuration for a process that never set logging up ($JARVIS_LOG_FILE, else data_path('logs','jarvis.log') with system.log_to_file: configured_log, agents/core/log_tail.py:86). It lists only that file and its rotations named with ASCII digits, and only regular files by lstat, so a link, a pipe or a directory is never listed (list_files, agents/core/log_tail.py:114; _listed, agents/core/log_tail.py:105); a file is chosen by one of those names, and it is opened without following a link or blocking and checked to be a regular file (_read_window, agents/core/log_tail.py:193). The read is bounded: at most the last 4 MiB, read in one go and closed before parsing, at most 500 records, each at most 8,000 characters (read_path, agents/core/log_tail.py:275). Lines are grouped into records by the log format's header, so a traceback stays with its error: a record over the limit keeps its header and its newest lines behind a "\[… N lines not shown\]" marker, so the error line is always there; lines above the first header read (a record whose header rotated away, a file not in the log format) are records of their own, beyond the window too. Filters: File, a minimum Level (WARNING shows WARNING, ERROR and CRITICAL; a line with no level is not shown under a floor), a Component (a logger and its children) and Lines (1-500), decided on the record as written, so only returned records are redacted. Redaction runs on every returned field, each line whole before anything is cut, so a secret is never split into a visible half: the H495 secret scanner, an assignment rule for names ending like a credential (\`SMTP_PASS=\`, \`?token=\`, \`password:\`), and the known values of the process's secret-named environment variables and encrypted settings, so it masks at least what /api/admin/env masks (_Redactor, agents/core/log_tail.py:159; _known_secret_values, agents/core/log_tail.py:137); when the scanner cannot be loaded nothing is shown, with a note (_redactor, agents/core/log_tail.py:177; _record, agents/core/log_tail.py:247). Terminal escape sequences, C0 and C1 controls and bidirectional overrides are removed (_clean, agents/core/log_tail.py:186). The answer carries the components seen (redacted), the bytes scanned and whether only part of the file was read, and an honest note: file logging off and how to turn it on, a file the hub could not open at start, no file yet, or a file that may no longer be written (read_log, agents/core/log_tail.py:323). The HUD's Logs panel (frontend/src/panels/logs.tsx:54, registered in the Console's Observe group) has the File / Level / Component / Lines filters, colours records by level (ERROR/CRITICAL red, WARNING amber, DEBUG dim), shows them newest first as text, never markup, auto-refreshes every 5 s while switched on and the page is visible, skipping a tick while a read is in flight and stopping on a refused credential, shows LIVE only while the hub writes its file, shows no old records after a refused filter (and goes back to the current log when a rotation is gone), and names the admin token on a 401 or 403. \`nerva logs -n N\` (cmd_logs, agents/cli/nerva.py:1218) reads the same bounded, redacted tail (N counts records), says when only part was read, and refuses cleanly on an unreadable file or a missing redactor. tests/test_log_tail.py (33 cases) covers the resolution and the off state, rotations and refused names, records and tracebacks, the caps, the level floor and component children, the byte budget, a partial last line, bytes that are not UTF-8, control characters, redaction (current file, rotation, traceback line, filtered fields), the route and the CLI; tests/test_h145b_log_review.py (32) covers the first review: a secret across the cut (four offsets, and in a continuation line), a long traceback keeping its error line, links and a link swapped in after listing, a pipe, secret-named environment values and credential assignments (and ordinary numbers left alone), a redactor that cannot load (route and CLI), C1 and bidi controls, filtering before redaction, the writer's error and its path, setup_logging's record, no file yet, plain lines beyond the window, the 4 MiB budget, level-less lines under a floor, component redaction, the CLI's records, window note and -n 0, rotation names, a directory and the 400's no-store; frontend/src/test/logs-panel.test.tsx (14) covers the registration, the query, the admin read, order and colours, each filter, the 5 s auto-refresh, a tick skipped in flight, a hidden page, stale records after a refusal, LIVE only while writing, the off note, no match and a partial read, 401 and 403, a kept component and text-only rendering. The first review (two majors: a secret split by the cut came back in part, and a long traceback lost its error line; ten minors; nine nits) is answered; the feature's 29 mutants were caught but one equivalent, and this round's 26, all but one equivalent (the regular-file check after open, which lseek or read also enforces), two after their cases were added (a pipe, the partial first line). Test manual: PNB-169, PNB-170; the API sweep lists the route. Adapted from Hermes: one log file and its rotations instead of six named files, since the hub writes one log; the level is a floor rather than an exact match. Known limits: a record forged by a multi-line message that contains a header-shaped line is shown as its own record (a property of the log format). [d1](../agents/core/log_tail.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/admin.py) [d3](../agents/cli/nerva.py) |

| <a id="h153"></a>H153 | Webhook subscriptions (create, enable, HMAC secret, delete) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 in five rounds. The first round shipped with H200 (critic note 7). After an adversarial review reopened it, the second round added the receiver switch, an event list and a prompt template. After a second review, the third added where a delivery goes (Hermes' Deliver to and deliver only), a description and a receiver switch that fails closed. A third review (review-H153c) found that as shipped, Deliver to reached none of the owner's channels, and the fourth round made a real send reach each offered channel, tested through the real path down to the channel adapter. A fourth review (review-H153d) found that the fourth round's plain-text rendering let one delivery freeze the hub, and rewrote the sender's file names and addresses; the fifth round sends the text as written, bounds every renderer, and fixes the review's minors. A fifth review (review-H153e) found no major; its three minors (inline code spans still quadratic in two renderers, first-read waiters holding worker threads, eight unpinned behaviours) and six nits are fixed. Backend CRUD is admin-guarded, and the store serves every change without a restart: - GET, POST, PATCH and DELETE /api/webhooks (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:174, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:180, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:193, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:220) list, create, update and delete hooks. - A create that carries a field it does not know (Hermes' skills, deliver_chat_id) is refused with 422, never silently dropped (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:86). - WebhookStore.create (agents/core/webhooks.py:486) mints a token and, when signed, an HMAC signing secret. Both are returned once, and every list masks them (agents/core/webhooks.py:635). A save that fails undoes the change in memory. (1) The receiver card (Hermes' platform-level toggle). The setting webhooks.receiver_enabled is a declared toggle, on by default. It is written through the audited PUT /api/admin/settings/webhooks, and the audit row ends with the value (receiver_enabled=false). A reseed is audited too (agents/core/routers/admin.py:266). When the switch is off, the trigger refuses every delivery with 503. It checks before the body is read and again after authentication, so a switch that lands while a sender streams its body stops that delivery (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:238). - It fails closed (agents/core/webhooks.py:251): - only a literal true is on, and a missing row is the declared default; - when the settings store cannot be read, settings_db.read_setting raises instead of answering the default (agents/core/settings_db.py:627), so the last state read or written stays in force; - a receiver never read is refused with "the webhook receiver's state cannot be read". - It is read off the event loop, and by one caller at a time: while a read runs, every other caller gets the last state at once. A caller with no state known yet (the first read after a start) waits for that read, at most two seconds, so a burst that arrives while it runs is answered, and a read slower than that is still refused, closed. The router waits on the event loop (agents/core/webhooks.py:377): who reads is decided on the loop, only the read runs on a worker thread, and a waiter holds none, so a burst on a slow fresh receiver leaves the default executor free (review-H153e MINOR-2). A caller from a thread waits in it, at most four at once (RECEIVER_FIRST_READ_WAITERS, agents/core/webhooks.py:247); any other is refused, closed. A write, a reset or another store wakes the waiters. A state is used for a second before the store is read again. A read that a write, a reset, a reseed or another store overtook while it ran is dropped, a failed one included. A write in this process is seen at once; one from another process (nerva config set) within the second. - The admin routes keep working, and hooks keep their credentials. - The panel's receiver row shows on, off, or "not read" with the reason: - a failed re-read drops the last state instead of showing it; - an answer is shown unless a newer one already is, so an earlier read never overwrites a newer one and a poller slower than its interval still sees each answer (frontend/src/panel-kit.tsx:20); - ↻ re-reads it; - a stored value that is not true or false is shown as off; - a store with no row yet, which the hub answers with its own 404 naming the category, shows the hub's default, "on · not stored", and can be switched off, because the write creates the row (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:196); any other 404 (a proxy's) is a failed read. - Interop and the /v1 panel show every hook as refused while the switch is off. (2) The New-subscription modal: name, description, agent or workflow target, target id, HMAC signing (the secret, shown once), an event list, a prompt template, where the result goes and deliver only. Create is last in the tab order (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:385). - Events: up to 32 printable names of 1 to 64 characters, no comma, stored stripped and kept once each, ignoring ASCII case (agents/core/webhooks.py:137). - The delivery's event comes from the sender's header (X-GitHub-Event, X-Gitlab-Event, X-Event-Key, X-Event-Type, X-Webhook-Event), else from the payload's event or type (agents/core/webhooks.py:121). It is bounded when read but never cut to the 64-character limit, so a longer name matches no subscription. - A hook with a list runs only the deliveries whose event is on it. Every other delivery is answered 202 with the reason, never run, and counted as skipped (agents/core/webhooks.py:605). That includes a delivery that names no event, and a list a hand edit broke (null, not a list, an item that is not a name). - Template: {a.b.0} reads the JSON payload by key and list index, {event} is the event, {payload} the whole body, and {payload.x} a payload key the template reserves (agents/core/webhooks.py:173). - Nothing else is read. A missing value is empty, a string is used as is and anything else as JSON. - The work is bounded by the caps (agents/core/webhooks.py:157): each path is read once per render, a value is encoded only up to 4,000 characters, and substitution stops at 16,000. - A template that renders nothing is skipped, not run. So is an untemplated delivery with no text, which gets its own reason. - A body over 5 MiB is refused with 413 before it is read whole (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:235). (3) Deliver to and deliver only (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:354, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:366). - deliver is log (the default: the reply stays in the sender's response and the session) or one of the owner's own channels that something receives: telegram, voice or ntfy (agents/core/webhooks.py:70). Names are matched exactly. - web is refused by name: nothing in the hub connects a client to the web channel, so a push there would never arrive. /api/channels/targets and nerva send --list say the same: web is not ready while no client is connected (agents/core/channels/outbound.py:88). - The reply, or a workflow's last step, goes through send_to_target and ChannelManager.send to the owner's own destination (Telegram's owner chat). send_to_target writes an IntentLog row per send. The text is labelled "Webhook \<name\> - \<event\>", and each channel carries the label its own way: - ntfy gets it as its title, folded to printable ASCII and cut to 120 characters; - telegram and voice get it on the first line. - The text goes as written: nothing is rendered and nothing is rewritten, so a file name, an address and a Markdown link arrive as the sender wrote them, and a link shows its address. What hides or reorders text is dropped: every format and control character but a newline, a tab, a zero-width joiner and non-joiner (the bidi controls and marks, zero-width spaces, word joiners, invisible operators, the soft hyphen, the BOM, the TAG plane), and the characters that render blank (the Hangul fillers, the grapheme joiner, the Mongolian selectors, the blank braille pattern); a subdivision flag emoji stays (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:312; review-H153e NIT-1). A lone surrogate becomes U+FFFD (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:338). Telegram sends it with no parse mode, no link preview and no voice note, and ntfy unrendered (agents/core/channels/outbound.py:208, agents/core/channels/telegram.py:338, agents/core/channels/ntfy.py:137). Voice speaks it without the markup. A longer text is cut at 4,000 characters with a note that says where the rest is (the session for a reply, nowhere for deliver-only text). - Nothing on the push path renders, so a delivery costs linear time in its text: one long line cannot hold the event loop. The renderers the rest of the hub uses bound every marker span (a link, bold, italic, a heading line), and put inline code spans back in one pass (agents/core/channels/render.py:52; review-H153e MINOR-1), so one long line costs linear time there too, and a span past the bound is left as written (agents/core/channels/render.py:40). - The hook is read again after the turn. If it was deleted or switched off, or the receiver was switched off or cannot be read, nothing is pushed and the hook says why; a hook moved to another destination is pushed there, labelled with its name as it is then. - Quiet hours (ambient.quiet_hours_start/end, 22 to 7 by default, hours taken modulo 24, the jobs' own reading, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:275) keep every push off the phone and the speaker. An hour setting that is not a number (an infinity, NaN, text) keeps its default, for the jobs too (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:82). The push is noted on the hook, not held for the morning as a job's message is. - A hook makes at most 30 push attempts an hour (agents/core/webhooks.py:422, per process). An attempt the channel refused counts, and the panel says so; a push held by quiet hours spends none. A push over it is noted, not sent. - deliver_only skips the agent: the rendered text goes straight to the destination, and no turn runs. It needs a channel: deliver-only with the log is refused on create and on PATCH, with the reason (agents/core/webhooks.py:101). A record from before that rule can still be switched off, and its delivery says the text was dropped. - A workflow delivers its last step's output, or nothing when the run failed or its last step returned an error, never an earlier step's output (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:439). The last step is the last to run, in the pipeline's batch order, not the last listed, and a run that stopped early delivers the last step that ran (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:415). An output the router cannot read is recorded, and nothing is pushed. - Each delivery's outcome (channel, ok, reason, time) is kept on the hook and shown in its detail pane, and the response carries it too. Every exit after a call is counted is recorded, a missing or invalid workflow and a failed turn included (agents/core/webhooks.py:695). - A delivery that fails is recorded and answered 200. Every answer is encodable whatever the sender or a step put in it: a lone surrogate becomes U+FFFD, a NaN or an infinity null, and anything else JSON cannot carry (a date, bytes, a set, an object) its text (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:464), so a delivery is never a 500 after its push. A workflow hook answers with ok, the steps that ran and the delivery, never the run's context, which holds the sender's text and every step's output. A failed turn itself still answers 500, so the sender's retry runs it again. (4) The subscriptions list with per-route enable/disable and delete. PATCH /api/webhooks/{hook_id} (agents/core/webhooks.py:553) takes any of enabled (a strict boolean), events, prompt, deliver, deliver_only and description, and changes them in place. It refuses: - null (and its schema advertises none); - an empty body; - an unknown field; - a destination the create would refuse. The schema names the four destinations as an enum. A hook switched off keeps its token and secret. The trigger refuses it with 403 after authentication, from the live record, before the event filter. Each row has an on/off switch, an HMAC or token badge, an event-count badge, the destination (none for the log) and delete with an inline confirmation. The detail pane shows the description, the calls, the skipped deliveries, where the result goes, how a push goes (on a hook that pushes) and how the last delivery went. It edits all of them and opens at the hook's own destination. After a save focus returns to the hook's row, or to the create form when the hook is gone. The legacy /v1 panel lists, creates and deletes through adminFetch, and shows the receiver as the hub reads it and each destination (agents/web/static/tools.js:341). (5) The restart banners. Nerva needs no restart for either kind of change: the receiver switch is a setting read at most a second old, and hook CRUD is served live. Audit: create, switch, update and delete each write a SecurityEvent (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:142). It names: - the hook id; - the target as a JSON string; - the event list; - the template's length (never its text); - the destination and deliver only; - the signed and enabled flags. It never records the token or secret. A switch alone is recorded as enable or disable; any other change is an update. The receiver switch is audited by the settings route, with its value, and a choice setting's value is in its row (a toggle, a select, a number, a slider). A nerva config set from the shell writes no audit row (H378). Deliveries: an agent-target delivery goes through handle_input(channel="webhook"). origin_for_channel (agents/core/action_origin.py:36) classifies that channel as inbound, so the payload is tainted and the kernel's GRANT-\>QUEUE escalation applies. A workflow target runs its pipeline (agents/core/routers/workflows.py:37) under the inbound action origin. The sender waits for the push as well as the turn. A retried delivery that carries an Idempotency-Key is not run again; it gets the first outcome back (H659, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:551). One without a key runs again. Adapted, not copied: - Deliver to: Hermes offers log, Telegram, Discord, Slack, email and a GitHub comment. Nerva offers the log and the owner's own telegram, voice and ntfy, and refuses the rest by name with a 422 (agents/core/webhooks.py:75): - Email is never an outbound side effect of an inbound delivery (the rule ChannelManager.send keeps). - A GitHub comment writes to someone else's system, so the hook's agent proposes it through the governed write-back path, one approval each. - Discord and Slack are reply-only transports here, with no home channel to address. - The web HUD has no push receiver yet. - Per-subscription skills: a Nerva hook targets an agent or a workflow, and the agent brings its own skills to the turn. Hermes' own modal does not expose skills either. Tests: - tests/test_h153_webhook_subscriptions.py (54 cases) covers the receiver, the event filter, the template and the update. - tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py (35) covers the switch, the audit and the in-flight race. - tests/test_h153b_webhook_delivery.py (63) covers the second round with fakes for the send and the clock. - tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py (80, the fourth round) runs the real path, the router through send_to_target and ChannelManager to a recording adapter per channel. It covers: - each channel (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:104), with and without a turn; - the real quiet-hours rule on a fixed clock (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:273); - the real Telegram adapter's HTTP body (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:229); - fail-soft delivery (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:319); - a switch thrown during the turn (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:455); - the workflow rule (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:534); - the push cap, the single-flight receiver (tests/test_h153c_webhook_sends.py:605), and the audit of free text. - tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py (61 cases, the fifth round) covers: - a long line of brackets pushed at once, and the text as written on Telegram and ntfy (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:67); - every renderer in linear time on one long line (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:129); - the hidden characters, the first-read burst (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:151), the workflow answer and the encodable answers (tests/test_h153d_webhook_review.py:253); - the last step as run, the unreadable receiver after the turn, a Telegram refusal, the hour of the allowance, quiet hours and the allowance, the live label, an overtaken failed read, a slider's audited value, the hour settings and web's readiness. - tests/test_h153e_webhook_review.py (49 cases, the sixth round) covers the renderers' scaling on inline code spans, the restore's escaping, an ordinary long link, the waiter cap and wake, a burst through astate holding one thread, every hidden character and what stays, the cut boundary, the quiet-hours end and the encodable answer; frontend/src/test/panel-state.test.tsx (2) a failed poll shown while loading. - frontend/src/test/webhooks-panel.test.tsx has 55 cases, frontend/src/test/use-api-sequence.test.tsx 4 (a poller slower than its interval, loading until the newest answer, an earlier answer never over a newer one, another address), frontend/src/test/interop-live-honesty.test.tsx 14 and the /v1 tests/frontend/tools.test.js 18. Mutation: - The fourth round: 92 mutants of its guards and of the reviewer's 35 unpinned survivors (70 backend, 20 panel and Interop, 2 /v1). Three no longer applied to the final code; the reviewer replayed the 89 that apply, and each is caught. - The fifth round: 40 mutants of its guards (32 Python, 8 HUD), each run against its own suites. All 40 are caught, one of them (the answer's dict keys) by a test added for it. Four were first written malformed and were rewritten before they counted. - The fifth review's own 68 mutants left eight unpinned behaviours (five hidden characters, the cut boundary, the quiet-hours end, the link bound); each has a test now. This round's 13 mutants (12 Python, 1 HUD) were all caught. - The fourth review's own 104 mutants left 20 survivors: seven equivalent, one a strengthening (catching an infinite hour, now the code) and twelve unpinned. Each of the twelve now has a test. [d1](../agents/core/routers/webhooks.py) [d2](../agents/core/webhooks.py) [d3](../agents/core/settings_db.py) |

| <a id="h156"></a>H156 | Edit an agent's persona and description from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | An agent's persona can now be edited in the UI and is live on its next turn. That is Hermes' persona editor, over the version history Nerva already kept. Until now the history was a side record: a commit or rollback in the Prompts panel changed a JSON file while the agent kept its old persona. agents/core/soul_edit.py holds the one path an edit takes, apply_soul (:152). (1) Refusals. An agent the hub has not loaded is refused (404; agent_folder, :100, returns an enumerated folder name in the id alphabet, so no request value reaches a path). So is text over MAX_SOUL_BYTES (256 KiB of UTF-8, :40; 413), safe mode (:171; 409, because overlays are not read there, H275) and a persona the H387 guard would drop (:174; 422 with the verdict). guard_verdict (:60) runs the same scan and cap as Agent._load_soul, and GET /api/agents/{id}/soul now reports it through the same function. A line the guard only quarantines is applied, and the verdict names it. (2) Nothing live is lost. The first edit keeps the file on disk as v1 ("on disk before the first edit"). A file changed on disk since the last version, such as a hand edit, is kept as its own version ("on disk, changed outside the editor") before it is replaced (:185). An agent with no persona file has nothing to keep. (3) Written where the model reads it. The text goes to the owner's overlay (overlay_path, :115): souls/\<id\>/SOUL.local.md in the data home, else the repo-local SOUL.local.md, never the shipped SOUL.md. The write is atomic (_write_atomically, :126: temporary file, fsync, replace; a failure leaves no temporary file and records no new version). (4) Versioned, then live. The text is committed (:195; the same text twice is one version). The agent re-reads its persona (_load_soul, :197), and the result says whether the running agent now reads the written file. (5) Audited without the text. A SETTINGS_CHANGE row names the agent, the version and its hash. A plain commit and a history-only rollback are audited the same way (record_version, :224; agents/core/routers/admin.py:561, :616). Surfaces. PUT /api/admin/agents/{id}/soul {content, message} (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:132, admin). POST /api/admin/prompts/{id}/rollback applies the version through the same path for a loaded agent (agents/core/routers/admin.py:602); the shared contract _identity (served from IDENTITY.local.md) and keys that are no loaded agent keep the history-only rollback. Description. A description: field in the SOUL front-matter is the agent's one-line description (description_of, :84, and description_in, :89: one line, at most 400 characters). It is listed on GET /agents and the SOUL read (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:179, :124) and is never part of the prompt. POST /api/admin/agents/{id}/description/draft (agents/core/routers/agents_api.py:150; draft_description, :238) asks the strict-local model for one paragraph from the live persona and saves nothing. With no local model it is a 503, never a cloud call (:255). HUD. The Dossier has an Edit persona button that opens the live SOUL in frontend/src/soul-edit.tsx (SoulEditor, :49, mounted at frontend/src/modes.tsx:96). It offers Preview against the live text, Apply, Draft description with Use it (withDescription, :15, writes the front-matter line), and the hub's refusal in words (refusalText, :32). The Dossier also shows the description. The Prompts panel gets apply beside commit (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2737) and says when a rollback is live. Tests: tests/test_soul_edit_route.py (29) covers the apply, the seed and hand-edit versions, deduplication, blocked and quarantined text, the audit row, size, ids, an unloaded folder, admin, the repo overlay, safe mode, a failed write, no store, a reload that misses the file, rollback (live, to the shipped text, refused, missing, history-only keys, audited), a plain commit, the description (listed, bounded, never prompted) and the draft (a proposal, strict-local, empty). frontend/src/test/soul-edit.test.tsx has 6. tests/test_h10_22_prompt_versioning.py now asserts that the rollback is live, in a throwaway data home. 61 mutants: 58 caught, eight of them after survivors got cases. Two survivors of the first-edit-only seed led to keeping hand edits as versions; that code's own five mutants are caught. The fsync is a crash-only property. [d1](../agents/core/soul_edit.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/agents_api.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/admin.py) |
| <a id="h157"></a>H157 | Edit every configuration key from the UI | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Every configuration key is reachable, searchable, portable and resettable from the UI. The backend it builds on: \`GET /api/admin/settings\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:73) returns every category, and the per-category PUT (agents/core/routers/admin.py:157) validates with \`validate_category\` and answers 422 before any write, auditing the changed key names and the new value of a choice only (\`_audited_values\`, agents/core/routers/admin.py:111). New in H157: \`GET /api/admin/settings/export\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:78, registered before the \`/{category}\` read so it is never taken for one) returns the declared settings as a \`nerva-settings/1\` document with an attachment disposition, leaving out every secret (encrypted at rest, or named like a token, secret, password, client id or API key), \`mcp.servers\` (it holds credentials by design) any URL that carries a password (\`http://user:pw@host\`, in a list too), and any free-form value the H495 secret scanner would mask (or every free-form value, when the scanner cannot run), and naming each left-out setting with its reason (export_settings, agents/core/settings_db.py:938; is_secret_setting, agents/core/settings_db.py:884; _looks_like_credential, agents/core/settings_db.py:917; _url_password, agents/core/settings_db.py:901). \`POST /api/admin/settings/import\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:204) takes such a document (at most 1 MB and 1,000 keys, the body counted as it streams in, so a chunked upload is bounded too: _bounded_body, agents/core/routers/admin.py:191). The import and the reset take only an \`application/json\` request (415 otherwise, a type a browser must pre-check before sending) and refuse one the browser marks cross-site (403), since the admin guard trusts loopback on a default install (_refuse_cross_site_write, agents/core/routers/admin.py:177); every reply is no-store, and only a JSON \`true\` makes a dry run. A NaN, an Infinity or nesting too deep to store is refused (422), never stored, an \`mcp.servers\` entry needs a name (validate_category, agents/core/settings_db.py:778, via _mcp_servers_problem, agents/core/settings_db.py:807; the loader skips a nameless entry too, MCPManager.load_from_config, agents/core/mcp/client.py:814), and 2000 and 2000.0 are the same number, so no change (_same_value, agents/core/settings_db.py:1020). In the document every key must be declared and passes the same \`validate_category\` a single write does, so a posture flag or an out-of-range value is refused like any other; one refusal and nothing is written (422 with every reason) (plan_import, agents/core/settings_db.py:968). Only the keys whose value differs are written, in one sqlite transaction, all or nothing, secrets encrypted as a single write encrypts them (apply_import, agents/core/settings_db.py:1054), with one audit row naming the settings (and the new value of a choice); \`"dry_run": true\` answers what would change, a secret's values shown as \`(secret)\` and a credential-shaped free value as \`(hidden)\`, so the preview never echoes one (describe_changes, agents/core/settings_db.py:1028). \`POST /api/admin/settings/{category}/reset\` (agents/core/routers/admin.py:240) puts one category back to its declared values and names the keys that moved, with an audit row; it keeps the category's secrets and names them (kept, reset_kept, agents/core/settings_db.py:1083) and names the settings the product posture still forces (overridden, posture_overridden, agents/core/settings_db.py:1090), where the global reseed (agents/core/routers/admin.py:261) was the only reset (reset_category, agents/core/settings_db.py:1103). The v2 console SettingsPanel (frontend/src/gap.tsx:2645; widgets typed by settingsField, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2631) now has a search across every category matching a setting's category.key or label, with a match count and an honest no-match line (settingMatches, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:24); a reset per category heading (only one with declared rows) that asks first, by a second click that names how many settings go back and moves the focus to itself, drops the category's unsaved edits once it runs and names what was kept and what the posture still forces (ResetCategory, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:43); edits hidden by the search are counted beside the save; and a MOVE A CONFIGURATION section (SettingsTransfer, frontend/src/panels/settings-tools.tsx:92) that downloads the export and names what the hub left out, and imports a pasted or chosen file by showing the hub's dry run (each change old → new) and applying it only on a second step, listing every reason on a refusal; a pasted \`dry_run\` is dropped so the apply step is never a dry run, the text is held while the hub answers, and the file picker is reachable from the keyboard. The standalone /admin app's GlobalConfigPage (agents/web/static/admin.js:447) is unchanged, and \`nerva config list\|get\|set\|check\` (cmd_config, agents/cli/nerva.py:803) still edits single keys offline. tests/test_settings_transfer.py (28 cases) covers the export (every declared key either exported or named as left out, secrets, client ids, mcp.servers, a token-shaped value, a scanner that cannot run, the stored value), the import (an export imports back as no change, valid changes, every refusal a single write makes, nothing written on one refusal, one transaction, the posture flag, a secret encrypted and never echoed, the bound), the per-category reset, and the routes (admin only, the export not read as a category, preview then apply with one audit row, every reason on a refusal, no row when nothing changes, reset and 404); tests/test_h157b_settings_review.py (30, the first review) covers the JSON-only and cross-site refusals, URL passwords, a credential in a list, the hidden preview, NaN, Infinity and deep nesting, nameless MCP servers, number equality, secrets kept on a reset, the posture's settings named, the change listeners, the 1 MB bound for a sized and a chunked body and the JSON-only dry run; frontend/src/test/settings-tools.test.tsx (12) covers the search, the two-step reset, the export note, the dry-run preview and the second-step apply with the admin credential, a refusal's reasons, text that is not JSON, an import that changes nothing, a reset that drops unsaved edits and names kept secrets, edits hidden by the search, a pasted dry run never applied and the keyboard-reachable file button. 25 backend mutants, all caught (one after its case was added: a scanner that cannot run). The first adversarial review (review-H157: 2 major, 7 minor, 12 nit) found that a page the owner visited could post a plain-text import to a loopback hub, and that a URL password was exported; all are fixed, and its 18 mutants were all caught but one equivalent (two after their cases were pinned). Test manual: PNB-171, PNB-172; the API sweep lists the three routes. Adapted from Hermes: no raw YAML editor (the typed widgets and the JSON import cover it, and a free-form editor would bypass the per-key widgets), and export/import are JSON documents rather than a Form/YAML toggle. | <a id="h161"></a>H161 | Warn when the box is running out of memory or disk | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The memory/disk warning Hermes shows is here: ranked conditions, only the worst undismissed one on screen, a suspected OOM restart, dismissal per boot, clearing on confirmed recovery, and a watch that does not depend on autonomy. agents/core/resource_pressure.py holds it apart from the autonomy observer, whose thresholds it reuses (85 % elevated and 95 % critical for memory and disk: DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS, :39, pinned to observer.DEFAULT_THRESHOLDS). (1) Ranking. CONDITIONS (:38) orders disk_critical \> memory_critical \> oom_restart_suspected \> disk_elevated \> memory_elevated. PressureMonitor.snapshot (:259) answers every raised condition in that order, with its level, percent, since and whether it is dismissed, plus the worst one not dismissed. Disk covers every watched volume, and the entry names the paths near or over the line. (2) Volumes. take_sample (:81) reads psutil memory and each distinct watched path once (watched_paths, :283: the filesystem root and the data home). A reading that fails is None, never a healthy 0 %, and changes nothing. (3) Hysteresis. Each resource's _Held.update (:109) raises at once. It clears, or steps critical down to elevated, only after RECOVERY_SAMPLES (3, :40) samples in a row under the line by RECOVERY_MARGIN (5 points, :41). A reading just under the line is not counted and restarts the count, so a flapping reading neither clears nor re-raises. (4) Suspected OOM restart. A small state file in the data home (_write_state, :159, atomic through a temporary file, left behind on no failure) holds the boot id (current_boot_id, :69: the kernel's /proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id, else one from psutil's boot time), the memory level, the dismissals and a clean-shutdown mark. start (:172) finds a run on the same boot that ended without the mark while memory was elevated or critical, and raises oom_restart_suspected naming that level. It logs a warning, and says "suspected" because the kernel's log is not read. stop (:191) writes the mark. The web lifespan calls start before the hub serves and stop as the very first teardown step, so a later step that hangs cannot make a clean stop look like a kill (agents/web.py:642, :649). (5) Dismissal. dismiss (:269) takes a condition and this boot's id. It refuses another boot's id, an unknown condition or one not raised. The dismissal persists across a restart on the same boot; a new boot re-arms it, and so does the condition's recovery, because a new episode is a new warning. Unknown names in the file are dropped. (6) Sampling apart from autonomy. SchedulerService.schedule_pressure_monitor (agents/core/scheduler_service.py:431) samples every 60 s on the hub's scheduler, so autonomy off or ESTOP engaged does not stop it. The route samples on demand when the last sample is over a minute old (fresh, :226). Samples from the route's worker thread and the scheduler are folded in one at a time under a lock. (7) Surfaces. GET /api/system/pressure and POST /api/system/pressure/dismiss {condition, boot_id} are both user-guarded (agents/core/routers/pressure.py:30, :36; 409 on a refused dismissal). The HUD's frontend/src/pressure-banner.tsx is mounted under the power chip (frontend/src/app.tsx:514). It polls the route, shows only the worst undismissed condition in one sentence (an alert for critical and a suspected OOM restart, a status for elevated), and dismisses it for this boot. Tests: tests/test_h161_resource_pressure.py (40) and frontend src/test/pressure-banner.test.tsx (6). They cover the ranking, the volumes, the hysteresis (a margin, a near reading, a step-down, an unreadable sample), the dismissal (this boot, stale boot, unknown, re-armed by a new boot and by recovery, persisted), the OOM suspicion and each of its three conditions, the state file (atomic, damaged, unwritable, failed replace, unknown names), the boot id, concurrent samples, the route, the guards, the scheduler job and the lifespan order. 50 mutants: 47 caught after ten survivors got cases. m11 (raise on \> instead of \>=) is equivalent: a same-level reading resets the count on both paths. The since line was simplified rather than kept as an equivalent. m23 (writing in place instead of the atomic replace) is a crash-consistency property that only a crash mid-write shows. [d1](../agents/core/resource_pressure.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/pressure.py) [d3](../agents/core/scheduler_service.py) |

| <a id="h165"></a>H165 | In-app documentation | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built on this branch. Hermes has a Documentation page inside its dashboard with an "open in a new tab" affordance. Nerva's written material (the user guide, FLAGS.md on what each posture flag costs, and the privacy and camera-privacy notes) was only in the repository. The Dockerfile copied it into the image, but nothing served it. (1) The route. agents/core/routers/help_docs.py is registered in agents/web.py (agents/web.py:1520) and user-guarded on the router (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:27). - GET /api/help/docs (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:103) lists the allowlist: slug, title, whether the file shipped, and each document's sections. - GET /api/help/docs/{slug} (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:113) returns the Markdown text, capped at MAX_DOC_CHARS with a truncated flag (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:121). - The allowlist is the fixed DOCS table (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:31): user-guide, flags, privacy, camera-privacy. A slug is only ever a key into it, never joined into a path, so an unknown slug, '../' or encoded traversal is 404. - A file must be a regular file under the install root; a symlink is refused (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:85). A file missing from an install reads as "not available", not an error. - Responses are no-store. (2) The page. DocsPanel (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:37) is registered in the console's Start group (frontend/src/console-routes.ts:6), in PANEL_COMPONENTS (frontend/src/gap.tsx:4810) and in the desktop app's route allowlist (desktop/src-tauri/hud-routes.json:49). - It lists the documents, disabling one not shipped, and renders the chosen one through the new shared Markdown component (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:98). - That component emits React elements only (no innerHTML path): headings with anchors, code fences, pipe tables, lists, quotes and rules, plus bold, italic, inline code and links. A link renders only for http(s) or a same-origin path, in a new tab with rel=noopener noreferrer (safeHref, frontend/src/markdown.tsx:25). Raw HTML in a document stays literal text. There is no regex lookbehind, so older Safari can parse the bundle. (3) Open in a new tab. Every document is a standalone deep link, /v2/console/docs?doc=\<slug\>\[#section\] (docHref, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:15). The panel offers it as "open in a new tab ↗" with rel=noopener noreferrer (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:67), and a #section is scrolled into view (frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:49). (4) The flag's cost where it is flipped, per the row rationale. - Each document's sections carry the names their headings document. The hub computes them with heading_anchor (agents/core/routers/help_docs.py:44), which one shared fixture pins character for character to the renderer's headingAnchor (frontend/src/markdown.tsx:94). - The Settings panel links every setting FLAGS.md documents to its section with a "cost ↗" beside the toggle (sectionIndex, frontend/src/panels/docs.tsx:20; frontend/src/gap.tsx:2648, frontend/src/gap.tsx:2699). - The security posture panel links to the flags document (frontend/src/gap.tsx:1020). The route/auth/OpenAPI snapshots, the HUD parity rules, the API sweep chapter and frontend/src/api/schema.gen.ts are regenerated. Tests: - tests/test_help_docs_route.py, 25 cases: - the list and each allowlisted document (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:35, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:49); - seven off-list and traversal URLs, all 404 (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:67); - the guard (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:71); - a missing file, the cap and a symlink (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:80, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:88, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:94); - the shared anchor fixture and the section index (tests/test_help_docs_route.py:109, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:114, tests/test_help_docs_route.py:122). - frontend/src/test/markdown.test.tsx: structure, snake_case kept as text, hostile HTML inert, safe links only, no raw-HTML sink and no lookbehind in the source, the same anchor fixture, heading ids. - frontend/src/test/docs-panel.test.tsx: registration, rendering, switching and truncation, deep links to a doc and to a section, and the Settings cost link. All 1,346 vitest cases pass, as do tsc, the e2e typecheck and the build. [d1](../agents/core/routers/help_docs.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../frontend/src/markdown.tsx) |

| <a id="h182"></a>H182 | Keep the machine awake through a turn, and be aware of power/battery state | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva keeps the machine awake while a turn runs, reads and streams its power state, and eases off on battery, as Hermes does. agents/core/power.py holds one shared power assertion while at least one turn runs (KeepAwake, refcounted). It is off by default and turned on with JARVIS_KEEP_AWAKE=1 (keep_awake_enabled). The mechanism is the platform's own (backend_name, backend_argv): caffeinate -i -w \<hub pid\> on macOS, systemd-inhibit --what=idle:sleep --mode=block around tail --pid=\<hub pid\> on Linux, so both helpers end with the hub even when it is killed, and SetThreadExecutionState on Windows. The helper is started in its own process group (start_new_session) and released by signalling that group while its leader is still unreaped, so the child systemd-inhibit starts ends too; a helper that already exited is reaped by its handle, and one that ignores SIGTERM is killed after one second. A missing binary or mechanism never fails a turn and is reported (status error), as is a helper that exits on its own. Every turn entry holds it: Orchestrator.handle_input, handle_input_stream and the one-shot process take it at the start (hold_for_turn) and drop it in their finally (release_for_turn), so a reply, an error and a cancel all release it; the lifespan releases every hold at shutdown (agents/web.py) and atexit does at exit. It is gated as host.control (authorize_keep_awake), in the llm_control order: the owner's system-control permission, the host-control contract, whose action list now names power.keep_awake (agents/core/autonomy/remediation.py), an Action Kernel GRANT (the kernel must be on; QUEUE, DENY and anything that is not a GRANT refuse), then a durable audit row (no audit log, or a refused row, refuses too). The action carries the fixed trusted origin, since keep-awake is the owner's switch and not the content of the turn that asks first. It is asked once per process; a refusal keeps it off until the next start and is named in the status. The power state comes from psutil.sensors_battery (read_battery: on battery, percent, plugged). A resume from sleep is seen as the wall clock running more than 60 s ahead of the monotonic clock between two reads (PowerMonitor.tick), which stops while Linux and macOS sleep, and is shown for ten minutes. The scheduler reads it every minute (SchedulerService.schedule_power_monitor, skipped under JARVIS_TESTING), so a resume is noticed without anyone asking. GET /api/power answers the power state, the deferral threshold, whether background work is being deferred and the keep-awake status (enabled, active, holders, backend, refused, error); GET /api/power/stream pushes the same payload to a connected client on every change, with a keepalive while it does not change (agents/core/routers/power.py). Both are user-guarded. Battery-aware throttling: below system.battery_defer_percent (a settings row, 50 by default; 0 never, 100 always on battery) on battery, or on battery with an unknown charge, the heavy background jobs skip their run and return deferred_on_battery, which scheduler health records as skipped: memory maintenance, the tech scout, the learning-loop promotions, prompt evolution and the WorldView KG sync (the last three through SchedulerService wrappers the scheduler now registers). An unreadable power state lets the job run. Nothing the owner asks for directly is deferred. The HUD shell shows a PowerChip (frontend/src/power-chip.tsx): on battery with the charge and whether background jobs wait, a wake from sleep, and keep-awake holding, ready or refused with its reason; a plugged-in machine with keep-awake off shows nothing. It polls GET /api/power every minute and follows the stream where the browser can attach to it. The desktop shell turns webview background throttling off for both windows (desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs, background_throttling), so a HUD in the background keeps reading a streaming reply instead of being suspended and having its turn cancelled; NERVA_DESKTOP_BACKGROUND_THROTTLING=throttle or suspend asks for it back and an unknown value is named and ignored (desktop/src-tauri/src/throttling.rs). macOS 14+ honours the policy; Linux and Windows webviews ignore it. The binding-writer inventory's pinned lines in scheduler_service.py move with the file. Tests: tests/test_h182_power.py (76), including a real helper process whose own child is ended by the release, and rustc unit tests of throttling.rs; src/test/power-chip.test.tsx (6). 100 mutants: 98 killed after five cases were added; the other two were redundant checks, removed. [d1](../agents/core/power.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/power.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h200"></a>H200 | Webhook subscription management surface | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 together with H153 (critic note 7: one spec, one panel), then fixed after an adversarial review. The v2 console has a Webhooks panel, WebhooksPanel (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:186), registered in the Interop group (frontend/src/console-routes.ts:50; frontend/src/gap.tsx:16). Interop mode's WEBHOOKS list links to it by an accessible name (frontend/src/modes2.tsx:341). Every panel call sends the admin credential. The backend stays admin-only CRUD: list, create, switch and delete all carry Depends(admin_guard) (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:174, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:180, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:193, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:220). Interop mode is built from its four sources alone (hydrateInterop, frontend/src/api/live.ts:126). All four (A2A peers, MCP servers, widgets, webhooks) are admin-only routes and are fetched with the admin credential, and so is the webhooks settings category: while the receiver switch is off every webhook row reads "receiver off", and a switch it could not read is said under WEBHOOKS (frontend/src/api/live.ts:119, frontend/src/modes2.tsx:342). A section whose source failed is empty and says "not connected", and an empty real list says "none" (Section, frontend/src/modes2.tsx:302); the demo seed's rows are never shown under the LIVE chip. When no source answers nothing is overwritten, so the mode stays "Not connected" and DEMO keeps its seed. The review found that fetching only the webhooks list with the credential had marked the whole mode LIVE over seeded peers, MCP servers and widgets. The five accepted parts: (1) Create dialog: name, agent or workflow target, target id and an HMAC-signed toggle, and, since H153, a description, an event list, a prompt template, where the result goes and deliver only, posted to POST /api/webhooks. A refusal shows the hub's own reason, and an empty target is refused before any request. The create button is held while the call is pending and while a new hook's credentials are on screen, so a second hook cannot replace a secret before it was saved. (2) Created result, shown once (Reveal, frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:144): the absolute trigger URL (triggerUrl, frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:51: the page origin, the base path and the encoded id), and the token, or the signing secret for a signed hook, each with a copy button that says "copied" in a polite live region, plus the header the sender must send. Only the warning sentence is an alert, so a screen reader does not read the secret aloud. On a loopback origin it says a sender elsewhere needs an address it can reach. The secret lives only in the panel's state and is dropped on "I have saved it" (focus returns to the create form) or unmount. The list never carries it: WebhookStore masks the token to a 4-character hint (agents/core/webhooks.py:635). (3) Detail pane: clicking a hook expands its trigger URL, auth header (with the token hint), calls, last call and creation time, the deliveries its event list skipped and the last event skipped, and an editor for its event list and prompt template (H153). The row shows target_type:target, an HMAC or token badge, an on/off badge and an event-count badge. (4) Delete with confirmation: "delete…" arms an inline confirmation that explains the sender's credential dies with the hook and moves focus to "delete for good", which sends DELETE; "keep it" returns focus to "delete…". Each row button's accessible name includes the hook's name. A row's buttons are held while its call is pending, and every switch or delete reloads the list whatever the hub answered: a hook already deleted elsewhere is reported as gone ("ci was already deleted", "ci no longer exists") and its row disappears, with focus moved to the create form; a refused delete keeps the confirmation and says why. A per-hook on/off switch (PATCH, H153) is offered as the reversible alternative. (5) Governance: creating, switching and deleting a hook each write a SecurityEvent (_audit_webhook, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:142, called at agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:189, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:214 and agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:226). It names the hook id, the target as a JSON string (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:136), so a target cannot pass its own text off as the row's id or flags, and the signed and enabled flags as the store reads them, never the token or signing secret. A delete of an unknown id writes nothing and answers 404 "webhook not found". The receiver-disabled banner: when the receiver switch (H153, the setting webhooks.receiver_enabled) is off, the panel's receiver row says every delivery is refused until it is switched back on (frontend/src/panels/webhooks.tsx:348). The restart-gateway banner is not needed: a delivery sees the switch at most a second old (at once after a write in this process), and hook CRUD is served live; the receiver row says so. Not built, and not part of this row: a \`nerva webhooks\` CLI verb (critic note 7 places it with H409, as \`nerva webhooks inbound\|outbound\`) and a mobile screen. Tests: frontend/src/test/webhooks-panel.test.tsx has 55 cases. By describe block, 22 are this row's (the panel, the trigger URL and the review round), and 13 of those failed on the pre-review panel: the pending hold, the reload and the ghost row after a switch or delete, the one-POST create and the create held while a secret shows, the hook-named buttons, focus, the alert without the secret, the "copied" live region, the loopback hint and an unencodable id. The other 33 are H153's: the subscriptions, the receiver row and banner, the event list, the template, Deliver to, focus after a hook is gone, the push note and a proxy's 404. frontend/src/test/interop-live-honesty.test.tsx (14 cases, 5 of them H153's receiver read) drives the real useLiveModes against admin-guarded mock routes and renders InteropMode. The backend audit and DELETE guards are pinned in tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py (tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:353, tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:362, tests/test_h10_8_webhooks.py:371). Mutation: 60 mutants of the review round's guards (11 backend, 27 panel, 15 Interop, 7 v1) and 16 of the H153 panel additions were each run against their own suite, and all were caught; before the review round, 12 of the reviewer's 42 mutants had survived. | <a id="h209"></a>H209 | Keyboard shortcut customisation with a recorder and conflict detection | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The HUD's keyboard shortcuts live in one registry, as in Hermes. frontend/src/shortcuts.ts defines every action once (ACTIONS: id, category, label, default chord, scope): the ten mode keys, World (w), the Console (\`), Ambient (a), Cinema (m) and its three stages (o, n, b, scope cinema), focus the message box (/), the command palette (mod+k) and the Keyboard Shortcuts panel (mod+/). app.tsx, world_app.tsx and the cinema overlay in shell.tsx dispatch through matchAction over bindings(overrides) instead of literal key maps. frontend/src/shortcuts-panel.tsx is the panel mod+/ opens: categories Navigation, View, Composer and Session with search (searchBindings); click a chord to record a new one ("Press a key…", Esc cancels, a lone modifier keeps listening; chordFromEvent, rebind); per-binding reset and Reset all (resetBinding); an "Also bound to X" note when two actions share a chord (conflictsFor). Overrides persist per viewer in localStorage under hud.shortcuts, validated on read (readOverrides, loadOverrides, saveOverrides), and survive storage that is off. Governance: defineActions refuses any action whose id, category or label names an approval, a decision, an accept, reject, deny or consent (FORBIDDEN_TARGET), so no chord can reach a Decision Inbox action. | <a id="h218"></a>H218 | Archived chats, auto-archive of stale chats, and a default project directory | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Archived chats exist, as in Hermes. CheckpointManager.set_archived stamps or clears archived_at in a session's metadata without creating a session. get_sessions(archived=...) filters on it in SQL, read only from valid JSON, so the limit counts only the view asked for. agents/core/routers/sessions.py serves several routes. GET /sessions leaves archived chats out; ?archived=true lists only them, each row with archived_at. POST /sessions/{id}/archive and /unarchive (user_guard) are 404 for an unknown session and 400 for an invalid id. Resuming a chat through POST /sessions/resume unarchives it. agents/core/session_archive.py holds the rest. run_auto_archive archives sessions whose ended_at, else started_at, is older than memory.auto_archive_days (0 = off, at most 3650) and never touches the active one; SchedulerService.schedule_auto_archive runs it daily at 03:40. DELETE /sessions/{id}?confirm=DELETE is admin_guard only and refuses the active session (409) and an unknown one (404). delete_session first collects every trace of the session: the sessions row, its checkpoints and session_clock rows, the transcript snapshot and JSONL log, its TodoStore plan and the precompress compaction archive. It writes them to \<data home\>/backups/sessions/\<id\>-\<stamp\>.json (mkstemp 0600, fsync of file and directory, os.replace, read back and verified). Only then does it delete them all and clear the live conversation. A backup that does not land raises backup_failed and deletes nothing. The HUD SessionsPanel (frontend/src/panels/sessions.tsx) has chats and archived tabs, archive, unarchive and a two-step "delete permanently" that names the backup. llm.project_dir, the default project directory inside the file roots, is where H594 reads convention files from (project_context.start_dir). Tests: tests/test_h218_archived_chats.py (39), frontend/src/test/sessions-archive.test.tsx (7); 41 mutants, 38 killed, 3 equivalent. [d1](../agents/core/session_archive.py) [d2](../agents/core/checkpoint.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/sessions.py) |

| <a id="h220"></a>H220 | Usage and cost accounting surface | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The owner still cannot see where the money went. The only HUD reader, APMPanel (frontend/src/gap.tsx:1868-1875), reads d.runs, d.tokens and d.cost, but apm_summary nests those under d.totals. Every field therefore renders '—', and no per-agent or per-model breakdown is shown. /api/cost, /api/analytics/cost and /api/analytics/model-tiers have no HUD caller. No surface shows a local-vs-cloud split of spend or tokens: the %-local meter counts runs only. The Command Center has no Usage section. There is no per-session context/usage meter (tokens, context used/max/percent, cost): the /chat ChatResponse in agents/web.py carries only reply, pending_approvals, H677's warming flag and H674's notices, and the CLI usage report hard-codes cost_basis 'unavailable' (agents/cli/nerva.py:1760).
