| <a id="h222"></a>H222 | Always-visible microphone / wake-word state indicator | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva shows whether it is listening outside its main window, as Hermes does. agents/core/voice/listening.py keeps one process-wide, thread-safe listening state per source (ListeningState, STATE). The host's own voice path publishes it: the wake-word detector marks hub armed once it holds the mic open and off when it stops (WakeWordDetector.start, stop; agents/core/voice/wake_word.py), and a capture marks hub listening while it records, thinking while it transcribes and answers, speaking while it plays the reply, and falls back to armed (or off when the detector is not running) in a finally, so an early end or a failure never leaves it lit (VoicePipeline._capture_and_process; stop marks it off; agents/core/voice/pipeline.py). Wyoming satellites publish it too: an authenticated satellite's detection or voice-started marks satellite:\<id\> listening and voice-stopped marks it thinking; a transcript marks it thinking while it is answered and clears it after; a closed connection clears whatever it lit; with authentication required an unauthenticated peer can set nothing, and with authentication off the one anonymous satellite is named satellite (LISTEN_EVENTS, WyomingServer._listening_source, handle_connection; agents/core/voice/wyoming.py). The API is get / set / on-change in process: set_state (a bounded, printable source name and one of off, armed, listening, thinking, speaking; off forgets the source; at most 32 sources), snapshot (each source's state, the loudest by ORDER — listening, then armed, both an open mic, then thinking, speaking, off — mic_open, and a seq that moves on every change), subscribe and unsubscribe (callbacks run outside the lock and a failing one is ignored). A transient state not re-said for 120 s expires, and the expiry moves seq, so a crashed capture never leaves the indicator on; an armed mic stays. GET /api/voice/listening answers the snapshot and GET /api/voice/listening/stream pushes it on every change with keepalives (agents/core/routers/voice.py); both are user-guarded and GET-only, so nothing can set the state over HTTP. Every writer and reader loads the one agents.core.voice.listening module (a test pins it, since core.X and agents.core.X load as different modules here). The push to the desktop shell: frontend/src/listening.ts follows the stream with the HUD's own token (EventSource cannot send one) and falls back to polling every 5 s; it combines the hub's state with the window's own mic (useVoice active counts as listening), and only inside the desktop app forwards the loudest to the shell (setDesktopListening in frontend/src/desktop.tsx sends only changes and re-sends after a refused call; a closing window sends off). The indicator surface is the tray icon (desktop/src-tauri/src/main.rs): a desktop_listening command, allowed only to the local HUD windows (verify, capability allow-desktop-listening, build.rs manifest), records each window's state on a board and shows the loudest (desktop/src-tauri/src/indicator.rs): a menu-bar title while a mic is open (● listening, ○ wake word) — shown on macOS and next to the icon on Linux, not on Windows — and a tooltip naming the state on macOS and Windows. It is read-only: nothing on the tray or in the command can open or close a mic. Tests: tests/test_h222_listening.py (44), including the capture's state trail, every early end, a failing transcription, authenticated, unauthenticated and anonymous satellites and a satellite dropping mid-capture, the stream framing, the routes and guard, the shell's command, permission and capability, and the rustc unit tests of indicator.rs; src/test/listening.test.tsx (8) and src/test/desktop-listening.test.ts (3). 65 mutants: 64 killed after five cases were added; the other was a redundant check, removed. The desktop crate was not compiled here: its tray calls were checked against the Tauri 2.11.5 source. [d1](../agents/core/voice/listening.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/pipeline.py) [d3](../agents/core/voice/wake_word.py) |

| <a id="h227"></a>H227 | "What can it do right now" inspector: live status line plus a session panel of tools, skills, resolved system prompt and MCP servers | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Hermes' "what can it do right now" view is here: one read-only answer, for one agent as seen by one principal, built from the code a turn runs and served on three surfaces. agents/core/inspector.py holds it; build_inspector (:108) returns up to five sections (SECTIONS, :63). (1) Views. VIEWS (:56) names the five tool-profile postures: the owner and a guest on the HUD (owner, guest), the owner and a stranger on an external channel (inbound-owner, inbound; Telegram stands for every channel, under the inbound action origin a real channel turn has) and no human (internal). The look binds that principal and origin in a task of its own (:125), so nothing it binds, and nothing the runtime notes while resolving the offer, outlives it. (2) status (_status, :155): version, the backend and model the router serves, the local model's state through project_llm_status, the transcript budget llm.tool_loop_context_tokens (0 reads as 75 % of the model window), the tool loop and safe mode. (3) tools (_tools, :194): the tool runtime's own resolved offer for the agent under that principal (_resolve_offer: registry or the capability registry's view, job toolset, posture, the agent's tools: list, the shared-session rule), not the raw registry. Each row says whether the tool is gated and whether its output is fenced as untrusted, with the description cut to DESCRIPTION_CHARS (160, :65). The withheld names are listed, a resolver failure offers nothing, and loop_enabled says whether the model sees any of them (with the loop off they are what the posture would offer). (4) skills (_skills, :215): the rows prompt_catalog gives the prompt under that principal and that offer, or none when llm.skills_in_prompt is off. (5) mcp (_mcp, :225): each server's transport, trust tier, transport-aware liveness (is_connected, so an HTTP server reads connected; a probe that raises is down) and visible tools, with no URL or command line. (6) system_prompt (_prompt, :253): the agent's system prompt and the user part Agent.build_prompt sends for an empty user message: persona, agent context, the core-memory block, the runtime, language and grounding rails and the skills block. tokens (:266) estimates what that fixed part costs every turn before history. The texts are masked by the log redactor (SecretRedactionFilter; nothing is shown when it cannot load, _redactor :73) and capped together at PROMPT_CAP_BYTES (64 KiB, :64) on a character boundary. The core block is read with freeze=False (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3056, :3098, :3125): the frozen block when a turn froze one, else a fresh render that is not kept, so a look never fixes the day's snapshot. (7) Surfaces. GET /api/admin/inspector?agent=&view=&section= (agents/core/routers/status.py:123, admin-guarded; 404 for an unknown agent, 400 naming the views or sections, 503 before the hub is up). nerva inspect \[agent\] \[--as VIEW\] \[--section tools\|skills\|prompt\|mcp\|status\] \[--json\] (cmd_inspect, agents/cli/nerva.py:927; render_inspector :948) prints the same payload section by section, or as it came. The HUD's Inspector panel (frontend/src/panels/inspector.tsx, Console → Admin) reads the same route with a status line, an As switch and folding sections. Tests: tests/test_inspector.py (43) builds a partial orchestrator over the coordinator's real ToolRPC registry. It covers the guest's tools equal to ToolProfileResolver's set and not the registry, every view's posture and bindings, the agent's list, the marks and the cut, the loop off, a failing resolver, an unwired runtime, the skills, the prompt (skills block, core memory, rails, empty message, tokens), the unfrozen core block, masking, the withheld prompt, the 64 KiB cap on a split character, no leaked binding, MCP liveness, the status line and safe mode, section order, the route (payload, defaults, refusals, admin token, guard snapshot, 503) and the CLI (--json equal to the payload, rendering, --section, the loop-off line, the tree, one list of views and sections). frontend/src/panels/inspector.test.tsx has 4. 82 mutants: 79 caught, ten after survivors got cases. One redundant reset was simplified away. The origin passed to classify_turn is equivalent, because each view's principal alone decides the surface. | <a id="h242"></a>H242 | Workspace/project model, repo discovery, strict setup verification and diagnostics bundle | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva copies Hermes's setup.runtime_check into the owner's terminal. The hub resolves the route with the same llm_router.select_backend call a Jarvis turn makes (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:269) and names its verdict: a local provider/model pair must be resident, and a cloud route (Gemini, Claude, or the cloud-compatible adapter set by llm.compatible_provider) counts only when the router handed back the very backend that route names (agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:142, agents/core/routers/onboarding.py:307-315), and (H380) when its provider accepts the key that route would use. A real HybridRouter pins this for the local, cloud-flash, claude and both cloud-compatible paths (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:533). \`nerva doctor\` (and \`python scripts/doctor.py\`) adds the advisory runtime_resolves row: one GET of /api/onboarding/command-center at the hub \`nerva status\` reads (scripts/doctor.py:275), run only after /readyz answered at that same address and otherwise reported as skip, never ok (scripts/doctor.py:424-428). It sends one credential, the user token when both are set and the admin token only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address (scripts/doctor.py:392-399, scripts/doctor.py:345), never prints it, and is ok only on a ready verdict that names the route, provider and model (scripts/doctor.py:475-479). A refused token, a reply cut off mid-body, a listener that does not speak HTTP, a bad URL or an odd resident list is a named warn, not a traceback (scripts/doctor.py:455-460; tests/test_doctor.py:433, tests/test_doctor.py:458). \`nerva status\` now labels the old any-model state 'resident' and adds a runnable: line from the same verdict (agents/cli/nerva.py:572); a timeout or reset on that read shows 'unknown — hub_unreachable' and the rest of the status still prints (agents/cli/nerva.py:582). A reply cut off mid-body or a garbled status line on any hub read is HubUnavailable, so \`nerva status\` reports an unreachable hub instead of a traceback (agents/cli/client.py:181, tests/test_nerva_cli.py:181). Both tools dial a wildcard bind (0.0.0.0, ::) over loopback (agents/cli/client.py:56). An end-to-end test drives both terminal checks against the real app (tests/test_first_run_command_center.py:590). Diagnostics stay local: the redacted bundle is served over one admin GET (agents/core/routers/support.py:21), and neither module imports a network client (tests/test_support_bundle.py:67), so the share_nous upload stays refused. The projects/workspaces model was skipped by decision. Known limits the row does not require: the verdict is for the route a short Jarvis chat takes; and a cloud route is judged by the router's own selection, not by a live call. [d1](../agents/core/routers/onboarding.py) [d2](../scripts/doctor.py) [d3](../agents/cli/nerva.py) |
| <a id="h243"></a>H243 | Completions (filesystem paths, slash commands) and provider/model options (list, save key, disconnect) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) No provider options: no route returns models grouped by provider (cloud and local) with cost hints, and cost_tracker's price table is not shown per model. (2) No save-key: no admin route writes a provider API key to the secret store and returns the refreshed model list. The pools read env vars only, so a key saved at runtime would never reach the router without a pool rebuild. (3) No disconnect: no route removes a provider's credential and rebuilds its pool, so there is no single place to revoke a provider. No audit event exists for either write. \`/api/secrets/broker\` (agents/core/routers/secrets.py:29, agents/core/routers/secrets.py:56) stores action-time \`{{secret:NAME}}\` values, not the router's provider keys. (4) No slash completion: neither the chat composer nor QuickbarPanel completes command names or their \`usage\` arguments from \`/api/commands\` as the user types. Arming also writes no audit event, and the host pipeline starts at boot with neither check. The separate open item that no install path installs a voice engine is outside this row. | <a id="h262"></a>H262 | Config-driven data lifecycle — prune, archive, vacuum on an interval-gated sweep | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | There is no reversible archive tier: purge_old_conversations unlinks files directly (agents/core/retention.py:76), with no soft-hide stage, no restore and no exemption for pinned sessions. Nothing runs VACUUM after a prune: prune_before issues DELETE FROM security_events (agents/core/security/audit.py:367) and commits, so the audit database file never shrinks. There is no vacuum_after_prune or min_vacuum_interval setting; the only VACUUM is on the forget path (agents/core/data_purge.py:243). There is no persisted last-run marker and no min-interval gate shared across processes; the sweep is a fixed daily cron in each scheduler process. Changes to retention TTLs or the enabled flag are ordinary admin PUT /api/admin/settings/retention writes. The governance field requires them to go through the irreversible approval tier instead. | <a id="h270"></a>H270 | Writer allowlist for names that hijack execution or soften posture | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Nerva has no write-side policy for environment-variable names — the row's deliverable (a declared-names allowlist, depends_on "the key registry") does not exist, and the row defers it rather than excluding it. #1179 changed nothing on this row's subject: its client.py change is an outbound disclosure (cwd_secret_files :215-242 and the "withholding is undercut" WARNING in _merged_env :561-572), not a name policy or a writer. Neither did the fail-closed tool-filter bounds change (56903ac1: tool_patterns_over_bounds, a deny-bounds block in MCPServer.__init__ and a tools_allow/tools_deny bounds refusal in admin_mcp_add) — it touches no env name, writer or override path and only moved line numbers. H502 (#1174) did change item 3 and half of item 2, and that is folded in here. Three items owed, two shippable today against real surfaces: 1. THE REGISTRY (the row's core item, blocked on its stated dependency). No ENV_VAR_NAME allowlist/denylist exists anywhere (grep for ENV_VAR_NAME/ENV_WRITE/WRITE_DENYLIST/writable_env across agents/, scripts/, tests/, frontend/src/ is empty at HEAD) and no \`config.write\` capability kind exists in agents/core/capability_actions.py. Of Hermes' hijack names, LD_PRELOAD, LD_LIBRARY_PATH, DYLD_\*, PYTHONHOME, PYTHONSTARTUP, NODE_OPTIONS, GIT_SSH_COMMAND and PAGER are named nowhere in code or tests — but PATH, SHELL (agents/core/mcp/client.py:162), NODE_PATH (:171) and PATH, SHELL, PYTHONPATH (agents/core/environments/__init__.py:18, :27, :30) ARE named, as members of the two OUTBOUND allowlists, and EDITOR is exercised at tests/test_nerva_import.py:165 (pinned dropped at tests/test_nerva_import.py:825). Design consequence: the outbound lists cannot double as the write-side registry — they deliberately admit the plumbing names Hermes forbids on write — so the registry is a separate, inbound-only list. When any env/config writer lands, that allowlist must land in the SAME PR, refusing an undeclared name before authorization — the shape of agents/core/capability_actions.py:139-143 refusing missing_inputs before the authorizer at :151-152. 2. THE MCP \`env\` BOUNDARY IS UNREFUSED (shippable now; the "untested" half closed with #1174). MCPServerConfig (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:131-142) sets no \`model_config\`, so pydantic's default extra="ignore" makes \`POST /api/admin/mcp {"env": {"LD_PRELOAD": "/tmp/evil.so"}}\` return 200 and silently drop the key instead of refusing it — reproduced end to end at HEAD through TestClient (200 {"ok": true}; stored MCPServer.env == {}; to_config() has no env key). The field it would populate is live — MCPServer.env (agents/core/mcp/client.py:424) → _merged_env (:531-577) → create_subprocess_exec(env=…) (:453-460). Already pinned by tests/test_mcp_transports.py:507-522: \`env\` is not a MCPServerConfig field, to_config (:798-812) does not export it and load_from_config (:814-849) does not read it. Reachability: the HUD only GETs /api/admin/mcp (frontend/src/api/live.ts:449) and no CLI adds servers, so the raw admin API is the only path today. Still owed: \`extra="forbid"\` (or an explicit named refusal) on MCPServerConfig, plus a test asserting the route refuses an \`env\` body rather than swallowing it — and the same refusal must cover the hijack names above on the day \`env\` becomes operator-settable. 3. PER-SERVER \`env\` BYPASSES THE ALLOWLIST IN BOTH BRANCHES. stdio_env_baseline applies overrides unfiltered after the allowlist loop (agents/core/mcp/client.py:278-279; docstring :253 "Explicit per-server overrides always win"), and the legacy branch does \`merged = os.environ.copy(); merged.update(self.env)\` with no filter (:575-576). The "decide whether the baseline should become the default" half of this item is DONE: since H502 the baseline is on unless JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ENV_BASELINE=0 (:179-190; docs/FLAGS.md "on by default since H502") and tests/test_mcp_transports.py:386-421 pins both directions, so the legacy branch is now the explicit escape hatch rather than the shipped path. Owed: gate per-server overrides on the item-1 write-side registry (NOT merely on STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST + _SECRET_LIKE — that policy passes PATH, SHELL, NODE_PATH and would pass an operator-supplied PATH/NODE_PATH override untouched), or make the bypass an explicitly reviewed, audited exception. Severity, checked directly rather than inferred: LATENT. \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field, the admin route constructs MCPServer without one (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:212-224), and the only production caller passing an override is Nerva's own WorldView writer with an in-repo secret plus the WORLDVIEW_HOST_ENV names (agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py:222-227, :49-56). No untrusted input reaches the override loop, so this is a boundary to close before \`env\` becomes operator-settable, not a live hole. The outbound allowlist's one intentional exception — a name the owner lists in JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV passes even when credential-shaped (:256-260, :272-274; tests :449-503) — is owner-declared and host-wide, and is not this row's write boundary. | <a id="h273"></a>H273 | Tell the owner where an effective value actually came from | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207, then fixed after eight adversarial reviews. The first found the stdlib parser was python-dotenv's pre-1.2.3 grammar, while the lock pins 1.2.3: the doctor could print value material as key names, a named-pipe .env was no longer loaded, and a data home named in the repo .env was no longer followed. The second found that the doctor sent the admin credential to whatever answered at the hub address, that more keys than serve.py's four were read before any .env was loaded, and that a one-case line of value material could still be printed as a name. The third found that the start itself read 31 more names before the load (so ten .env knobs, the audit key and the public-profile gate among them, were shown as in effect and were not), that two read-again entries were false, and that the MCP transport ignored a user token kept in .env. The fourth found that the MCP transport and the HTTP user guard asked different questions, so a lapsed or revoked user credential locked every route but left MCP open to a local caller; that the coordinator built its Orchestrator before the load, so its audit rows went unkeyed beside the hub's keyed ones; that an IPv6 bind (JARVIS_HOST=::1) escaped both loopback checks; and that the CLI sent the admin token to any hub. The fifth found that the admin tier still trusted a loopback caller once no admin credential was active, so after the owner revoked every credential a local process with no token could mint a fresh user token over HTTP and reopen the user routes and MCP (older than H273, but it falsified this row's credential sentence); that a managed user token that had expired counted as never configured; that the CLI asked the owner to set an admin token it had withheld; and that the supervisor wrote its respawns apart from a run-log named in .env. The sixth found that the sentence about minting did not hold where no admin credential was ever configured, that the offline recovery wrote a tokens.db the hub never read when the data home was named only in a .env (and a packaged build had none to run), that the CLI's withheld message fired on refusals https would not fix, and that a named-pipe .env still split the run-log. The seventh found that the supervisor now told its child a default the hub never read (a named-pipe .env hid the runtime from the brief), that the packaged recovery revived env tokens that had been rotated away or revoked, and that the CLI still asked for a token that was set. The eighth found that the packaged recovery still missed a token in the start environment or a moved data home, and left a LAN-bound app unable to start, and that the new hint called a valid user token refused on an admin verb and proposed a rotate that revoked the owner's working admin token. Every effective configuration key now carries the layer that supplied it, the note says when a .env value is not in effect, and every surface the row names reports names and layers only. The table. load_layered_env (agents/core/env_provenance.py:356) loads the files. Precedence: the process environment (the shell, a systemd EnvironmentFile, docker -e), then the repo .env, then the data-home .env, and the first layer to set a key wins (override=False). It keeps python-dotenv's behaviour: - Each file is read once, whether a regular file or a named pipe, the way python-dotenv reads it. 1Password mounts a .env as a FIFO. - The load is python-dotenv's public load_dotenv on the text that was read. The names come from python-dotenv's own parser (dotenv.parser.parse_stream), which logs nothing, so a bad line is warned about once. No private python-dotenv API is used. - PYTHON_DOTENV_DISABLED is checked before each layer, as python-dotenv checks it on every call, so a repo .env that sets it stops the data-home layer. Its spellings are python-dotenv's own, kept in env_config.py, the repo's one home for env-flag parsing (agents/core/env_config.py:112); a parity test pins them against python-dotenv's own switch. - The data home is a callable resolved after the repo layer is loaded, so a JARVIS_USER_HOME set in the repo .env names it, as before. A first-run home is scaffolded before its .env is read. - A second load in one process keeps a file's attribution only while the environment still holds the value that load set (a private digest, never the value), and works out afresh what the files shadow. The same file as both layers is read once. - It records which layer supplied each key, by diffing the environment around each file, and the lower layers that also define it (shadowed: their value is ignored). That answers "why is my .env change not applied?". - files() records what the load did with each file: its kind (file, fifo, absent, the same file as the repo .env, or disabled), whether it is present, and whether it was read. provenance() (agents/core/env_provenance.py:494) is the queryable in-process table; a key that appeared after the load reads as set while running. The hub loads first. load_hub_env (agents/core/env_provenance.py:436) is the hub's own load, once per process, so a named pipe is read once: - serve.py calls it before it builds the server and runs its guards (serve.py:239). The server's host, port, log level and shutdown timeout, the posture guards and the bind guard see a .env value, so a token in .env satisfies the bind guard. - The lifespan calls it before its boot guards (agents/web.py:457). The data home, logging, the orchestrator with its audit log, memory and budget ledger, and the router's detect() all read after it: the audit key, the public-profile seed gate, turn embeddings, the budget, the router's URLs and cloud keys in a .env are in effect. The boot guards, the front door among them, judge the loaded environment, so the late re-check was removed. - The coordinator (the systemd jarvis-runtime unit, compose runtime-coordinator) calls it first in run(), before its run-log path and cycle (scripts/coordinator.py:92), and again, idempotently, before it builds its Orchestrator (scripts/coordinator.py:57): it shares the hub's data root, so it keys the audit chain the hub keys. The supervisor that spawns it (scripts/runtime_supervisor.py) does not load, since its child would then read every file key as the process environment's; it reads the run-log path through hub_value (agents/core/env_provenance.py:456), the value the files give without loading them, and it hands its child only a path it found (the process, or a regular .env), which the child's load does not override, so both write that one run-log (scripts/runtime_supervisor.py:56; review-H273g m4). A path in a named-pipe .env, which the supervisor never reads, is not handed down: the child reads the pipe as the hub does, so the cycles land where the brief reads them, and only the supervisor's own events stay in the default file (_child_env, scripts/runtime_supervisor.py:94; review-H273h m1). An empty value is unset on both ends. The reality-evidence harness does too (agents/core/observability/reality_evidence.py:183). - agents/run.py, the REPL, loads before its logging and its imports (agents/run.py:21), so a log file or scanner pattern in .env is in effect. - PluginManager.build asks for it too (agents/core/plugin_manager.py:80), for any other entry. scripts/install_smoke.py does not load before it builds its isolated hub (a temp data root, from the process environment); its PluginManager.build loads afterwards, as for any entry. - Log redaction is imported first, so its switch stays the boot environment's alone: a .env cannot turn it off. Keys read before the load. note_for (agents/core/env_provenance.py:480) flags what is still read before any file can be. That is what importing agents.web freezes or resolves: the ASGI root path, the rate limit, CORS, CSP, the router's auto-deep switch, the analytics cap, the graph's Neo4j defaults, the app and data roots, and the log-redaction switch (agents/core/env_provenance.py:60). A .env value for them is recorded but not in effect, and the note says to set it in the process environment. - What locates the hub's own files (JARVIS_APP_ROOT, JARVIS_HOME, JARVIS_MEMORY_DIR) is never taken from a file (agents/core/env_provenance.py:69). A .env value would have split the stores between two roots. It is recorded and kept out of the environment, and its own note says so (agents/core/env_provenance.py:91). - JARVIS_USER_HOME from the repo .env names the data home and its .env. When JARVIS_HOME is not set, the stores opened while the hub was imported stay under the default root, and its note says so (agents/core/env_provenance.py:72). - Read again after the load, so a .env value is in effect (agents/core/env_provenance.py:84): DEV_MODE; the admin and user tokens (every guard reads them per request, the MCP transport included); the OAuth client ids; the trusted proxies. The lists are measured, not declared: - A test spies on the environment in a subprocess across the import and the whole start up to the load, with the data root set either way. Every Nerva name read there must be listed, and every listed name must be read there. - A second spy runs the start and a few requests. Every read-again name must be read after the load, and no name listed as read before the load may be (tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py:666). The two false read-again entries are fixed: - The MCP transport, the HTTP user guard and the MCP identity check ask one predicate, _user_credential_required (agents/web.py:286; agents/core/routers/mcp.py:393). A token from .env guards MCP on this machine and lets a remote client in. The question is whether a credential was ever configured (_ever_configured, agents/web.py:108): an env token, active or superseded; a rotation or a revoke with --revoke-env, which leave the store's persistent flag; an issued token, live or expired. Such a credential that has lapsed, been rotated away or been revoked locks MCP exactly as it locks HTTP. The admin guard asks the same question for its tier (_admin_configured, agents/web.py:136), and so does the owner flag on /chat, so where an admin credential was ever configured and every credential is revoked or has expired, no local process mints its way back in over HTTP. Recovery is scripts/token_recover.py rotate admin on the box, which loads the hub's .env files first so it writes the tokens.db the hub reads, and says which (review-H273g m2); a packaged build first removes every JARVIS_ADMIN_TOKEN and JARVIS_USER_TOKEN from both places the app reads them, the data home's .env (wherever the home is) and the environment the app starts from, unsets JARVIS_HOST for the restart (the boot guard refuses a LAN bind with no token), then removes security/tokens.db under its data root (JARVIS_HOME and JARVIS_MEMORY_DIR named), because that file is also what records the rotations and revokes that superseded env tokens (docs/PACKAGING.md; review-H273h m2, review-H273i m1). The check lists the token table once per store: once a tier shows configured it stays so for that store (review-H273g n1). Only a hub that never had a credential of the tier trusts a localhost caller without one (for the exception, see Known limits). - The OAuth routes use the plugin manager's copy of the OAuth module (agents/core/routers/oauth.py:33), which re-reads the client ids after the load. Key names come from python-dotenv itself whenever it can be imported (text_keys, agents/core/env_provenance.py:193): every binding in file order, a rebound key each time. The stdlib port (_port_keys, agents/core/env_provenance.py:134) is 1.2.3's grammar and is the fallback for a broken install. A parity test pins it on 25 edge cases, including the BOM, escaped backslashes and a key rebound without "=". A seeded differential fuzz of 4,000 random files pins it too. derive (agents/core/env_provenance.py:318) builds the same table offline. It follows a data home named in the repo .env, or built from ${VAR}, resolving every binding in file order as load_dotenv does: the environment wins over the file's earlier values, a rebound key takes its last value, and a last binding without "=" sets nothing (agents/core/env_provenance.py:285). It parses each file once, keyed on its size, times and inode, and never opens a named pipe. Surfaces: - GET /api/admin/env/sources (agents/core/routers/admin.py:320) is admin-guarded and no-store, at the same boundary as GET /api/admin/env. Each key comes with its layer, a label, the layers it overrides, whether /api/admin/env masks its value, and its note. It also names what the load did with each file (path, kind, present, read); values are never included. - \`nerva doctor\`'s advisory config_sources check (scripts/doctor.py:641): - Reading the hub. It reads the running hub's own table when /readyz is ok, asking without a credential first. Every request to the hub goes over http or https, has a timeout, refuses a redirect, and never goes through a proxy to a hub on this machine (scripts/doctor.py:371). The hub address brackets an IPv6 bind (JARVIS_HOST=::1 gives http://\[::1\]:8080; scripts/doctor.py:289), and an unbracketed IPv6 address, whatever its first group (0:0:0:0:0:0:0:1 too), is read the way http.client dials it (scripts/doctor.py:329). The admin credential goes only to a hub on this machine, decided by its address rather than its spelling (127.0.0.2, 127.1, localhost., ::1 and ::ffff:127.0.0.1 count; scripts/doctor.py:345). config_sources sends it only after a refusal, and runtime_resolves only when no user token is set (scripts/doctor.py:387). Otherwise the doctor predicts the table from its own shell and says which of the two it did and why. - Which keys. It shows every key a .env file sets, and the shell's keys that the hub's code reads or that carry Nerva's prefixes. The hub's reads are found by reading agents/ and serve.py: helper calls, a bound mapping, \*_ENV constants (private ones too), a pool's from_env names and a descriptor's \*_env keyword (scripts/doctor.py:509). - Which names are printed. A .env name is printed when the hub reads it, .env.example declares it, it is an identifier with Nerva's prefix, or it is a plain one-case name with a value, an empty placeholder included (scripts/doctor.py:747). Any other name is counted, never printed (withheld_env_names). That includes a name shaped like a digest, a seed or an access key id, whatever follows its "=" (scripts/doctor.py:527); a value that is only = padding; a PEM line; a mixed-case name; and a name with control characters. - Hub mode. On this machine it reads the files the hub names to tell names from value material. For a hub on another machine it shows only the names Nerva knows, and says why. - Other reports. It names a .env that is not UTF-8, which the hub's load fails on (env_not_utf8), and a named pipe it does not open. For each key it prints the .env values a higher layer overrides and the note. Any failure inside the check becomes a named reason, never a crash. - The \`nerva\` CLI's client (agents/cli/client.py:111) follows the same transport rules: no redirect (urllib carried both tokens to wherever one pointed), no proxy to a hub on this machine, and http or https only. Its loopback rule is the doctor's, pinned equal over one table of spellings. It sends the admin token only to a hub on this machine or over https (agents/cli/client.py:151), never in clear text to another host, whether or not a user token is set; the user token goes to the hub it names. When it withheld the admin token and the hub refuses, it names that as one possible cause beside the user token, never as the only one (review-H273g m3). The hint reads the hub's own reason for the tier it wanted: a verb that needs the admin tier asks for JARVIS_ADMIN_TOKEN when only the user token is set, only a token of the wanted tier that was sent is named as refused, a hub with no token of the tier configured is named as such, and the recovery offered is the additive token_recover.py issue (rotate only for a leaked token, since it revokes every token of the tier; review-H273i m2); nerva status reads the same reason on its runnable and e-stop lines (_auth_hint, agents/cli/nerva.py:632; review-H273h m3, review-H273i m2, n4). - The settings plane: every row says default, set, undeclared, or unreadable (an encrypted secret whose key is lost) (value_source, agents/core/settings_db.py:536). A value a selected product posture puts in effect is named beside the stored one (in_effect, overlay). An unknown or unreadable stored posture is off, and the posture is read in the listing's own connection. \`nerva config list\` prints both (agents/cli/nerva.py:803). - The HUD's Admin "API keys & secrets" list shows each key's layer, the layers it overrides and its note (hydrateAdminKeys, frontend/src/api/live.ts:205; frontend/src/modes3.tsx:256). Hermes records provenance only for vault-supplied names, and its own notes ask for this per-key table; Nerva has it. Nerva has no managed or secret-source layer in the environment: vault secrets are injected at action time, not loaded into the environment. Known limits: - On Windows, key names are compared case-sensitively although the environment is not; that case is theoretical and not handled. - A raw uvicorn start binds where uvicorn's own flags say. JARVIS_HOST there, from a file or from the shell alike, reaches the Host-header guard and the boot guard's bind check, which judges that value rather than the address uvicorn bound. - In a process that loads more than once (tests), a value equal to one an earlier load set keeps that load's attribution. The hub loads once. - JARVIS_USER_HOME set only in a .env, with no JARVIS_HOME, still splits the stores: what the hub opens while it is imported stays under the default root, and the rest follows the home (autonomy.db opens under both). Its note says so on the route, in the doctor and in the HUD. Moving either half would move an existing install's history, so it is left, and set in the process environment it does not split. - The CLI sends the admin token to a hub on another machine when that hub is reached over https: the owner named that hub, and its admin verbs need it. The doctor, which only reads, never sends the admin token off this machine. Over plain http to another machine the admin verbs (the e-stop, approvals, tools, jobs) are refused, and the CLI says why. - A box that never had an admin credential trusts a direct localhost caller as admin, whatever happened to its user tokens: after revoke user --revoke-env, or once a user token has lapsed, a local process still mints a user token. The user-token lock binds callers from other machines; revoke all --revoke-env closes it for this one too (pinned, review-H273g m1). - A run-log path set only in a named-pipe .env reaches the coordinator and the hub, not the supervisor, which cannot read the pipe: its own spawned, child_exited and respawned events go to the default logs/runtime.jsonl, apart from the cycles. Setting the path in the process environment keeps them together. - A token issued with token_store issue and deleted by a revoke without --revoke-env leaves no trace in the store, and so does anything that empties it (purge_expired, which nothing in the hub calls, a deleted tokens.db, a new data root), so that hub reads as never configured for its tier at its next start and trusts a localhost caller again (a running hub that saw the credential stays locked until then). Closing it needs a persistent flag in agents/core/security/token_store.py, the owner's security lane. A rotation, a revoke with --revoke-env, or an expired token keep the hub locked. - A managed user token alone now guards this machine too (ENV-163): an install that has only a store token needs it in the HUD ("store in this browser"), the CLI, the doctor and nerva_mcp_stdio.py. - Under a raw uvicorn start, uvicorn's own proxy knobs (FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS, UVICORN_PROXY_HEADERS) are read before the app is imported, so a .env value for them is not in effect, while announce_trusted_proxies reports the file's value. - A trailing-dot name (localhost., 127.1.) counts as this machine, though the resolver treats it as a DNS name; a resolver that answered it with another address would receive the admin token. - The value-material shape misses uppercase hex, digitless base32 and short mixed ids, and withholds digit-bearing names with no underscore (contrived lines). - The data home is scaffolded (its README, a .env copied from .env.example) before the boot guards run, so a start they refuse still writes it. - Hub mode on this machine trusts the paths the hub names: a hub in a container names paths that do not exist on the host, and every plain custom key is then withheld with a reason that is false for it. - The scan for the hub's reads misses computed and mapped names (a URL key built from a prefix, a budget key read through a mapping); both are declared in .env.example or carry the prefix today. - JARVIS_HOST=0::0 or ::ffff:0.0.0.0 gives a URL judged not this machine (the proxy is used and the admin token withheld, the safe direction); JARVIS_HOST=localhost:9000 gives an invalid URL, as it did before. Tests: - tests/test_env_provenance.py (61 cases): parity and fuzz (tests/test_env_provenance.py:130), the named pipe, the plugin manager's order and the posture overlay. - tests/test_doctor_hub_credential.py (8): a real redirect and a real proxy, pinning where the credential goes (tests/test_doctor_hub_credential.py:93). - tests/test_h273c_provenance_review.py (56): the import, the names withheld, the bound-mapping scan and the second round's nits. - tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py (74 cases, the third round): - the start measured and the lists by where they are used (tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py:119); - knobs the start reads, in effect from a .env (tests/test_h273d_provenance_review.py:158); - the bind guard through serve.py and the lifespan, the redaction floor, one load per process, the refused roots; - the MCP transport, the OAuth routes, and the doctor's and CLI's transport rules; - hub mode, the names withheld, the widened scan, the reviewer's survivors and the nits. - tests/test_h273e_provenance_review.py (48 cases, the fourth round): the MCP transport, its per-tool identity check and the HTTP guard in every credential posture, rotated, expired, revoked, a managed token only and never configured (tests/test_h273e_provenance_review.py:59); the coordinator keying the audit chain from .env; the IPv6 bind; the CLI's admin-token destination; the CLI and doctor loopback rules over one table (tests/test_h273e_provenance_review.py:238); the REPL's log file; and the refused roots' note. - tests/test_h273f_provenance_review.py (43 cases, the fifth round): an admin env token alone closing the loopback fallback; no local caller mints a token once every credential is revoked or has expired, in three postures, while a box that never had an admin credential still mints its first from loopback; the owner flag on /chat; the offline recovery; an expired managed user token keeping the hub locked, and the recorded residual; the CLI's admin token for an admin-only environment and over the whole table of spellings, and what it says when it withholds it; the supervisor's and the coordinator's run-log from .env; hub_value's layers; the reality-evidence harness's load; a bare IPv6 hub whose first group is not empty. - tests/test_h273g_provenance_review.py (18 cases, the sixth round): a user-only box still trusting this machine as admin, and the docstrings that say so; an issued-only admin token, live and expired, closing the loopback fallback; the configured scope remembered per store; nothing purging expired tokens; the recovery writing the store a .env names; the CLI's hint for the client that was refused, with and without an admin token, and on nerva status's two lines; one run-log for a named-pipe .env and for a path the child is told; an empty run-log value; hub_value's disabled and interpolated layers; the supervisor run as a script. - tests/test_h273h_provenance_review.py (14 cases, the seventh round): a named-pipe run-log path reaching the coordinator and the hub alike, through a real load of the pipe; a process path handed down as it is; nothing handed down when the app cannot import; the packaged recovery removing the env tokens before the file, and naming the JARVIS_HOME path; a refused token that is set named as refused for each tier and both; the status lines, loopback and LAN; scripts/token_recover.py run as documented, as a script; both spawn sites in the supervisor's main() carrying the run-log. - tests/test_h273i_provenance_review.py (12 cases, the eighth round): the hint for each tier the hub names (a user token on an admin verb, a refused admin token and its issue verb, the user tier, a hub with no token of the tier), the verb passing the hub's reason, the status lines naming a hub with no user token, the child keeping its parent's environment, the packaged recovery naming both places, the bind and JARVIS_MEMORY_DIR, the test manual's recovery rows, and the supervisor's docstring. - tests/test_doctor.py keeps the JSON contract, and frontend/src/test/env-sources.test.ts the HUD mapping and its note. Mutation: - The first round's 41 mutants and the second's 29 were each caught, as were the second review's twelve survivors. - The third review's own 67 independent mutants left 16 survivors, four of them equivalent. Each of the other twelve now has a test: the lists' classification, a mixed-case name, a missing value, the data home's values, the cache key, a commented declaration, a 403, a posture that is not JSON, a third load, and the same-file order. Re-run against the new tests, all twelve are caught. - This round's 46 mutants (44 Python, 2 HUD) were each run against their own suites: 45 are caught. The survivor drops the IPv4-mapped check in the loopback test. It is equivalent on the test interpreter, whose patched ipaddress already counts ::ffff:127.0.0.1 as loopback, and the check stays for the 3.12 releases before that fix. - The fourth review's 50 independent mutants left three survivors (the MCP gate's predicate and two CLI loopback spellings); each is caught now. This round's 20 mutants (the bracketed IPv6 address and the bare IPv6 host in both copies, the CLI's admin-token destination, the shared predicate in the guard, the MCP gate and the identity check, the coordinator's and the REPL's load, the refused roots' note, the CLI's spellings) were all caught, one after the identity-check case was added. The fifth review's 22 independent mutants left four real survivors (the CLI with an admin token and no user token, a localhost-prefix spelling for the admin token, the coordinator's run() load, the reality-evidence harness's load); each has a case now. This round's 15 mutants (the ever-configured rule for both tiers and each of its terms, the CLI's hint, the supervisor's read and hub_value's layers, the bare IPv6 order in both copies) were caught but one, the admin rule ignoring the env token, which is caught now that the case exists; one more is equivalent (hub_value's early process-environment return, which the repo layer's merge gives anyway). The sixth review's 30 independent mutants left nine real survivors (an issued-only admin token, the generic hint, two hub_value layer rules, the supervisor run as a script, an empty run-log value, and three bare-IPv6 spellings in the safe direction); the first six have cases now. This round's 13 mutants were all caught, one after its case was added (the e-stop status line). The seventh review's 22 independent mutants left three real survivors (the recovery's import path run as a script, and the supervisor's two spawn sites); each has a case now. This round's 12 mutants were all caught, one after the supervisor's two .env readers became one (its import path, now covered by the script-run case). The eighth review's 22 independent mutants left one real survivor (the child's environment dropped whole) and one unpinned hint verb; both have cases, and this round's 12 mutants over the hint and the child's environment are all caught. | <a id="h275"></a>H275 | Boot into a known-good state when customizations break the install | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub can boot in safe mode, with every owner customization left out and no gate relaxed. JARVIS_SAFE_MODE=1, or \`python serve.py --safe-mode\`, which sets it before the hub is imported (apply_safe_mode_switch, serve.py:33), is the one flag, read at call time by one module (enabled, agents/core/safe_mode.py:110). It leaves out seven layers (LAYERS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:54). Skills: discovery does not open the data home's skills folder (agents/core/skills/loader.py:641), and any skill in the bundled tree that is not a shipped one (generated, imported, edited or pending review, by the loader's own external classification) is not loaded (agents/core/skills/loader.py:709); every shipped skill still is. MCP: the saved servers are not registered (agents/web.py:1807) and their saved configuration is not touched: _save_mcp_config never rewrites it from the empty manager (agents/web.py:1778), and adding or removing a server answers 409 \`safe_mode\` before anything changes (_safe_mode_refusal, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:160). Acquired packages and extensions: the acquisition runtime reports itself disabled (agents/core/acquisition/runtime.py:39), so nothing promoted is re-registered and extensions() returns None, which the activate route answers as unavailable. Plugin grants: JARVIS_PLUGIN_GRANTS, which only ever widens access under least privilege, is ignored (agents/core/plugin_gate.py:613). Persona and contract overlays: soul_path_for (agents/core/agent.py:389) and identity_path (agents/core/agent.py:438) resolve through _pick_overlay (agents/core/agent.py:375), which in safe mode returns the shipped SOUL.md or IDENTITY.md and never a SOUL.local.md or IDENTITY.local.md from the data home or the repository; construction and the H672 compaction-boundary refresh both resolve through it, so a safe-mode session cannot pick an overlay up mid-conversation. Heartbeat: a HEARTBEAT.local.md is passed over for the shipped HEARTBEAT.md (agents/core/heartbeat.py:219). Owner jobs stay saved and are not put on the scheduler (agents/core/scheduler_service.py:64). What was left out is recorded by layer (note, agents/core/safe_mode.py:115; status, agents/core/safe_mode.py:202), logged once per layer and at boot (agents/web.py:479), and said on /healthz as a boolean (agents/core/routers/ops.py:56), on /readyz, whose readiness is unaffected (agents/core/routers/ops.py:142), on /status (agents/core/routers/status.py:109) and /api/status (agents/core/routers/status.py:119). The HUD reads it from /status (frontend/src/api/loaders.ts) and shows a red SAFE MODE strip that names the layers left out and cannot be dismissed (SafeModeBanner, frontend/src/safe-mode-banner.tsx). Safe mode only removes: the kernel, the security, secrets and approval modules, the tool profiles, the capability actions and the egress policy do not read it, and a test pins the exact list of modules that do. The binding-writer inventory's pinned lines in web.py and scheduler_service.py move with the files. tests/test_h275_safe_mode.py (29 cases) covers the flag, the status and its order, the serve switch and that it does not leak into later tests, the data-home skills, a data-home skills folder that cannot be read, an owner skill in the bundled tree, every shipped skill still loading, the saved MCP servers not loaded and never rewritten, the add and remove routes refusing, the acquisition runtime and extensions, the plugin grants (least privilege unchanged), the persona and contract overlays from the data home and from the repository, the boundary refresh, the heartbeat overlay from both places, the owner jobs, /healthz, /readyz, /status and /api/status, and the no-relaxation property; src/test/safe-mode-banner.test.tsx and src/test/loaders.test.ts cover the HUD. 28 mutants over the gates, the routes, the switch and the banner: all caught (three after their cases were added: status after the flag is cleared, the unreadable folder, no heartbeat overlay). Test manual: ENV-167, ENV-168. Adapted from Hermes: one flag that leaves out the owner's layers rather than a separate minimal profile, with the MCP writes refused so the saved configuration survives the safe-mode run. | <a id="h283"></a>H283 | Know what kind of machine this is, and tell the model | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The hub now speaks systemd's sd_notify, and the operator can tell the model what kind of machine this is. Host detection was already richer than Hermes' (probe_host, agents/core/host_probe.py:406; detect_hardware, agents/core/hardware.py:215; /healthz and /readyz, agents/core/routers/ops.py:42 and :149), but nothing wrote to NOTIFY_SOCKET and the model was told only the live backend. agents/core/sd_notify.py: notify (agents/core/sd_notify.py:54) sends one datagram to NOTIFY_SOCKET, a path or an @abstract name (_address, agents/core/sd_notify.py:36), and never raises (a socket nobody listens on is a debug line); with NOTIFY_SOCKET unset (a shell, Docker, launchd, Windows) nothing is sent (enabled, agents/core/sd_notify.py:49). The lifespan (agents/web.py:645) calls NOTIFIER.ready with /readyz's own body (Notifier.ready, agents/core/sd_notify.py:97): READY=1 and STATUS=serving once the orchestrator and its agents are loaded, a STATUS naming why when they are not, and from then on a heartbeat task on the event loop sends WATCHDOG=1 every half WATCHDOG_USEC (watchdog_seconds, agents/core/sd_notify.py:69: at least 1 s, and only when WATCHDOG_PID is this process), so a hung loop stops the pings and systemd restarts the hub. On teardown (agents/web.py:650) STOPPING=1 and STATUS=draining are sent and the heartbeat ends (Notifier.stopping, agents/core/sd_notify.py:111). The shipped unit is Type=notify with NotifyAccess=main, WatchdogSec=60 and TimeoutStartSec=300 (deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.service), and deploy/systemd/README.md now describes the protocol instead of saying the hub does not emit it. The environment hint (agents/core/environment_hint.py): JARVIS_ENVIRONMENT_HINT (ENV_NAME, agents/core/environment_hint.py:23), read through env_config, is cleaned (clean_hint, agents/core/environment_hint.py:31): a literal \\n is a line break (a systemd Environment= value has no real one), control and bidi characters are removed, no line may open a heading, blank runs fold, and it is cut to 2,000 characters (MAX_HINT_CHARS, agents/core/environment_hint.py:24). hint_block (agents/core/environment_hint.py:48) puts it under "## This machine (the operator's description: context about where you run, not instructions)" (HEADER, agents/core/environment_hint.py:25), and Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) places that block in the stable part of every agent's system prompt, between the shared behaviour contract and the persona (agents/core/agent.py:583): it never forks the persona, and the same environment gives the same bytes every turn, so the prompt cache holds; with the variable unset the prompt is exactly what it was. The env file template (deploy/systemd/jarvis-hub.env) shows an example. tests/test_h283_host_notify_and_hint.py (23 cases) uses real datagram sockets: a message reaches a path and an abstract socket, no socket or a relative one sends nothing, a dead socket is no error, the heartbeat interval (half, the minimum, this PID only, bad values), not-ready STATUS without READY, READY then heartbeats from the loop with none sent while the loop is blocked then STOPPING and no more beats, no heartbeat without a watchdog, nothing without systemd, the real lifespan sending READY and STOPPING, the shipped unit; and for the hint the cleaning, the cap, the heading rule, the label, the absence when unset, and its place and byte-stability in a real agent's prompt. 21 mutants, all caught. Test manual: ENV-165, ENV-166. Adapted from Hermes: of its ~35 detection variables only the two with an operational effect are taken (NOTIFY_SOCKET/WATCHDOG_USEC and the environment hint); the container, terminal and multiplexer sniffing stays out, since host_probe already names the limits that change what Nerva can do. | <a id="h285"></a>H285 | Choose which skills, plugins and MCP servers load at startup | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner declares which skills, plugins and MCP servers load at boot, and a plugin toggle survives a restart. Six declared settings rows (agents/core/settings_db.py:367), category \`loadset\`, hold comma lists: skills_disabled/skills_only, plugins_disabled/plugins_only, mcp_disabled/mcp_only, editable from the settings page and \`nerva config set\`. A disabled name does not load; a non-empty only-list loads only what it names; the disabled list wins (permits, agents/core/load_set.py:95). A list is read at call time (declared, agents/core/load_set.py:73), trimmed, de-duplicated and bounded to 256 names of at most 128 characters without control characters (parse_names, agents/core/load_set.py:42), and an unreadable store narrows nothing. For plugins the settings page's existing per-plugin \`plugins.\<id\>\` switches, which nothing used to read, count too: an explicit off is off (agents/core/load_set.py:83). SkillLoader.discover reads the lists once per pass (agents/core/skills/loader.py:647) and a skill named by its registry name or its folder is not registered at all, before any quarantine row (agents/core/skills/loader.py:720). PermissionGate switches off the plugins the lists name when it is built (apply_load_set, agents/core/plugin_gate.py:647), and each gate now holds its own copies of the built-in manifests (agents/core/plugin_gate.py:641), so switching one off never reaches the module table or another gate. The plugin toggle (toggle_plugin, agents/core/routers/plugins.py:97) writes the choice into plugins_disabled, into plugins_only when one is declared and it is switched on, and into its own settings switch when it has one (persist_plugin, agents/core/load_set.py:139), and answers \`persisted\` (false when the store refused, the live toggle standing). The MCP load registers only the permitted saved servers (agents/web.py:1814); a switched-off one is held (_MCP_HELD, agents/web.py:1793) and written back unchanged by every save, so the saved configuration never loses it, and adding a server of that name or removing it answers 409 \`switched_off\` naming the list (_held_refusal, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:145). The lists only narrow: nothing is installed, approved, registered or written to the approval store, and a declared name that matches nothing installed is reported as unknown (finish, agents/core/load_set.py:120). The status rows carry it: /skills (agents/core/routers/skills.py:74), /plugins (agents/core/routers/plugins.py:90) and /api/admin/mcp (agents/core/routers/mcp.py:118) return \`load_set\` with the declared lists, what was switched off and what is unknown (status, agents/core/load_set.py:131). Safe mode (H275) applies first; the load set narrows further. tests/test_h285_load_set.py (34 cases) covers the parsing and its bounds, the six declared rows, the permit table, an unreadable store, a disabled skill and an only-list with their status rows and unknown names, a skill named by its folder, a named-but-absent skill installing or approving nothing, the skills route, MCP servers switched off and still saved, an MCP only-list, the add and remove refusals for a held server, adding and removing other servers keeping the held one, the MCP list route, the gate's disabled and only lists, the settings-page switch and that only an explicit off counts, that the lists never enable or register, the toggle kept across a restart both ways, a plugin without a settings switch, enabling under an only-list, a toggle the store refuses, and the plugins route. 33 mutants over the parser, the permit table, the persistence, the three loaders, the refusals and the status rows: all caught (two after their cases were added: an explicit off, a refused write). Test manual: ENV-169, ENV-170. Adapted from Hermes: the lists live in the settings store Nerva already persists and edits rather than a YAML file, the plugin toggle writes them, and an MCP server switched off is kept in the saved list rather than dropped. | <a id="h288"></a>H288 | Scheduled and queued work, and its delivery destination | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Three cron-lane clauses of Hermes' contract are unmet. The Kanban claim-lease and crash-grace asks are done. 1. HERMES_CRON_TIMEOUT: the plain cron path has no per-run wall clock. \`JobRunner._fire_once\` → \`_execute\` → \`_ask\`/\`_brief\`/\`_deliver\` (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1442-1504, 1550-1571, 1573-1612, 1683-1709) has no \`asyncio.wait_for\` at any level. In particular, \`_send_tracked\`/\`_send\` (jobs.py:1711-1744) awaits the channel adapter bare. A hung Telegram/Discord send therefore holds the job's gate stripe (jobs_dispatch.py:44-59) and its APScheduler \`max_instances=1\` slot, and nothing breaks it. The script path already does this correctly: \`asyncio.wait_for(self.runner._ask(...), 120)\` at agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py:405 and \`asyncio.wait_for(self.runner._send_tracked(...), 30)\` at :455. The fix is to apply the same bound to the cron path, ideally as a \`jobs.run_timeout_seconds\` setting next to the existing \`jobs.media_send_timeout_seconds\`. The overrun should be recorded as a normal failed run through \`_failed\`, so the 3-strike pause and the incident path still apply. 2. \`JARVIS_TASK_MAX_SECONDS\` defaults to 0, which means unbounded (agents/core/autonomy/executor.py:39-42, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1212-1215). Out of the box, a hung in-process queued task is bounded only by the 3600 s stuck-running reaper. Fix: give it a non-zero shipped default, or promote it to a validated \`autonomy.\*\` setting like \`running_ttl_seconds\`, and add a test asserting the default is finite. 3. HERMES_CRON_SESSION_DB_TIMEOUT: the jobs store opens sqlite with neither \`timeout=\` nor \`PRAGMA busy_timeout\` (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:734-737). The task queue sets \`timeout=30.0\` + \`PRAGMA busy_timeout=30000\` (agents/core/autonomy/queue.py:212-214). jobs.db writers therefore fall back to SQLite's 5 s default, on a WAL shared by the gateway drain loop and cron firings. One-line parity fix. Out of scope, not gaps: the row's own rationale excludes the delivery-destination knobs (_AUTO_DELIVER_PLATFORM / _CHAT_ID / _THREAD_ID, TELEGRAM_CRON_THREAD_ID). The residual ~14-knob multi-agent Kanban board (_BOARD / _WORKSPACES_ROOT / _ATTACHMENTS_ROOT / _GOAL_MODE / _DISPATCH_IN_GATEWAY) is a product decision about whether Nerva grows a shared task board, not a slice of this row. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/queue.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/worker.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy/executor.py) |

| <a id="h295"></a>H295 | Hide a tool for the turns where its backend is not usable | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Un profil nu probeaza backendul: execute_code, de exemplu, se inregistreaza pe un comutator al owner-ului si refuza la apel cu sandbox_not_isolated cand gazda nu are Docker/WASM, in loc sa dispara din oferta turei. Lipsesc check_fn per-unealta, cache-ul de ~30 s si fereastra last-known-good. [d1](../agents/core/tool_profiles.py) [d2](../tests/test_tool_profiles.py) |
| <a id="h296"></a>H296 | Adapt a tool's advertised schema to the live configuration | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A tool now tells the model what this install can do, and the live shape reaches a running session. register_tool takes schema_overrides (agents/core/tool_rpc.py:299), a zero-argument hook (SchemaOverrides, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:97) refused at registration when it is not callable. ToolRPCServer.tools() (agents/core/tool_rpc.py:366) asks every hook each time the tool list is built and merges the answer over the static description and schema (advertised_schema, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:145). The merge (_apply_override, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:103) takes only a \`description\` (non-empty text of at most 2,048 characters), \`properties\` whose keys are merged over properties the static schema already declares (an override narrows an argument, it never invents one) and \`required\` (a list naming declared properties); None or {} changes nothing, and the result must be plain JSON. A hook that raises, or answers with an unknown key, an undeclared property, a non-mapping, a bad \`required\` or a value JSON cannot carry, is logged at WARNING once per tool, naming the tool and the exception type but not its message, and the static schema is advertised (_OVERRIDE_WARNED, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:50); a hook that recovers is cleared and warns again if it fails again. The static schema is never mutated and every row is a copy. The registry projection gets the same shape, because its records are built from tools() on every snapshot (_tool_records, agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:310, inputs at agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:334). At the H672 compaction boundary refresh_tools compares each still-offered tool's description and schema (_shape, agents/core/session_refresh.py:95) and reports the ones that moved as \`reshaped\` (agents/core/session_refresh.py:153), which is a change and a rebuild (agents/core/session_refresh.py:159); the runtime's tool_offer_refreshed event names them (agents/core/agent_runtime.py:585) and rebuilds the specs, and boundary_note says "tool schemas moved: …" (agents/core/session_refresh.py:180). Three tools use it. terminal_run (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:642) advertises \`target\` as the registered target names, sorted and at most 64 (TargetRegistry.names, agents/core/environments/targets.py:245), or says in its description that terminal targets are switched off (JARVIS_TERMINAL_TARGETS) or that no target is registered, so every call is refused (_terminal_run_overrides, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1090). desktop_run (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:559) advertises its step \`action\` as the actions its validator's own table accepts (_desktop_run_overrides, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:100). speak (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:674) advertises \`target\` as the announce-capable device ids, the rooms whose one announce default resolves (an ambiguous room is left out) and presence:auto when the presence room resolves, at most 64 (MAX_ADVERTISED_TARGETS, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:144), or says that the Media Director is not available or that no speaker can announce yet (SpeakTool.schema_overrides, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:297). An override changes what the model is told; each handler still checks every call. tests/test_h296_schema_overrides.py (51 cases) covers the registration check, the merge, a row as a copy, nothing to change, the hook asked on every build, seventeen malformed or failing answers falling back to the static schema, the longest description, the one warning and its re-arm after recovery, the capability-registry projection, the boundary's reshape (a moved schema, a moved description, key order that is not a move, an added tool that is not also a reshape), a real tool loop in which a target registered mid-loop is offered after the fold with the event naming it, terminal_run switched off, with no target and with targets (sorted and bounded), desktop_run's enum without touching the static schema, and speak's devices, rooms and presence, an ambiguous room, a room named like a device, a stable room order, the bound, no announce speaker, no Media Director and the registered tool picking up a new speaker at once; tests/test_agent_runtime_v2.py pins the two changed rows of the wired tool list. 35 mutants over the merge, the fallback, the boundary, the event and the three overrides: 33 caught (five after their cases were added: an unknown key beside a known one, pairs instead of a mapping, a tuple for \`required\`, a row shared with the registered schema, the room order) and two equivalent (removing the mapping check or the declared-property check still raises on the next line). Test manual: GOV-261, GOV-262. Adapted from Hermes: dynamic_schema_overrides becomes a registration hook merged over a static schema that bounds what it may change, and a changed shape is picked up at Nerva's compaction boundary rather than on every call. [d1](../agents/core/tool_rpc.py) [d2](../agents/core/session_refresh.py) [d3](../agents/core/agent_runtime.py) |
| <a id="h297"></a>H297 | Execute a named tool call and return a bounded, well-formed result | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h298"></a>H298 | Keep a huge tool result out of context without losing it | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The complete frozen tool-result contract is wired into real tool turns: - the threshold precedence: pinned infinity (PINNED_THRESHOLDS: file_read, keyed on READ_TOOL, and since H315's review \`todo\`, whose store keeps its whole answer under 7,000 bytes), then owner override, the MCP family, tool declaration and the default threshold; - per-result/per-turn scaling; - bounded previews with full-file paths, sizes and hashes; - finalized-file retention; - centralized byte/line/line-length limits. Since H661 the spill notice is an exact, followable call. \`page_recipe\` fills in \`file_read(path=…, offset=0, max_bytes=…)\` with the resolved path, the preview and execute_code stream fields carry it as text and as \`read_with\` arguments, and \`file_read\` pages from any offset and returns \`next_offset\`. The call is checked, not assumed: the store's \`read_back\` probe is asked about that exact path. The coordinator wires it to three checks: registry + job-toolset allowance, the turn's own offer, and FileTools reach. The spill directory is also a read-only door for exact spill names, and spill names never look like secrets. When file_read cannot page the file from this turn, the notice says the file is kept for the owner and does not name a dead call. Other guarantees: - Exclusive per-writer temporary files preserve concurrent stream and whole-result receipts. - A shape-truncated stream keeps its bytes even when its annotation makes the preview longer. - Upstream byte truncation and small-output discard still work. Ordinary Agent and Orchestrator tool paths snapshot configured/cached backend capacity before guardrails. They forward immutable per-turn metadata without unwrapping or bypassing generation. Known capacity and resolved job bounds constrain the completion reserve, initial and later transcripts, schemas, structured tool arguments and finite result thresholds, and file_read never re-spills. Invalid metadata refuses the tool turn. Unknown capacity, including adapters without loaded metadata, keeps the documented model-family estimate, and token accounting remains heuristic. Re-run at 55d625f: 243 cases pass across tests/test_tool_result_store.py, test_tool_loop_result_spill.py, test_environment_output_limits.py, test_code_tools.py, test_effective_tool_window.py and test_spill_paging.py. Honest limits: - A \*streamed\* spill now has a ceiling, DEFAULT_MAX_STREAM_BYTES = 5,000,000, which the code calls Hermes' own figure. Past it, the file ends in one explicit '\[... spill capped ...\]' marker, the result reports capped/kept_bytes/file_bytes, and the stream's end survives only in the inline tail. Whole-result spills are uncapped. - Retention has a newest-file exception. - Interrupted captures, orphan cleanup and arbitrary storage failure are not permanent complete-output guarantees. - This is code equivalence, not live provider/Windows containment proof. | <a id="h303"></a>H303 | Read files and search a codebase | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Paging is by bytes, not lines: file_read output is not line-numbered, there is no 1-indexed line offset/limit window, and there is no per-line length cap, so one long line can fill a page. file_search matches a literal phrase, not a regex, with a Python line scan rather than ripgrep. There is no glob file-finding mode: the glob only narrows which files' contents are searched, a pattern is required, and file_list lists a single directory. Document extraction covers .pdf and .docx only. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../tests/test_file_search.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_read_documents.py) |

| <a id="h309"></a>H309 | Show the user where something is, in their UI | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The model points at the owner's HUD, as Hermes' pointing does. agents/core/canvas.py gains two element types. A tip is a target and a one-line caption; a tour is a title and at most MAX_TOUR_STEPS (8) such steps. Both are sanitised in _sanitize: whitespace is collapsed, captions are cut to MAX_CAPTION (160), and a target must be one of POINTER_ANCHORS. Those 19 anchors are the rail's 16 modes, the message box, the Decision Inbox panel and the Console button; no single approve or reject button is one. agents/core/pointer_tool.py registers canvas_point, an ungated ToolRPC tool whose schema offers exactly those anchors (register_pointer_tool, wired in agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py on the live orch.canvas). It posts through CanvasStore.post as the calling agent and refuses a call that is both or neither by name. A tip written by an untrusted turn, by a turn that is not the owner's, or by a turn whose origin cannot be read is marked untrusted. It is not in DEFAULT_GUEST_TOOLS. frontend/src/pointer.tsx mirrors POINTER_ANCHORS (a parity test reads both) and mounts PointerOverlay in app.tsx (not in demo or the floating window). The overlay polls GET /api/canvas every 5 s and takes the newest tip or tour of the last 600 s that this page has not closed (currentPointer; closes kept in sessionStorage). It finds the element carrying that data-anchor (added to the rail buttons, the composer, the decisions panel and the Console button), rings it, and draws the caption with an arrow, flipping above near the window's bottom. A tour pages with Back/Next/Done and restarts at step 1 for a new tour. A target not on screen is named, and an untrusted tip says so. frontend/src/artifacts.tsx lists tips and tours as text. Tests: tests/test_h309_pointer.py (38), frontend/src/test/pointer.test.tsx (10), artifacts.test.tsx (+1); 37 mutants, all killed. [d1](../agents/core/canvas.py) [d2](../agents/core/pointer_tool.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) |
