| <a id="h313"></a>H313 | Speak | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The model can decide to say one short text aloud on one room's device, and can do it again. register_speak_tool (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:640) adds a ToolRPC \`speak\` tool with gated=True, trusted execution and its own gated intake (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:667, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:673). It is registered only while JARVIS_MEDIA_DIRECTOR is on (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:653), and AutonomyCoordinator wires it with the broker kernel and the governed enqueue (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:500, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1391). It reaches the same Media Director instance the media routes use, so the owner's device registry and session board apply (agents/core/routers/media_director.py:272). SpeakTool.preflight (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:344) runs at proposal and again before the approved run. It refuses text over the spoken-reply cap and code-only text (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:350, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:352). It resolves a room to that room's one announce-capable default device, so the card title and the persisted args name the speaker (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:237, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:423). It raises no card the run could only refuse: unified API off, kernel off or unbound, no media root, no speech backend, no driver for the device, or no configured presence room each refuse by name (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:371, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:366). SpeakTool.intake (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:401) shows the kernel the exact toolrpc.speak row it enqueues. Under the production composition (worker.kernel_gate over MediationKernelBridge, agents/core/autonomy/worker.py:220, agents/core/kernel/binding.py:108) that decision binds as the row's intake evidence, and a DENY raises no card (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:427). Only the human-accepted durable toolrpc.speak row runs. The kind is trusted (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:66) and the executor routes it to the trusted rail (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:1412-1415). ToolRPC re-runs the preflight and requires the trusted execution context (agents/core/tool_rpc.py:545, agents/core/tool_rpc.py:573), and the handler refuses a row of any other kind (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:497). SpeakTool.execute (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:492) synthesizes the clip through SpokenReply on the host TTSEngine in the owner's configured voice (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:508, agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:233, agents/core/voice/tts.py:150). It writes the clip owner-only under the first media root (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:595, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:609) and presents it only through CapabilityActionAPI.perform('action:media.present') in announce mode (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:548, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:553). The facade asks the Action Kernel before the handler runs (agents/core/capability_actions.py:168) and refuses on a DENY (agents/core/capability_actions.py:175), so the kernel authorizes the effect and the tool never reaches a driver. A kernel QUEUE on that present is honoured as the accepted row and audited as speak.durably_approved (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:456, agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:478). A DENY becomes kernel_denied with the kernel's reason (agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py:570). The Media Director treats an announce as a one-shot notice (agents/core/media_director.py:738): it never blocks the next present and costs no interrupt budget (agents/core/media_director.py:890), and whatever it cut into stays the restore point (agents/core/media_director.py:992). Because the tool is gated, tool_profiles offers it only to the owner on the operator surface (agents/core/tool_profiles.py:190, agents/core/tool_profiles.py:194), and the snapshot pins this (tests/_snapshots/tool_profiles.json:50, tests/test_tool_profiles.py:202). Tests cover: registration and postures (tests/test_speak_tool.py:170, tests/test_speak_tool.py:196); named preflight refusals that enqueue nothing (tests/test_speak_tool.py:271, tests/test_speak_tool.py:320); and the card naming the device (tests/test_speak_tool.py:286). With the real kernel.authorize, AutonomyPolicy and KillSwitch behind worker.kernel_gate, an approved speak is mediated and audited, and an engaged kill switch refuses both the run and the card (tests/test_speak_tool.py:520, tests/test_speak_tool.py:543, tests/test_speak_tool.py:576). Three back-to-back normal speaks on one device all play at no budget cost (tests/test_speak_tool.py:591). Speaking over music needs high urgency, pays once and keeps the music as the restore point (tests/test_speak_tool.py:608, tests/test_media_director.py:762). A kernel DENY, a missing TTS backend, a missing media root and a driverless device each refuse by name with no playback, and an engine that yields no audio refuses as tts_failed with nothing presented (tests/test_speak_tool.py:681, tests/test_speak_tool.py:469, tests/test_speak_tool.py:666, tests/test_speak_tool.py:703, tests/test_speak_tool.py:716). A row of another kind cannot speak (tests/test_speak_tool.py:753), and the clip spool stays bounded (tests/test_speak_tool.py:779). Known limits outside the accepted adaptation: an accepted speak runs at the next autonomy tick (default 60 s, floor 15 s, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:291), like every gated tool. A presence:auto card names the sentinel, because only play time knows whether the owner is in the presence room. Audible output needs an owner-wired speaker driver (the in-repo NullMediaDriver refuses and LocalFileMediaDriver is a reference), the same host seam as every media.present. The speech-backend gate needs edge-tts or Kokoro installed, like the spoken-reply channel. An announce of any length, including one sent through POST /api/media/present, no longer holds its device, as docs/FLAGS.md states. Hermes's tts.provider, custom command providers and tts.speed are outside the accepted adaptation, because Nerva's TTS subsystem was already ahead. [d1](../agents/core/voice/speak_tool.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) [d3](../agents/core/media_director.py) |
| <a id="h314"></a>H314 | Let the model decide what to remember | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The model now writes its own long-term memory during a turn, through a tool that is atomic, guarded, recorded and undoable. \`memory\` (register_memory_tool, agents/core/memory_tool.py:354; registered ungated at agents/core/memory_tool.py:457 and wired at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:950) takes \`operations\`, each an add, a replace of the one fact \`old_text\` matches, or a remove of it, against target \`memory\` (the LivingMemory \`core\` ring of agent notes) or \`user\` (the \`user_core\` profile ring) (TARGETS, agents/core/memory_tool.py:58); a call with no operations reads both. The rings are reached only when cognition memory is on (_living_memory, agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:944), and otherwise the tool's offer says memory is off through the H296 schema hook (agents/core/memory_tool.py:400). Every operation is checked on a working copy before anything is written (plan, agents/core/memory_tool.py:146): one line of at most 300 characters, the core block's own cut (MAX_FACT_CHARS, agents/core/memory_tool.py:60), with no control, format or private-use character (_clean_text, agents/core/memory_tool.py:109), scanned with the normalised injection scanner (_scan, agents/core/memory_tool.py:127), an old_text that matches exactly one fact (_match, agents/core/memory_tool.py:136), no duplicate, and a full ring refusing an add instead of silently dropping its oldest fact; one refusal changes nothing. The rings are then written by a new compare-and-set (CoreMemory.compare_and_set, agents/core/cognition/memory.py:314) under the lock a forget clears them with, so a forget or another write that ran in between wins and nothing forgotten is written back; a second ring that fails puts the first back (_commit, agents/core/memory_tool.py:272). Only a clean owner turn at the operator surface writes: the resolver offers the tool to operator/owner alone, whatever llm.guest_tools says (OWNER_OPERATOR_TOOLS, agents/core/tool_profiles.py:102 and :237), the handler refuses any other posture (agents/core/memory_tool.py:390) and a turn whose origin is untrusted, an inbound message, web content or a flagged recall (agents/core/memory_tool.py:393), so an injected instruction cannot make itself permanent. Every write is committed and then recorded as an intent-log \`memory.write\` entry carrying the actor, the undo ref, the targets and per operation its action and a hash of the fact, never the text, since the intent log survives a forget (agents/core/memory_tool.py:437); a record that cannot be made puts the rings back (agents/core/memory_tool.py:442), and with no intent log nothing is written (_record, agents/core/memory_tool.py:264). The rings' previous contents are kept under the ref in a bounded undo store beside the rings, which a forget deletes with them (UndoStore, agents/core/memory_tool.py:208). undo (agents/core/memory_tool.py:307) restores a write while the rings still hold what it wrote, by the same compare-and-set, recorded as \`memory.undo\`; the owner reads the rings and the undoable writes at GET /api/memory/core (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:872) and undoes one at POST /api/memory/core/undo, admin-only (agents/core/routers/memory_kg.py:885). The Console's Long-Term Memory panel shows both rings and the recent writes with an undo each, and says when an undo is refused (LongTermMemoryPanel, frontend/src/panels/long-term-memory.tsx). A write leaves a \`memory_updated\` trail row with targets and actions, never text (_record_event, agents/core/memory_tool.py:335). The prompt's core block stays frozen per session, so a write reaches the system prompt at the next session and the tool answers with both rings as they now are. tests/test_h314_memory_tool.py (57 cases) covers the plan and every refusal (twenty-three malformed, injected, ambiguous, duplicate or over-full operations), the compare-and-set and a ring that cannot be saved, an owner write of both rings with its record and its undo, a read, an add already saved, the five postures and untrusted origins, a failing getter, memory switched off and its offer, a broken memory module, an unknown argument, no intent log or one without a record method, a record that fails, a forget between the read and the write, a second ring that fails, a full ring, undo after the ring moved, undo of a replace and a remove, undo without memory, an undo that cannot be recorded, the undo store's bound, the trail row, the profile offer and the routes; frontend/src/test/long-term-memory-panel.test.tsx pins the panel. 33 mutants: 32 caught (four after their cases were added: a raw over-long padded fact, an audit sink without record, an empty operation list, the undo listing's limit) and one equivalent (rewriting a ring the call did not change). Test manual: MEM-204, MEM-205. Adapted from Hermes: its memory tool's add/replace/remove on the two injected stores, with the writes restricted to the owner's clean turns, committed by compare-and-set, recorded in Nerva's intent log without their text and undoable by the owner. [d1](../agents/core/memory_tool.py) [d2](../agents/core/cognition/memory.py) [d3](../agents/core/tool_profiles.py) |
| <a id="h315"></a>H315 | Keep a visible checklist of what the agent is doing | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207, then fixed after nine adversarial reviews. The model keeps a checklist of its own work and re-reads all of it on every change. The owner can read the same list, with who wrote each item and whether its text is trusted, before any approval card it leads to. The tool. agents/core/todo_tool.py registers \`todo\` on the ToolRPC server, ungated (agents/core/todo_tool.py:590), with the schema {todos: \[{id, content, status, parent?}\], merge} (agents/core/todo_tool.py:90); the optional parent is H666's. - Items use Hermes' four statuses: pending, in_progress, completed, cancelled. A call with todos replaces the list. With merge=true it updates items by id, changing only the fields sent, and appends new ones. A call without todos reads the list. Every call answers with the whole list and its counts. - TodoStore.apply (agents/core/todo_tool.py:296) is bounded: at most 50 items, content of one printable line up to 200 characters, ids of 1 to 64 characters, 7,000 bytes for the whole list in the UTF-8 the tool sends it in, and at most one item in progress. A merge that only moves statuses is never refused as too long. - Control and format characters are dropped, text is NFC, each character keeps at most three combining marks (enclosing marks included), and whitespace runs collapse. Text that renders blank (only fillers or marks) is no content; punctuation or a symbol alone is content (agents/core/todo_tool.py:136). So what the owner reads is what was written. Two ids that are canonically equal are one id; look-alikes from another script or width stay two. - Every refusal is named, and each comes with a sentence the model can act on: todo_too_many, todo_content_too_long, todo_content_required, todo_bad_status, todo_bad_id, todo_duplicate_id, todo_one_in_progress, todo_bad_list, todo_bad_item, todo_bad_merge, todo_no_session, todo_unknown_field (a key the schema does not have, top level or item), todo_plan_too_long and todo_shared_session. A refused call changes nothing, because a merge is built on a copy. Whose plan it is. The coordinator passes the session, the turn's posture and whether the turn runs on the shared default session (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:874; Orchestrator.on_shared_session, agents/core/orchestrator.py:875). A turn keeps the answer it got when it resolved its session (agents/core/orchestrator.py:286), so an owner resuming another session does not let an in-flight turn in. A turn that binds no session of its own runs on that shared session, and the plan kept there is the owner's. Such turns include the HUD, the web widget (its route binds none), voice, rooms, notes, workflows, MCP ask_\*, a webhook, a job, a subagent and a direct POST /api/toolrpc/call. - On it, only an owner's turn keeps a plan. Anyone else is refused todo_shared_session and is never read the plan (agents/core/todo_tool.py:561). Neither the turn's offer nor a script's reach includes the tool for them (SESSION_SCOPED_TOOLS, agents/core/tool_profiles.py:235; agents/core/code_tools.py:362). Voice carries no identity, so even the owner's voice is a guest's turn there and keeps no plan. - A turn on any other session keeps that session's plan, whoever it is. That covers a chat of its own and an explicit session a user token continues. Text written there by a turn that is not the owner's (a household member, a guest) is untrusted to the owner, and the owner's next turn reads it as DATA (agents/core/todo_tool.py:574). - Otherwise it is offered like any ungated tool. An inbound guest reaches it through \`llm.guest_tools\`, whose default is now echo, time and todo (agents/core/tool_profiles.py:84). An install that still holds the old default (echo, time) gets the new one once, at start (agents/core/settings_db.py:520). A list the owner changed is left alone. An owner who empties it keeps guests off the tool loop entirely. - At most 256 plans are kept in memory. The one the agent least recently wrote or read is dropped first, and an empty list frees its slot. Where its text came from. Each item records three things: the posture of the turn that set its text (by), which turn that was, and whether it was untrusted (tainted). A turn is untrusted when it came from an inbound channel (the owner's own Telegram DM included), is not the owner's, or had already read untrusted content. - A status set by an untrusted turn taints the item too. A clean rewrite of the text keeps the taint, in a merge or a replace alike, because the id came from whoever wrote it first (review-H315f n3). The same text again is no write, in a merge or a replace: the item keeps its writer, its turn, its taint and the plan's labels, and a status or place moved by an untrusted turn still taints it (review-H315e n2; agents/core/todo_tool.py:367). A status re-sent unchanged moves nothing in either mode. - A call that answers with a tainted item another turn wrote says so (agents/core/todo_tool.py:587). The loop then fences the answer as DATA and raises the reading turn's taint. An item the reading turn wrote itself is its own argument, already in its transcript, and is not fenced (agents/core/todo_tool.py:472; the loop gives each run a turn token, agents/core/agent_runtime.py:365). - So the owner's HUD turn that reads a plan written from Telegram is tainted by it. That follows the kernel's inbound policy, and the HUD tags such items "untrusted source". - A script's own writes cannot launder text into the plan (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:37). The broker for an execute_code script's calls reads an answer as the loop reads a result (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:106): it raises the origin once a tool that declares untrusted_output answers ok, an answer says tainted, or the answer is flagged as the loop's fence flags one (an injection pattern anywhere in it, or the fence's own markers). Once the run is tainted, by the broker's own flag or by the origin an earlier read raised, nothing is scanned: the K1 loop builds a broker per request, so the origin is what carries it there (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:104; review-H315f m3). Otherwise a small answer (under 16 KiB as it encodes, counted exactly with the encoder's own string function: a newline costs two characters and a quote two, non-ASCII one, bytes as str() spells them, and the separators between items (review-H315i m1, n4; review-H315j n1, n2), a container wider than what is left is refused before it is listed, and a value JSON spells through str(), a huge int or an object, is never small) is scanned inline, where it costs microseconds, and a larger one on the scan's own single thread, never the loop's default pool, so one script's small calls do not queue behind other scripts' multi-megabyte scans (review-H315g m2). One thread, not four: the scan holds the GIL, so more gave no throughput and made the loop wait 4-5x longer at p99 (review-H315h m2, review-H315h n1). An answer too deep to encode is flagged, never raised out of the broker. It covers the whole answer in overlapping 64 KiB slices, and a slice never ends inside a word of up to 256 more characters, so a pattern's closing boundary is the answer's own (review-H315g n2); no single regex call holds the interpreter lock for a large answer, and a forked child gets a pool of its own (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:224; review-H315e m2). A refusal, or a tool that raised, taints nothing. Every later call of that run then writes tainted items. - A script's calls belong to no model turn (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:89), so what it wrote is data even to the turn that ran it. - A session kernel that has held untrusted text taints every later cell's calls until a reset replaces its record (agents/core/session_kernels.py:829). It has held it once a cell read it, or ran in a turn that had. Each interpreter gets a mailbox directory of its own, removed with it (a reset, a crash, an idle expiry, an eviction, a failed start). The directories sit under one per manager (p\<pid\>-\<token\>), whose lock (an exclusive flock on its .lock) the manager holds while its process runs. The directory is made and locked under a temporary name and only then renamed into place, so no start ever finds it with a lock nobody holds; before each mount the manager checks that the lock it holds is still the file there, and when it is not (the file is gone or another file has the name) locks a new file there again, where its live mounts are (made and locked under a staging name, then renamed over .lock, so the name never holds a file nobody has locked: review-H315i m2), taking a new directory only when that cannot be had and giving no mount when no lock can be had at all (review-H315g m1). An owner path that is no longer a directory (ENOTDIR, a link loop) or an fd that is gone (EBADF) reads as lost too, and closing that dead fd never fails the mount (review-H315i n1, review-H315j m2); any other error reading the lock (ESTALE, EACCES) says nothing about it, so the lock is kept: giving it up let the next start remove the live mounts under it (review-H315h m1; agents/core/session_kernels.py:129, agents/core/session_kernels.py:160). With no flock (Windows) a kernel is mounted as before, and the directory ages out. A start removes an owner directory whose lock it can take and that is still the file at .lock after it took it (a file an owner relocked away from is an orphan that says nothing about it: review-H315j m1), which is a process that ended, in any pid namespace sharing the root: a pid is not asked, since the hub is PID 1 on every container start (review-H315f m1; agents/core/session_kernels.py:234, agents/core/session_kernels.py:1144). A directory with no lock (an older layout) ages out after the idle expiry, an owner directory by its newest interpreter's time, which a busy interpreter moves. A lock that cannot be asked (another user's, ENOLCK), or a directory that cannot even be looked into (another user's under umask 077), reads as alive and is kept, and one unreadable entry never stops a sweep or a start (review-H315i m3). So two processes on one data root (jarvis-hub and jarvis-runtime, or a container and the host) never delete each other's live mounts (review-H315e m1), and a file a tainted kernel wrote there never reaches the clean kernel after it. - A run's result says tainted whenever it printed anything, read untrusted text, or ran on a kernel that held some (agents/core/code_tools.py:604), so the loop fences its output and raises the turn's taint whether the run succeeded or failed. A plan therefore cannot carry an injected instruction into a later, clean turn as the tool's own words, and the web tools' taint refusals and the kernel's queueing apply to the turn that ran the script. The loop. It never replaces the answer with a "same as call N" stub, and never counts a todo call against a per-tool cap (agents/core/agent_runtime.py:125). The cap setting's label says so (agents/core/settings_db.py:181), and a start gives a stored row the current label (agents/core/settings_db.py:506). - The repeat detector keys a todo call on its arguments and on the plan the model last saw (agents/core/agent_runtime.py:1612). A read after the plan changed is the tool doing its job. The same call with nothing changed in between is stopped like any other repeat: the third is refused and the fourth ends the turn. A failed call leaves the plan the model saw unchanged. A script (execute_code) may have changed the plan behind the model's back. So each one that made tool calls, or did not finish cleanly (a crashed one reports none), opens its own revision, and the next read after it is a new call, however many scripts ran (review-H315e M1; agents/core/agent_runtime.py:1583). The revision is applied after the reads of the script's own step, which answered the plan from before it (review-H315f m2). A script that ran cleanly and called nothing changed nothing, so the repeat stop holds across it (review-H315f n2), as does one execute_code refused before any script ran (switched off, no sandbox, not isolated, no authority) or a session-kernel call refused before its cell (e-stop, expired, denied, no kernel, an empty or overlong cell; review-H315h n2), but not an unconfirmed teardown, which also ends a cell that ran; a handler that raised may have raised after its script ran, and opens a revision. A K2 cell's call count is the host's, never the count the kernel reports, and 0 when the cell had no mailbox (review-H315g n1, review-H315h n2). - The answer is never cut: 7,000 bytes fits the smallest per-result budget, and the tool is pinned whole even after the turn's allowance is spent (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:100). - The description asks the model to mark the item it starts in_progress, and completed when it is done. The owner's view: - A write leaves a todo_updated event in the tool trail (agents/core/todo_tool.py:484). It carries the session, the statuses, the position of the item in progress and the total. It never carries the text or the ids, which are free text too. A read or a refusal leaves no event. - GET /sessions/todo and GET /sessions/{id}/todo (agents/core/routers/sessions.py:102, agents/core/routers/sessions.py:111) are user-guarded and no-store; an invalid id is a 400. - Each item carries its by and tainted, never its turn. The recent list puts first the plan the agent most recently wrote or read. A plan's labels (when, which agent, whose turn) move only when its list changes. - \`nerva todo \[SESSION\]\` (agents/cli/nerva.py:1563) prints the plans with \[ \] \[\>\] \[x\] \[-\] marks and each item's tags (agents/cli/nerva.py:1521): guest turn, household turn, background turn, untrusted source. A bad session id exits 2 before any request. A malformed reply, a non-object item included, exits 1. - The Decision Inbox shows the plans with open work under the decisions (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx:65, mounted at frontend/src/gap.tsx:3109). It shows at most three plans of eight items each, how long ago each plan was written, and every tag of each item (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx:39). A failed read is shown as failed, with its reason, not as "no plans". - The capability registry calls the tool reversible session state with a compensating rollback: a later call can write back what a call replaced, and the tool keeps no copy of it (agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:283). - A forget clears every plan (agents/core/data_purge.py:547) and lists todo_plans in live_stores_cleared. A turn already running when the purge starts can still write its plan afterwards, just as it still saves its reply. Adapted: Hermes classifies todo as mutating in its loop guardrails. Here it is ungated because it writes only its own session's list: the shared session's list only for the owner's turns, and every item's origin is kept and shown. Tests: tests/test_todo_tool.py (36 cases) covers the store, the tool, the loop, the routes, the purge and the CLI. tests/test_h315b_todo_review.py (56 cases, first review) covers: - the shared session through the real routes. A widget visitor, a webhook (tests/test_h315b_todo_review.py:187), a subagent and a direct tool call (tests/test_h315b_todo_review.py:216) neither read nor replace the owner's plan; - a later turn tainted by text copied from an untrusted page (tests/test_h315b_todo_review.py:353); - status updates between steps, which never end the turn (tests/test_h315b_todo_review.py:395). tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py (37 cases, second review) covers: - scripts in the real subprocess sandbox (tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py:115). A script that read an untrusted tool or a tainted plan writes tainted items, and a clean one writes clean items. Its write is data even to the turn that ran it; - the real session kernel (tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py:241). A later clean cell writes tainted items, and a reset kernel's record starts clean; - the upgrade migration and the label refresh (tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py:328); - the pinned shared verdict (tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py:381); - a turn's own plan unfenced (tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py:413); - the repeat detector's revisions (tests/test_h315c_todo_review.py:482); - a household member's text, the status and rewrite taint, the labels, the status-only cap, the byte measure, the mark cap, punctuation content, the resolver default, the script's reach, the capability record and the CLI's non-object items. tests/test_h315d_todo_review.py (27 cases, third review) covers: - a script and a kernel cell that read a page and fail, printed or silent, through the real loop: fenced, the turn tainted, the plan write tainted (tests/test_h315d_todo_review.py:90); - a kernel's files gone after a reset, a crash and a restart (tests/test_h315d_todo_review.py:183); - the broker's rule (a refusal, a raising tool, a scanner flag), a same-text merge, an untrusted turn's status, the posture rule, the coordinator's wiring, a failing shared-session getter, bind's default, a channel session that is the default, and the repeat detector after a script (tests/test_h315d_todo_review.py:262). tests/test_h315e_todo_review.py (13 cases, fourth review) covers plan reads after several scripts and after a crashed one, the sweep of a dead process's mounts beside a live one's, a failed start, and the broker's deep scan and markers. tests/test_h315f_todo_review.py (20 cases, fifth review) covers liveness by the owner's lock through a real second process whose pid names nothing here, a dead PID 1's directory, an out-of-range pid, per-interpreter directories, a refused or cancelled start, a read beside a script in one step, a crashed K2 cell, the K1 scan stopping once tainted, the scan's own thread, slices across a boundary and 1.5 MB deep, the fence's open marker, and the two write modes' parity. tests/test_h315g_todo_review.py (26 cases, sixth review) covers two concurrent runs on the one shared sandbox each running their own code (every run wrote the same script.py, so one ran the other's script against the other's tool-RPC mailbox and answered it to the wrong caller; each run now writes a file of its own and removes it, agents/core/sandbox.py:281), the owner lock never seen unheld, a swept or replaced lock, no mount without a lock, a lockless owner aged on its newest interpreter, a symlinked lock, locks that cannot be asked, the inline and pooled scans, the size walk, a slice at a word, a forked child's pool, when a script opens a revision, a K2 cell that zeroes its count, and a clean rewrite of a clean item and the rewriting turn's own read. tests/test_h315j_todo_review.py (10 cases, ninth review) covers a gone fd that still gives a mount, an fstat error other than EBADF, ELOOP, a lock file replaced while it is judged, a failed relock's staging file, a root that cannot be listed or searched, non-ASCII text, bytes, and separators. tests/test_h315i_todo_review.py (16 cases, eighth review) covers a multi-line answer of a few KiB scanned inline, quotes and backslashes counted as they encode, a string and a wide dict refused before they are looked at, a huge negative int, a start during the relock, a directory that cannot be looked into, one unreadable entry in a sweep, a stat that keeps failing, no lock at start, an owner directory replaced by a link, an owner path that is a file, an fd that is gone, and empty file names (tests/test_h315i_todo_review.py:29). tests/test_h315h_todo_review.py (34 cases, seventh review) covers one failed stat that keeps the lock and the live mount, errors that say nothing about the lock, a lock file that went or was replaced being held again where the mounts live, one replaced while it is being locked, one someone else holds, no fd leaked over 20 switches, a mount with no flock, no staging directory after a failed lock, a forked child's large scan (the hook itself), the size walk (bytes, dict keys, scalars, a container refused before it is listed, a huge int, an object, control characters), an answer too deep to encode, an underscore at a slice end, the session kernel's own refusals and an unconfirmed teardown, a raising tool that is not a script, a K2 cell that inflates its count, a cell with no mailbox, and a dotted directory and a dotfile as the run's file name (tests/test_h315h_todo_review.py:36). frontend/src/test/plans-in-flight.test.tsx (10 cases) covers the inbox section: the admin credential, marks with spoken names, every tag of an item, the plan's age at its boundaries, the caps, cancelled items struck through, a failed read with its reason, and reload. Mutation, second round: 48 mutants of its guards (42 backend, 6 HUD), each run against its own suites. 38 were caught at once. Of the other ten: - Two were code with no effect, and were removed: a broker per script run (the service loop's one context already carries the raised origin) and the kernel's mark before a cell (the broker and the mark after the cell cover it). - One is equivalent: the HUD's clamp at zero, since a negative age already reads "just now". - Seven got tests that now catch them. The tests also catch one new mutant: a broker that keeps its flag without marking the context. - The second review's own set left 12 mutants no test caught. Re-run against this code, all 12 are caught. One of them, the resolver's fail-closed default, is now the code itself, and flipping it back to fail-open is caught. Mutation, third round: 15 mutants of its guards, each run against its own suites. 12 were caught at once. Three were caught once a test was tightened or added: a cell's taint when it printed nothing (twice) and a script that made no tool calls. The third review's own 76 mutants left 11 that are not equivalent; each now has a test. The fourth review's own 30 mutants left five that are not equivalent (an untrusted rewrite of a clean item, a scan limited to the head of an answer, a refused start's mount, per-key mounts, stderr-only output) and the third review's X27 (a same-list replace moving the turn). That round's 15 mutants were all caught, one after a case was added, and one equivalent branch was removed. A raising tool taints nothing by design, and that is pinned too. The fifth review found two of those five still unpinned (per-key mounts, a refused start) and X27 equivalent once a same-text replace keeps its turn. Its own 32 mutants left seven that are not equivalent: - four have a test now: an untrusted replace that changes the text, an untrusted re-parent, the fence's open marker and a cancelled start; - the EPERM case went with the pid check; - the two on the moved item's turn are left, since they change only whether the moving turn's own later read is fenced. The two it found unpinned have tests now. So does a crashed K2 cell, which only a print-only test had caught. That round's 15 mutants were all caught, one after its case was corrected. The sixth review's own 35 mutants left six that are not equivalent (a clean rewrite of a clean item, a refused script between reads, the rewriting turn's turn, a symlinked lock, two lock errors); each has a test now. That round's 16 mutants were all caught, two after their cases were added. The seventh review's own 36 mutants left 11 that are not equivalent (the fork hook itself, a mount with no flock, bytes sizes, a raising tool that is not a script, an inflated K2 count, a kept fd, a failed lock's staging directory, an underscore at a slice end, a dotfile's name, dict keys, scalars); each has a test now. That round's 23 mutants were all caught. The eighth review's 38 mutants left six that are not equivalent (a stat error that persists, no lock at start, the walk's two early exits, an owner directory replaced by a link, a huge negative int); each has a test now. That round's 14 mutants were all caught. The ninth review's 27 mutants left six that are not equivalent (ELOOP, a failed relock's staging file, a root that cannot be listed, ASCII-only counting, a fixed staging name, bytes decoded); five have tests now, and a fixed staging name only matters for a crash leftover, which O_EXCL then refuses and the next relock takes a new directory. This round's 10 mutants were all caught, one after its case was tightened (dict separators). | <a id="h318"></a>H318 | List, read and author the agent's own skills | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207, with H340 in the same change (critic note 21: one skill_view), and fixed after seven adversarial reviews. The first (review-H318: two majors, six minors, nine nits) found an unkeyed signature counted as a vouch and approved changes that never landed. The second (review-H318b: two majors, seven minors, seven nits) found that an approved change disabled the skill it improved (a bundled skill unbundled, a signed or approved one losing its standing) and that the owner still approved without seeing the change. The third (review-H318c: one records major, eight minors, seven nits) found a write between the standing read and the renewal could ride into the vouch, a renewal failure reported as applied, a keyed signature overwritten while its key was missing, a publish that silently dropped a linked file, and cards that could be lost or left pending. The fourth (review-H318d: one major, five minors, six nits) found that text-mode SKILL.md writes made every vouched apply fail on Windows, a partial renewal left a new signature over the old text, publish still followed a link planted after its check, and cards beyond the route's page were called inert. The fifth (review-H318e: two minors, seven nits) found the old SKILL.sig read after the write with no rollback behind it, text-mode test fixtures on the Windows lane, a signature that could not be put back reported as the old text staying, a truncated write, the review superseding by the record's origin, a /v1 card approvable without its diff, and publish describing another read of SKILL.md. The sixth (review-H318f: one minor, four nits) found the whole-file write staging its temporary file inside the skill, where one a crash left would void the signature. The seventh (review-H318g: two minors, five nits) found a standing that could not be read for a moment read as unsigned, and the crash-safety claim wider than the code. All are fixed or stated below; two nits are stated by decision below. Hermes gives the agent skills_list, skill_view (a skill's SKILL.md or one of its linked files) and skill_manage (create, patch and delete). Nerva now offers all three powers under its governance: the two reads as ungated read-only ToolRPC tools, and authoring as a proposal the owner approves. The three tools are in agents/core/skills/tools.py; register_skill_tools (agents/core/skills/tools.py:192) registers them on the orchestrator's ToolRPC server in AutonomyCoordinator, next to todo (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:961). The trust gate: - SkillLoader.catalog_gate (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1329) is the prompt catalog's gate, factored out and now shared by the catalog, skills_list and skill_view. - It says why a skill is not shown: sandboxed (quarantined included), untrusted (a signature that does not verify here), or declared for other agents. prompt_catalog applies it unchanged, and its suite passes as before. skills_list {query?, offset?, limit?}: - It returns name, a one-line description and the command names for every skill the gate passes for the calling agent (agents/core/skills/tools.py:265). - A row whose text is injection-flagged is left out, as the catalog leaves it out. The query matches name or description, case-insensitively. Pages hold at most 50 rows, with total and next_offset. - Unknown fields and malformed paging are refused by name. skill_view {name, file?} (agents/core/skills/tools.py:302): - It returns the SKILL.md body (frontmatter removed; the heading dialect whole) with its template variables rendered (H340), plus the list of the skill's other files, at most 3 KiB of names as the answer carries them (escapes counted) with files_more counting the rest; a file or body is served up to 64 KiB on disk, as always, and refused only when its escapes pass 128 KiB (MAX_ENCODED_BYTES), and the description is cut to 2 KiB escaped (agents/core/skills/tools.py:87). With file, it returns one of those files, listed or not. - It serves the bytes the trust checks ran on at load, kept as Skill.view_files (agents/core/skills/loader.py:334): each file up to 64 KiB as bytes, at most 1 MiB of a skill, any other file only as its size (listed, refused by name). An edit on disk after the signature check is not what the model reads, and a skill's assets are not held for the process's life. Signing a skill loads it again, so the view follows the signed bytes. - A path can only name a file of that snapshot. It must be relative, with no '..', leading '/' or backslash, and it is normalised (agents/core/skills/tools.py:182). The snapshot refuses links when it is taken. - Only text is served (a NUL or bad UTF-8 is refused, a BOM dropped), at most 64 KiB (MAX_FILE_BYTES, counted in bytes) after the template variables render and 128 KiB once escaped. The tool declares the escaped ceiling, the file list's and the description's bounds and 4 KiB of envelope as its own result budget. - An unknown skill and one this agent is not shown answer alike (skill_unknown). - The answer is tainted, and carries a warning sentence saying why next to the content, in two cases (agents/core/skills/tools.py:248): - the body or the description is injection-flagged; - the skill comes from outside the product and the owner has not vouched for it. - Skill.owner_vouched (agents/core/skills/loader.py:773) is true for a bundled skill, or for an outside one with a keyed signature or the owner's approval of these exact bytes. That is the same rule that decides whether its code may load in-process. - An unkeyed SKILL.sig is a sha256 anyone can compute. It verifies as integrity-only, but it is no vouch, so a self-signed import stays data (review-H318 M-1, SEC-B2). - The loop fences a tainted answer as DATA and raises the turn's taint. skill_propose (agents/core/skills/tools.py:389): - {name, content} proposes a new SKILL.md for an existing skill that this agent is shown. It becomes a pending SkillProposalStore proposal with origin agent:\<agent\>, plus one skill.patch_proposal approval card bound to it in the ledger (queue_card, agents/core/skills/proposals.py:213). The background review and /refine queue theirs the same way. - A bundled skill is refused (skill_propose_bundled): it is product source, and an edit would unbundle it and, with its code, stop it loading. A change that would rename the skill is refused (skill_propose_rename). - What the owner reviews comes from the ledger, never from the card. GET /api/skills/proposals (admin, agents/core/routers/skills.py:731) serves the pending proposals, a page of 20 with more counting the rest, each with its whole diff against the live SKILL.md, whether the skill drifted since, and flags for a rename or a bundled skill (describe, agents/core/skills/proposals.py:272); a proposal from before cards were bound, or whose card is gone from the queue, gets its card there, every pending proposal's, not only the page's, and the answer names every pending proposal's card (cards). The /v1 console's panel holds every skill-change card it has no diff for (one beyond the page, or one no proposal names) with Approve disabled, offering Reject (review-H318e n-4), re-reads the action list once the proposals answer, and says the change is loading, or that it could not be read, and holds Approve until it knows; a change the hub will refuse (a bundled skill, a rename) offers reject only in both consoles. The HUD's Decision Inbox shows these under SKILL CHANGES, whole, with approve and reject on the proposal's own card (frontend/src/panels/skill-changes.tsx), and the /v1 console's action panel shows the same diff under a skill-change card. - Only the proposal's own card decides it (agents/core/skills/curator.py:205): a card anyone with a user token queued naming a proposal id changes nothing, and the decide route says so. - Re-sending the same text, from any origin, makes no second proposal and no second card; a card that failed to queue is queued by the retry. A newer proposal from the same origin (the caller's own, even when the text re-sent was another origin's) for the same skill supersedes its older pending one (marked superseded, its card withdrawn), whether the tool, the background review or /refine proposed it (each passes its own origin, review-H318e n-2); binding a card withdraws the unbound cards naming the proposal, and a proposal superseded while its card was queued gets that card withdrawn, and a process takes at most 10 proposals a day, new skills included (skill_propose_limit). - The owner's decision applies it at once: POST /api/actions/{id}/decide on the proposal's card runs SkillCurator.apply_decisions (agents/core/routers/actions.py:95, agents/core/skills/curator.py:142), one pass at a time, the nightly pass included, not gated by the learning loop's flag or the night window. The apply is hash-checked against drift and backed up under the skill's directory name with a unique suffix. - The applied bytes keep the skill's standing (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1261, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1292): judged on one snapshot of the tree read before the write; after the write, the tree must be that snapshot with only SKILL.md replaced by the approved text, and the SKILL.sig that verified or the owner's approval is renewed over exactly those checked bytes, never a later read. SKILL.md is read and written as bytes, never in text mode, so the line ends on disk are the ones checked, on Windows too, and written whole: staged beside the skill's folder where it can be (a file a crash leaves inside the folder counts as a member and fails the signature closed; where beside it cannot be, another filesystem or a root the hub cannot write, the folder itself is used: review-H318g n-1), created with the old file's mode, flushed to disk and renamed over it, so a write cut short leaves the old text (review-H318e n-3, review-H318f m-1). The old SKILL.sig is read with SKILL.md, before anything is written (review-H318e m-1); a read of it that fails changes nothing and is tried again at the next decision (unreadable_signature, review-H318f n-1), and so does a standing that could not be read for a moment, which no longer reads as unsigned (review-H318g m-1); a signature that stays unreadable is warned about once per proposal. The apply is not crash-proof: a crash between the rename and the renewal leaves the skill sandboxed (untrusted, never trusted) and the next decision reports it as drifted, and on Windows a SKILL.md another process holds open cannot be replaced, so the change is retried as write_failed. Otherwise, or if the renewal fails, the old SKILL.md and the old SKILL.sig are put back byte for byte, where the old signature and approval still hold, the outcome says changed_during_apply or standing_not_renewed (rollback_failed, with the backup, if the old text could not be put back; signature_not_restored if the old text is back but its SKILL.sig could not be, which leaves the skill untrusted until it is signed again; when the old text could not be put back, its signature is not either, so what was renewed is not undone over the text that stays), and the proposal is marked stale with that reason rather than retried every pass. A standing that cannot be read (a SKILL.sig that is not UTF-8) reads as unsigned, and one proposal that fails never stops the pass. A keyed SKILL.sig whose key is not configured is not overwritten: the change waits (signing_key_missing). A shipped skill is known by its load or by where it lives (a shipped name directly under the product's skills tree), so one whose bytes drifted is still refused, by the tool, the background review and the apply. A skill nothing vouched for gains no vouch, a signature that no longer verifies is not renewed, and code changed on disk since the approval does not ride the patch. The answer reports each outcome with the state the skill is left in (shown, sandboxed, untrusted, hidden) or why it was not applied (bundled_skill, renames_skill, drifted_since_proposal, write_failed). A write that failed stays approved and is retried at the next decision, leaving no backup behind for the attempt. Publishing builds the package from one snapshot of the source, the bytes it read, and its registry row describes that snapshot's SKILL.md (review-H318e n-5): a skill whose folder is a link, a member that is a link or not a regular file, or a file that changed while read is refused (422, skill_source_refused: a link, a special file, or a file that could not be read stably), and a link planted after the check is never followed. - {description, steps} asks for a new skill, which SkillLoader.generate_skill writes into CDX-8 quarantine (PENDING_REVIEW, never executed until the owner approves). - Nothing writes a live SKILL.md: the live file is byte-identical after a proposal. - Only an owner's turn that has read nothing untrusted may propose. A guest, a household member, a job, an inbound channel or a turn that read a web page (the action origin a read raises) is refused by name, and so is a turn whose origin cannot be read. Injection-flagged text is refused, as are mixed or malformed arguments. Differences from Hermes, by decision: - Authoring is a proposal, never a write. Deleting a skill and adding supporting files stay owner actions. - skill_propose is offered wherever an ungated tool is, and refuses every turn that is not the owner's clean one; it is not withheld from the offer (a nit both reviews left). - The daily limit is per process and follows local time: a restart resets it. - Publishing a skill to the marketplace signs a staged copy, never the owner's tree, so sharing a skill is not vouching for it (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:391). - All three tools are ungated, so an inbound guest is offered them only when the owner names them in llm.guest_tools (critic note 22). tests/_snapshots/tool_profiles.json is regenerated. Tests: tests/test_h318_skill_tools.py (43 cases), tests/test_h318c_skill_review.py (22, the second review), tests/test_h318d_skill_review.py (22, the third review), tests/test_h318e_skill_review.py (21, the fourth review), tests/test_h318f_skill_review.py (10, the fifth review), tests/test_h318g_skill_review.py (5, the sixth review), tests/test_h318h_skill_review.py (7, the seventh review), vitest files for the HUD's JSON setting field (3) and the Decision Inbox's skill changes (frontend/src/test/skill-changes.test.tsx, 12), and the /v1 action panel's skill-change cards, a refused change's included (tests/frontend/tools.test.js), with the live orchestrator's pinned tool list in tests/test_agent_runtime_v2.py. They cover: - the list against the catalog, query, paging and refusals, and hidden skills; - view of a bundled body, unknown and hidden skills, linked files and every path refusal, binary and oversized files, and load-time bytes; - taint and warning for an unsigned outsider and for injected text; - the template variables; - patch and new proposals, the posture and turn refusals, and the malformed cases; - the review's cases: a self-signed outsider stays tainted; a keyed signature and a bundled skill are vouched; signing reloads the view; the rendered bound; large assets kept as sizes and the per-skill total; NUL, BOM, a one-lined description and the declared budget; an injected description; the list's page end and command cap; a hidden skill cannot be proposed to; an unreadable origin is untrusted; supersede and the daily limit; the card retry; an approval through the real route applies the change; - registration, the inbound-guest default and the live hub answering all three; - the second review's cases: a generated-and-approved skill with code, a keyed signature, an integrity-only one, an unvouched outsider, a tampered signature and code changed on disk after the approval, each through the real apply; a bundled skill refused at propose and at apply; a rename; the route's whole diff, flags, drift and old-proposal card; a card that is not the proposal's own; supersede per origin and per skill; one card per proposal; a new skill counting toward the limit; the byte-counted render bound; a file of exactly 64 KiB; 2,000 files within the budget; the backup name; a keyed publish; two overlapping decisions. - the third review's cases: code changed during the apply, and after the check but before the renewal, for an approval and a signature; a failed renewal; a keyed signature with its key missing and back; the answer-byte bounds; a lost card, legacy cards, a proposal superseded mid-bind and the route's rebind; a linked publish; a bundled skill and a gone skill in describe; pending only; the background review's bundled and shipped skip and its supersede; the nightly lock; the route's page; a rename behind a blank line; a drifted shipped skill; the HUD's reject-only, count and outcome texts. Mutation: 41 mutants of the new guards (the template-variable ones shared with H340). 33 were caught at once, and of the six survivors: - four were caught after cases were added or corrected (one test was vacuous: two fixtures pointed at different skill roots); - one was malformed and, rewritten, was caught; - one survived because its guard was redundant with the proposal store's own no-change check, and the guard was removed. Two mutants added afterwards were caught. The review's own 55 mutants left 15 survivors; its seven real ones (a proposal to a hidden skill, an origin error failing open, a NUL served, the next_offset edge, the description, the declared budget, a BOM kept) are now caught, and the rest change only a refusal reason or are equivalent. The first round's 25 mutants were all caught, two after a case was added. The second review's 44 mutants left six real survivors (the daily limit for new skills, supersede across origins and across skills, the diff cut, the render bound in characters, the 64 KiB boundary); each has a case now, and the diff is no longer cut. That round's 31 mutants (26 Python, 5 HUD) were all caught, one after the tampered-signature case was added. The third review's 60 mutants left four real survivors (a bundled flag in describe, a gone skill, the pending-only filter, the review's bundled skip); each has a case now. The third round's 28 mutants (25 Python, 3 HUD) were all caught, four after cases were added. The fourth review's 43 mutants left five real survivors (a SKILL.md swapped after the write, an integrity-only signature with a key configured, a re-bind touching other proposals' cards, the publish route's 422, a body's escapes); each has a case now. That round's 17 mutants were all caught. The fifth review's 35 mutants left four real survivors (a description cut to nothing, the escaped ceiling's boundary, two HUD reason texts) and an untested /v1 panel; each has a case now. That round's 16 mutants were all caught. The sixth review's 25 mutants left three real survivors (a signature restored over text that stayed, /v1's reject-only for a refused change, a weak one); the first two have cases now. That round's 7 mutants were all caught. The seventh review's 17 mutants left three real survivors (a removed flush, a removed cleanup, /v1's reject-only for a rename); the write no longer buffers, and the other two have cases. This round's 8 mutants were all caught, one after it was rebuilt (it had set the flag back itself). | ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |

| <a id="h326"></a>H326 | Model-facing skill discovery (prompt index + progressive disclosure) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Still missing from Hermes' inventory: - the prompt index is flat command rows, not a category-grouped name-and-description block; - the index has no LRU or manifest-validated disk snapshot, so it is rebuilt on each prompt; - there is no per-session dedup of repeated skill loads across turns: only the in-turn repeat stub exists. | <a id="h327"></a>H327 | SKILL.md frontmatter contract (agentskills.io-compatible) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The SKILL.md frontmatter contract Hermes parses (agent/skill_utils.parse_frontmatter and tools/skills_tool_plugin.py) now lives in one shared module, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py, used by both the loader and the importer. Parsing. split_frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:47): - drops a leading UTF-8 BOM before the fence check (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:56); - takes the closing fence at column 0 only, as Hermes' \`\\n---\\s\*\\n\` does, so an indented \`---\` inside a block scalar is content (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:63); - falls back to key: value lines for a block that is not valid YAML (_naive_key_values, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:83), instead of demoting the file to the heading dialect. The fallback is stricter than Hermes' line split: - only column-0 keys count, so a nested \`name:\` cannot replace the skill's own; - a value wrapped in matching quotes loses them; - a fallback that finds neither a name nor a description counts as no frontmatter (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:77), so a heading-dialect file framed by fences keeps its heading parse. Identity decisions pass lenient=False (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:72), so a name only the fallback can find never decides one: the Hermes pin gate (agents/core/skills/importer.py:270) and the migration and rescan slugs (agents/core/skills/importer.py:1189, agents/core/skills/importer.py:1373). SkillLoader._parse_manifest decodes with utf-8-sig on both the snapshot and the path read (agents/core/skills/loader.py:839, agents/core/skills/loader.py:841). A manifest the normaliser cannot read registers under the directory name instead of failing (agents/core/skills/loader.py:856). The manifest: - The name is capped at 64 characters and stripped after the cut (agents/core/skills/loader.py:875; the caps are at agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:28-29). - The description is capped at 1024 characters with an ellipsis, quotes kept (cap_description, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:351). When none is declared, it is the first non-heading body line, as Hermes' listing reads it (agents/core/skills/loader.py:877-878). - A list-valued author or license is joined (agents/core/skills/loader.py:880). - agents and requires read a YAML list, \`\[a, b\]\` or \`a, b\` as plain strings (agents/core/skills/loader.py:867-868). - Command entries are reduced to JSON types (agents/core/skills/loader.py:952). contract_fields (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:359), merged into every frontmatter manifest (agents/core/skills/loader.py:887), keeps the keys Hermes recognises: - homepage and compatibility; - platforms, read as Hermes matches them (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:204): a list, or one string as one name. Each name maps through PLATFORM_MAP and matches the OS whose sys.platform starts with it, so darwin and win count and osx and win64 do not. An unmatched name is kept, so a gate refuses it everywhere, as Hermes hides it; - environments and requires_apps, where a string is one item (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:224, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:234); - triggers and dependencies; - tags and related_skills, where a declared metadata.hermes value wins over the top-level key (skill_view's \`hermes_meta.get(k) or frontmatter.get(k)\`); - setup, prerequisites and required_credential_files; - required_environment_variables (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:256), which matches Hermes' _get_required_environment_variables: it merges the declared list, setup.collect_secrets and the legacy prerequisites.env_vars, first entry wins, names are validated, and \`optional\` is read for truth; - metadata.hermes.{tags, category, upstream_skill, supersedes, session_platforms, requires_toolsets, requires_tools, fallback_for_toolsets, fallback_for_tools, config} (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:340). A config entry needs a key and a description. Skill exposes platforms, environments, requires_apps, required_env (names only; no value is read) and hermes_meta, and to_dict carries them (agents/core/skills/loader.py:476, agents/core/skills/loader.py:524, agents/core/skills/loader.py:532, agents/core/skills/loader.py:536, agents/core/skills/loader.py:542). Imported frontmatter is third-party text: - json_safe (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:128) bounds depth, width, string length and total nodes, with a 2,048-node budget per value (agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:134). A 2 KB YAML alias bomb that would expand to 64^6 nodes parses in about 0.02 s. - Dates become ISO strings, and NaN, infinities and integers past 64 bits become null or "". A hex integer past Python's str() limit no longer raises (_scalar_text, agents/core/skills/frontmatter.py:112), so the security-posture route serialises Skill.to_dict with allow_nan=False. - discover() skips a skill whose load raises and keeps the rest, while the signing misconfiguration still stops discovery (agents/core/skills/loader.py:656-661). - A second skill taking an occupied registry name is logged: a warning within one tree (e.g. two names sharing their first 64 characters), info for a user skill replacing a bundled one (agents/core/skills/loader.py:668). Deliberate differences from Hermes: - A closing fence at end of file with no trailing newline is accepted, as Nerva always did. - The registry is last-writer-wins with that log line; Hermes' listing keeps the first. Tests: tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py has 51 cases, including the alias bomb (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:286), a hex integer in seven fields (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:306), NaN and huge numbers through to_dict (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:315), one broken skill among others (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:328), the fallback rules (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:354), Hermes platform matching (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:406), the strict identity parse (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:434) and the snapshot BOM path (tests/test_skill_frontmatter_contract.py:482). A mutation pass over 25 mutants (first-wins, precedence, the depth, width and node caps, both utf-8-sig decodes, the fence, the fallback rules, optional, the strict gate, NaN and big ints, the discover guard, the collision log, the platform string, author join, quotes, the body-line fallback, name strip, command sanitising, requires_apps) killed all of them. 454 tests across 28 skill, import, marketplace, migration and posture files pass. Gating on these fields is not this row: H328 (visibility) and the setup rows consume them. | <a id="h328"></a>H328 | Conditional skill visibility (OS platform, runtime environment, channel, tool availability) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A skill is shown where it makes sense, by the four Hermes gates split into hard and soft (agents/core/skills/visibility.py; HARD_GATES and SOFT_GATES, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:46-47), reading the fields H327 keeps. Hard: \`platforms\`. readiness (agents/core/skills/visibility.py:89) answers \`unsupported on \<host\> (it declares ...)\` for a skill that declares only other operating systems. The host is sys.platform mapped to macos, linux or windows (host_platforms, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:80), and on Termux it also counts as linux and android. A skill that declares none is ready everywhere. An unsupported skill is left out of every offer (catalog_gate answers \`unsupported\`, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1329). skill_view answers \`skill_unsupported\` with \`readiness_status: unsupported\` (agents/core/skills/tools.py:312). A command naming it is refused before it runs (Skill.execute, agents/core/skills/loader.py:548). Skill.to_dict and GET /skills carry \`readiness\` and \`readiness_reason\` (agents/core/routers/skills.py:59), and the Skill Switches panel marks it. Soft: the other three gates only hide a skill from what the model is offered, meaning the prompt catalog (read once per catalog, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1431) and skills_list (read once per list, agents/core/skills/tools.py:283). Named explicitly, it still works: skill_view reads it (agents/core/skills/tools.py:229) and its command runs, because an explicit request is consent (offer_gate, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:177). \`environments\` are shown only where one holds (host_environments, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:102). \`docker\` and \`container\` come from /.dockerenv or /run/.containerenv, and \`s6\` from /run/s6 or /command/s6-svscan. \`kanban\` holds for a turn with no human in it (the internal surface: a job, a workflow, the heartbeat). Any tag the owner lists in JARVIS_SKILL_ENVIRONMENTS also holds. \`metadata.hermes.session_platforms\` shows a skill only on the channels named. \`cli\` names the operator channels (the HUD and voice), and a turn bound to no channel is not gated. The unbound principal's \`unknown\` channel now counts as no channel for the H329 switches too. \`requires_tools\` / \`requires_toolsets\` hide a skill unless every named tool, or one tool of every named toolset, is offered this turn. \`fallback_for_tools\` / \`fallback_for_toolsets\` hide it while those are offered. Hermes toolset names map onto Nerva's tools (TOOLSETS, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:50); an unknown toolset is never offered. The turn's offer is read without side effects (AgentToolRuntime.offered_names, agents/core/agent_runtime.py:265; empty when the tool loop is off). The orchestrator binds it around the catalog (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3185; bind_offer, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:116), and inside the tool loop the loop's own offer is used (current_offer, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:125). When the offer cannot be read, the tool gates stand aside. Every hide is logged once per skill and gate (note_hidden, agents/core/skills/visibility.py:204). Nothing here enables anything: it only narrows what is shown. tests/test_h328_skill_visibility.py (33 cases) covers the host names, Termux, readiness, environment detection and the owner's tags, the unsupported skill left out, refused and answered by skill_view and skills_list, an environment skill hidden but still viewable and runnable, kanban per surface, session_platforms with the cli alias and no channel, fourteen tool-gate cases, the tool loop's own offer, malformed fields, the logging, the prompt-context binding (reset after, an unreadable offer), the runtime's offer, the /skills readiness and the unbound channel. The panel has a vitest case. There are 42 mutants, all killed. [d1](../agents/core/skills/visibility.py) [d2](../agents/core/skills/loader.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/tools.py) |
| <a id="h329"></a>H329 | Turning individual skills off without uninstalling them | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A skill can be switched off without being uninstalled, everywhere or on one channel, at once and reversibly. Two declared settings rows hold the switches (agents/core/settings_db.py:343-344): \`skills.disabled\`, the skills off everywhere (Hermes' \`skills.disabled\`), and \`skills.channel_disabled\`, a map from a channel (telegram, voice, web, ...) to the skills off there only (Hermes' \`skills.platform_disabled.\<platform\>\`), validated on write (channel_map_problem, agents/core/skills/switches.py:101; agents/core/settings_db.py:797). An entry is the skill's name, ignoring case. The route and the CLI also accept a folder, and store the name it resolves to, so one entry never matches one skill by name and a second by folder. Both rows are read at call time (state, agents/core/skills/switches.py:115; off_reason, agents/core/skills/switches.py:157), so a switch applies to the next turn without a restart. A switched-off skill stays installed, signed, approved and counted. The shared catalog gate answers \`disabled\` for it (SkillLoader.catalog_gate, agents/core/skills/loader.py:1329), which leaves it out of the model's skill catalog. prompt_catalog reads the switches once per catalog (agents/core/skills/loader.py:1427). The gate also leaves it out of \`skills_list\` and \`skill_view\`. A command that names it is refused with the reason ("\[skill:Weather Intel\] is switched off everywhere; …") before the usage counter or the command runs (Skill.execute, agents/core/skills/loader.py:548). A channel switch applies to turns on that channel, taken from the command context or the turn's bound principal (current_channel, agents/core/skills/switches.py:145). An unreadable store leaves every skill on. ESSENTIAL_SKILLS (agents/core/skills/switches.py:48), the security monitor, has no off switch, and a stored entry for it is ignored. POST /api/skills/switch (admin; switch_skill, agents/core/routers/skills.py:91) switches one skill (a manifest name wins over another skill's folder of the same spelling) or every skill of a declared \`metadata.hermes.category\`, on or off, everywhere or on one channel, in one settings write (apply, agents/core/skills/switches.py:187). Switching on everywhere clears every entry; switching on for one channel clears only that channel's. Every change is recorded in the intent log (\`skill.disable\` / \`skill.enable\`, actor owner, the skills, the channel and the category). A switch-off that cannot be recorded still applies and says \`audited: false\`. A switch-on widens what the hub does, so it is refused with 503 when the intent log cannot record it (agents/core/routers/skills.py:130), and one whose record fails is put back by compare-and-set (restore, agents/core/skills/switches.py:236; agents/core/routers/skills.py:153). Nerva has no kernel mediation for a settings write, so the governed step is an owner-only admin act with an intent-log record rather than a permission.grant task, and no tool the model is offered writes these rows. GET /skills gives each skill \`disabled\`, \`disabled_channels\`, \`essential\` and \`category\` (list_skills, agents/core/routers/skills.py:38). \`nerva skills list \| off \| on \[--channel\] \[--category\]\` drives the route (_skill_switches, agents/cli/nerva.py:1247). The Console's Skill Switches panel (frontend/src/panels/skill-switches.tsx) shows per-skill and per-category toggles with a channel field, marks essential skills "always on", and shows refusals and unrecorded switches. This is distinct from the H285 load set, which keeps a skill from loading at all at boot. tests/test_h329_skill_switches.py (39 cases) covers the rows and their validation, name and channel cleaning, off everywhere by name only (a folder entry does not match), off on one channel, the turn's channel and an explicit one, the essential floor, an unreadable store, the refused command with no usage counted, the catalog read once and hidden per channel, skills_list and skill_view, apply off, on, on-one-channel and unchanged, no empty channel kept, the essential skill in a category, a refused or half write, restore and a lost race, and the route: records, one channel, a category, every refusal, admin-only, a switch-on without a usable intent log, an unrecorded switch-on put back, a 500 on a refused write, the listing fields, a folder-versus-name collision, and a disable/enable cycle that leaves the skill's files and manifest unchanged. It also covers the CLI. The panel has 4 vitest cases. There are 58 mutants, all killed. [d1](../agents/core/skills/switches.py) [d2](../agents/core/skills/loader.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/skills.py) |

| <a id="h340"></a>H340 | Skill body preprocessing: template variables | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 together with H318 (critic note 21: one skill_view that returns this row's rendered body under H318's gates), and fixed with H318's review round. The renderer: - agents/core/skills/template_vars.py render_skill_body (agents/core/skills/template_vars.py:68) replaces, in one pass, the fixed set: ${NERVA_SKILL_DIR} and ${HERMES_SKILL_DIR} (the skill's absolute directory), and ${NERVA_SESSION_ID} and ${HERMES_SESSION_ID} (the turn's session id). The Hermes spellings are the ones imported skills use. - It also replaces the owner's skills.template_vars, a JSON setting seeded empty (agents/core/settings_db.py:341). - Unknown ${...} tokens stay literal. A value is never expanded again, and the process environment is never read. What the owner may set: - clean_template_vars (agents/core/skills/template_vars.py:49) keeps only plain literal entries, at most 32. - A key is an identifier of at most 64 characters and cannot shadow a fixed name. - A value is one printable line of at most 256 characters holding no '$' and not starting env: or secret:. - A bad entry is dropped, logged, and its token stays literal. Where it applies: - The substitution happens where the body reaches the model: skill_view renders the SKILL.md body with the calling turn's session id and the setting as read at call time (agents/core/skills/tools.py:361). - Linked files are served as written. - The 64 KiB bound is checked after rendering, so neither an owner variable nor an outside author repeating ${NERVA_SKILL_DIR} can grow a body past it (review-H318 m-1). - The setting is checked on write: PUT /api/admin/settings/skills refuses a skills.template_vars that clean_template_vars would change, with one sentence naming the rules (agents/core/settings_db.py:819). The HUD's settings panel edits a JSON setting as JSON and saves it parsed (it used to save a string the hub ignored). Tests in tests/test_h318_skill_tools.py: - ${HERMES_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/x.sh renders to the absolute skill dir, and the session variables render to the active session id; - the owner's variables render; - a value naming an environment variable ($HOME), a pointer, a too-long value, a bad key or a fixed-name override is refused and stays literal; - ${UNKNOWN} stays literal; - a value holding ${...} is not expanded; - an environment variable named in a body is not read; - the rendered bound, and the write-time refusal (a string, an env pointer, a fixed name, a too-long value, a list). Mutation: nine mutants of the cleaner and renderer were all killed. One did not apply (a fixed-name override), and it is covered by the key check. | <a id="h344"></a>H344 | Reading skills from directories shared with other AI tools | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Import-once stays the only way a Hermes / OpenClaw / Claude Code skill enters Nerva's skills tree (no live-scanned external_dirs tier; SkillLoader.discover() is unchanged), and a re-run of \`python scripts/nerva_import.py\` now notices upstream movement instead of reporting a blanket skipped/exists. Each imported skill is re-detected against the absolute source_path and sha256 recorded in its manifest.json (importer.py:952, importer.py:997): unchanged; changed, with the old and new digests plus a bounded line diff shown by --json; moved to another folder of the same install and followed (importer.py:931); removed; renamed; unreadable; or source_untrusted when a record names a file outside the install, which is then never read (importer.py:1287). The default run is a dry run. \`--apply --update\` re-imports only changed skills that this source imported (importer.py:1062): the current copy is backed up under data/imports/skill_backups, PENDING_REVIEW is written before the new bytes land, SKILL.sig and the legacy approval marker are cleared, and the owner's approval is revoked. A linked control file, an unusable backup dir or a refused save fails closed, and the write is bound to the probed digest. Every import and re-import crosses skill.install through the real Action Kernel at the shipped CLI (nerva_import.py:577, bound at nerva_import.py:1032 and as the ImportRunner default at nerva_import.py:682), behind SKILL_IMPORT_CONTRACT (nerva_import.py:145). The persisted kill switch is re-read on every decision, so an engaged halt refuses an import or re-import before a byte moves. The external origin keeps a policy GRANT at QUEUE, and only GRANT or QUEUE writes (nerva_import.py:764), so every write lands quarantined. The human summary names a local edit that --update replaces (with its backup path), a failed revoke, and moved, untrusted and refused rows (nerva_import.py:938). Tests in test_nerva_import.py: 44 passed and 1 live-gated skip under python 3.11 and 3.12. They include test_cli_skill_writes_cross_the_real_kernel_and_honour_the_kill_switch, test_runner_default_authorizer_is_the_kernel_for_skills_and_queue_for_memory, test_source_moved_within_the_install_is_followed_not_reported_removed, test_apply_update_reimports_changed_skill_into_quarantine_and_revokes_approval, test_rescan_never_follows_a_record_outside_the_install and test_reimport_refuses_a_linked_control_file_and_never_writes_through_it. Two known limits sit outside the accepted requirement. (1) The CLI binds no durable audit sink, so each kernel decision is recorded only on its report row (kernel/kernel_reason) and in the in-process kernel metrics. (2) A record written before this change with a cwd-relative source_path (relative --home) is not rewritten by an unchanged re-run. From another directory it reads skipped/exists plus source_untrusted until a changed source is re-imported with --apply --update, which records the absolute path. [d1](../agents/core/skills/importer.py) [d2](../scripts/nerva_import.py) [d3](../tests/test_nerva_import.py) |
