| <a id="h465"></a>H465 | Run the self-improvement review on demand | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 and fixed after six adversarial reviews: review-H465 (one major, seven minors, seven nits), review-H465b (three minors, six nits: a per-turn pass could still run beside /refine and borrow its label, a cut-off review read as nothing, and four mutants were unpinned) and review-H465c (three minors, six nits: a per-turn review cut off by the token cap was refunded, so a model that always truncates reviewed every turn; the settings the replies named could not be set; four survivors) and review-H465d (two minors, seven nits: a day of cut-offs made /refine name the wrong setting, seven real survivors, and a GA4 private key the CLI printed unmasked) and review-H465e (two minors, seven nits: the cut-off refusal on a day of mostly real reviews, nine real survivors, a budget read outside its bounds, a secret echoed by nerva config set, a pass in flight at midnight counted on the next day, a burst of turns past the cadence) and review-H465f (seven nits: a /refine refund keyed on the wrong day, a burst given too few passes, a later bracket closing an unclosed error, a fraction below one read as reviews off). All are fixed. Hermes' /refine \[focus\] forks the background memory and skill review against a snapshot of the conversation, so the live session is untouched. It refuses while a turn is in flight and reports the new or updated memories and skills back into the chat. Nerva's review engine is the per-turn BackgroundReviewer (strict-local, budgeted); it now also runs on demand. The command: - /refine \[focus\] is an owner command (agents/core/commands.py:347, registered at agents/core/commands.py:405). It answers from any channel's owner, and a guest is refused like any owner command. - The focus is one line of at most 200 characters. - It replies with what the review kept, one line per action (profile or core memory updated, corrections recorded, skill patch proposed, new skill quarantined, facts found but not kept because living memory is off). A skill change is said to wait in the Decision Inbox, and a new skill in the pending skills list. - Every non-run is named: a review already running, the daily budget spent, no local model answering (reviews never leave the machine), a model that spent its whole answer thinking (review_cut_off), a review cut off or malformed (review_unparsed, which names learning.review_max_tokens), reviews switched off (a daily budget of 0, reviews_off), the review outlasting its bound, nothing to review, no reviewer. An empty reply, a cut one, or a JSON object carrying none of the review's keys ({} or {"error": …}) is unparsed; only an object that says it found nothing reads as nothing worth keeping. A reply that parses as a review is the model's answer, whatever it opens with or mentions; only a reply that does not parse and is the local degraded message or opens with a bracketed \[… error …\] closed on its own line by its own bracket is a backend failure; an unclosed "\[Note: … error", or one a later \[1\] or link would close, is prose (review-H465e nit 7, review-H465f nit 3). - A local backend that is configured but down answers with a degraded string rather than raising. That is no review: it is reported as no local model and costs no budget (review-H465 M-1). A cut-off or malformed /refine costs none either; a per-turn pass cut off the same way does spend its unit, since the model ran to its cap, so the daily budget still bounds local GPU use when a model always truncates, and it is logged at INFO once a day, naming learning.review_max_tokens as the cause of a thinking cut-off and as a possible one of a malformed answer; a /refine cut-off does not use that line up. When such cut-offs are at least half of the day's spent units, /refine answers daily_budget_cut_off and says that, if they were cut off, the token cap is what to raise rather than the budget; one cut-off among real reviews is plain daily_budget (review-H465e m-1). A pass still waiting on the model at midnight refunds or counts against the day it spent its unit on, never the next (review-H465e nit 4), and GET /api/cognition/learning counts cut_offs_today. The unit is spent in run() after the budget is checked again there, so a burst of passes that all saw budget left in should_run cannot pass it; the cadence is checked there again too, so a burst of turns cannot pass it either (review-H465e nit 6), and a pass uses up only the turns that admitted it, so a burst gets the passes the same turns one at a time would (review-H465f nit 2) (_cut_off, agents/core/learning/background_review.py:513). - It runs whether or not the per-turn learning loop (cognition.review_enabled) is on: the owner asked, as Hermes' manual /refine does. Orchestrator.refine (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3270): - It reads a snapshot of the session's history and never writes to it. - The snapshot is conversation_snapshot (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:98): the newest turns whole and contiguous, each cut at 2,000 characters, up to 12,000 characters in all. Slash commands and the hub's replies to them are left out, so a chat of commands only has nothing to review. This replaces the per-turn review's last-6-turns, 300-character window for this path. - In flight, as it really behaves: every path that dispatches /refine holds the session's turn lease first. A /refine sent while another turn runs therefore waits for that turn (up to the lease's 180-second bound) and then reviews the conversation as it stands; it is not refused (review-H465 m-1 corrected the earlier claim). The review itself is bounded at 150 seconds (REFINE_TIMEOUT_S), below that wait, so the chat is busy for at most that long. - A direct caller holding no lease while another turn does is refused turn_in_flight: the lock is locked and the key is not among this context's held leases (agents/core/orchestrator.py:3290). No new in-flight set was added (critic note 1). - A review's result is the reviewer's last_result, which GET /api/cognition/learning shows (reviewer.status()); a refusal does not overwrite it. Each action is logged. The learning loop's knobs are declared settings (category learning, agents/core/settings_db.py:281): review_max_tokens (512, 1–32768), review_daily_budget (20, 0–1000), review_cadence (every_turn, every_n_turns, idle_gap), review_every_n (3), review_idle_gap_s (90) and review_max_facts (3), with the defaults the code already used, bounded in _LEARNING_INT_BOUNDS (agents/core/settings_db.py:710). The admin settings API, the HUD and nerva config set can change them; nerva config get shows a number named …max_tokens as the number it is, not masked as a credential, and masks every key the settings store encrypts (the GA4 service-account JSON among them), and nerva config set echoes such a value masked too (review-H465e nit 3). A model id is masked only under a hinted name: llm.default_model shows as typed. Every knob reads within its declared bounds whatever the stored row (a row from before the bounds, restored or hand-edited, reads as the default), the review's model call's token cap included (bounded_learning_int, agents/core/settings_db.py:716). A declared 0 for review_max_facts or review_idle_gap_s is honoured, not read as unset; corrections have a cap of their own (3), so a review_max_facts of 0 keeps no facts, still records corrections, and /refine says what it found but did not keep (_number, agents/core/learning/background_review.py:293). BackgroundReviewer.run_on_demand (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:335): - It skips the cadence gate but spends the daily budget, rolling over at midnight, and refunds it when no review was had (a model down, a cut-off answer, a timeout, a cancellation), on the day the pass spent it (review-H465f nit 1). A budget of 0 means no reviews and /refine says so (reviews_off); 2.5 reads as 2, and a value that is not a number or lies outside 0–1000 (text, infinity, a boolean, a hand-edited -3, a fraction below one) is logged once and 20 used (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:268). A per-turn pass that does not run leaves a DEBUG line naming why. It keeps the strict-local model, and is bounded at REFINE_TIMEOUT_S (150 s, below the lease's 180-second wait); no setting raises it. - It neither resets nor delays the per-turn cadence. It runs one at a time and never beside a per-turn review: a /refine while a per-turn pass runs is refused busy, and a per-turn pass that would start while /refine runs is skipped, not deferred. The exclusion is taken in run() itself (agents/core/learning/background_review.py:394), so a per-turn pass spawned before the /refine, still waiting on the memory lock, is refused on_demand when it enters. A cancelled review leaves no busy flag. - Its proposals, approval cards and new skills are labelled refine, not background_review; the label is passed down per pass, never kept on the instance. An injection-flagged correction is not recorded (on both paths). Facts found while living memory is off are counted after the injection scan, and said only to the owner who asked. - run gains a focus slot in REVIEW_PROMPT ("Focus for this review: …", absent when there is none, so the per-turn prompt is byte-identical) and a context bound, which this path sets to the snapshot's. Tests: tests/test_h465_refine.py (35 cases), tests/test_h465c_refine_review.py (9, the second review), tests/test_h465d_refine_review.py (31, the third review), tests/test_h465e_refine_review.py (37, the fourth review), tests/test_h465f_refine_review.py (34, the fifth review), tests/test_h465g_refine_review.py (18, the sixth review), tests/test_nerva_cli.py (a token budget shown unmasked), and tests/test_slash_commands.py pins the command in the owner's list. They cover: - the focus slot and its bound; - cadence skipped while the budget is spent, and the day's rollover; - one review at a time, and facts written; - the snapshot's bound, order, cutting and junk; - a whole snapshot reaching the prompt; - the reviewer receiving this session's snapshot while the session stays unchanged; - busy while another turn holds the lease, and not busy inside the command's own lease; - the empty and unavailable cases; - the owner-only command and its report, each named refusal, and /help; - the review's cases: a degraded reply is no review and costs no budget; the timeout; a cancelled review; mutual exclusion and the cadence kept; facts found with living memory off; an injected correction; the refine label; commands left out and contiguity; a chat of commands only; a refusal keeping the last result; the focus cut and where a new skill waits; a guest told /refine is the owner's. Mutation: 20 mutants; 18 were caught at once, and the other two after cases were added (the snapshot's bound reaching the prompt, the day rollover). The review's 28 mutants left four real survivors (a cancelled review's flag, the snapshot's contiguity, a refusal overwriting the last result, the focus cut); all are now caught. The first round's 19 mutants were all caught. The second review's 29 mutants left four real survivors (a new skill's label, a setting raising the bound, a garbage bound setting, a timeout's stored result): the bound setting is gone and the other two have cases. The third round's 14 mutants were all caught. The third review's 36 mutants (29 killed) left four real survivors (an empty reply, a reply cut after an inner }, a thinking cut-off's stored result, a cancelled /refine staying cancelled); all four have cases. That round's 22 mutants: 21 caught, one equivalent (an explicit unset check the except branch already covers, since removed). The fourth review's 40 mutants left seven real survivors (the documented values accepted, a search for an error anywhere, a /refine cut-off using up the day's line, a skip reason other than on_demand); each has a case, the day's line after a /refine only since the fifth round. That round's 19 mutants were all caught, two after their cases were added. The fifth review's 51 mutants left nine real survivors (the cut-off refusal's threshold, which pass logs the day's line, the orchestrator's bounded token cap, the rollover of cut_offs_today in both entry points, a /refine cut-off counted among them, the corrections cap, the found-not-kept count against injected and agent facts); each has a case. That round's 20 mutants were all caught. The sixth review's 40 mutants left two real survivors (a regex closed by a later bracket or line), each with a case now. This round's 6 mutants: four caught, two equivalent (the refund's day check and its reset, since nothing rolls the day while a /refine runs, and a /refine refunds at most once). In wake mode the agent should be woken in that chat to read the full context and answer in its own voice, spending interrupt budget and respecting is_night_window. - No per-subscription last_event_id cursor advanced transactionally, so a restarted notifier would have no way to avoid re-notifying. - No auto-subscribe of the creating chat. - No 30-day pruning of finished subscriptions. - No entry point: no command, route or HUD control. | <a id="h477"></a>H477 | Point an external client at Nerva (OpenAI-compatible chat, and capability discovery) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Discovery half (the row's own scope), still owed: (1) a \`/v1/toolsets\` equivalent — \`GET /api/capabilities\` and \`GET /api/toolrpc/tools\` enumerate individual tools only; the toolset groups in \`agents/core/job_toolsets.py\` CATALOG (basic/files/terminal, with \`available\`) are reachable only inside admin-guarded \`GET /api/jobs/doctor\` (\`agents/core/autonomy/jobs_health.py:116\`) and are jobs-scoped, not the agent's platform toolsets with \`enabled\`/\`configured\`/expanded \`tools\`; (2) the API-surface half of \`/v1/capabilities\` — \`snapshot()\` (\`agents/core/observability/capability_registry.py:503\`) returns no \`features\`/\`endpoints\`/\`auth\`/\`session-header\`/idempotency/protocol-version block, so an external UI cannot feature-detect the server; \`/openapi.json\` partly covers the endpoint list but is not declared a stable client contract anywhere in docs/. Rolled-up children with no Nerva surface at all: \`.api-browser-control\` — no \`POST /v1/browser-control/register\`, no single-use TTL ticket, no controller websocket, no server-side controller capability allowlist (\`rg 'browser_control\|controller_id\|extension_control'\` over agents/ returns nothing; \`agents/core/browser_transport.py\` is SSRF resolver pinning for in-process Chromium); \`.api-platform-events\` — no normalization of platform-native reactions/edits (\`rg 'reaction\|message_edited\|platform_event'\` over agents/ returns no code, only food-diary prose in agents/frigga/\*.md; \`POST /api/channels/{channel_id}/inbound\` is message ingress). Partially satisfied children: \`.api-model-routing\` — \`ChatRequest\` (\`agents/web.py:1056\`) is \`extra="forbid"\` and accepts only \`agent\`/\`reasoning\`/\`session_id\`, so per-request \`model\`/\`provider\` and \`model_routes\` alias precedence (including the 400-on-provider-conflict rule) do not exist; only the \`reasoning\` override is governed and live (\`agents/web.py:1252\`). \`.api-models\` — no \`/api/model/options\` provider-aware picker payload with refresh/cache-bust semantics; \`/api/models/local\` and \`/api/models/info\` (\`agents/core/routers/models_llm.py\`) are both behind \`admin_guard\`. \`.api-artifacts\` — \`agents/core/routers/artifact_store.py\` is bounded, quota'd and guarded, but performs no server-side SHA-256 digest validation and binds no artifact to the scope that created it. Out of scope by the row's own rationale, not gaps: \`POST /v1/chat/completions\` (explicitly refused) and \`POST /v1/responses\` (same chat-façade family; any façade must re-enter the governed \`POST /chat\` path at \`agents/web.py:1214\`). Namespace note for the build: \`GET /v1\` is already the legacy HUD HTML (\`agents/web.py:1176\`), so a \`/v1/\*\` API prefix has to be reconciled with it or the discovery surface stays on \`/api/\*\`. | <a id="h478"></a>H478 | Bot-to-bot messaging between machines | keep | Parțial | Reevaluat | Nothing sends outbound. Peer records store no endpoint, and no code signs a task and POSTs it to another machine's /api/a2a/task, so this box has no \`peer dm\` equivalent. An approved inbound task has no execution path: decide only changes the status, no turn runs and no reply reaches the sender. The accepted async handle is missing: there is no start/status/stop for an approved remote turn and no peer-authenticated status/result poll. Without them the sender cannot collect a result without holding a connection open. The row depends on H476 (Runs API), which is itself partial. Any execution must stay behind owner approval and node.dispatch/kernel mediation, and no peer-side grant may skip the pending inbox. | <a id="h479"></a>H479 | Named inbound webhook routes that wake the agent — or deliver without one | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h480"></a>H480 | Send a message to any configured channel from a script, with no model call | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Atașamentele \`MEDIA:\<path\>\` nu sunt purtate: o rută care citește căi locale și le trimite pe un canal e egress de fișiere și trebuie să ajungă în spatele Action Kernel-ului, cu un director-sursă delimitat, înainte să existe. Cerința structurală „fără gateway pornit” rămâne o divergență documentată: \`nerva send\` trece prin hub ca să existe IntentLog și rate-limit. Corpurile peste 4.000 de caractere sunt refuzate, nu fragmentate (Hermes fragmentează per platformă). Slack și Discord rămân reply-only (restul H018); email-ul e exclus deliberat. Nu există adresare \`platform:chat_id\[:thread\]\` / \`platform:#canal\` ca la Hermes: \`--channel telegram\` înseamnă întotdeauna chat-ul owner-ului. Precedența diverge deliberat: la Hermes argumentul câștigă în fața \`--file\`, aici ambele deodată e eroare de utilizare. Erorile nu au prefixul \`hermes send:\`. \`JobRunner._send\` își păstrează propria rezolvare a destinației (lot-ul jobs). Fără probă live pe un canal real din această sesiune: verificarea a fost prin hub fals și prin ChannelManager-ul real cu adaptoare false. [d1](../agents/core/channels/outbound.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/integrations.py) [d3](../agents/cli/nerva.py) |

## security

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h481"></a>H481 | Refuse a catastrophic shell command no matter who approved it (hardline floor + risky-pattern tier + de-obfuscation) | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The middle tier does not exist.\*\* The row asks for three stacked layers; this is the first, with its matching repaired (and repaired again in PR #1177). There is still no ~40-rule severity/reversibility-tagged risky-pattern catalogue feeding the approval card's description, so \`rm -rf ~/projects\` is neither hardline nor pattern-classified — re-checked 2026-09-19 on 494a13b2: \`hardline_match("rm -rf ~/projects")\` and \`hardline_match(\['rm','-rf','/home/x/projects'\])\` both return None, and the module carries no severity/reversibility/catalogue vocabulary at all — and the owner's card still says "run this argv" rather than "recursive delete", which the row's rationale names as the point. No user-editable \`approvals.deny\` fnmatch list at hardline level (no \`fnmatch\`/\`deny\` in the module). No parser limits that themselves hardline-block and save an oversized payload to a file for manual review (128k chars / 4096 separator-free / 25k segments); \`MAX_ARG_CHARS = 4000\` is argv validation, not a refusal. \*\*Variants Hermes produces and this still does not:\*\* NFKC normalisation, \`$HOME\`/\`$HERMES_HOME\` folding, grep-pattern span masking, and \`python -c\` / \`perl -e\` / \`node -e\` payload extraction — the last left alone on purpose, since \`jobs_scripts.snapshot_script\` legitimately builds \`\[sys.executable, '-I', '-c', source\]\`. \`$()\`/backtick substitution is now extracted in ONE place only: #1177's \`_substituted\` narrows a heredoc BODY line to its substitutions, so \`cat \<\<EOF\` / \`$(rm -rf /)\` / \`EOF\` refuses; an ordinary line is still not expanded, so \`echo $(rm -rf /)\` and a backtick \`rm -rf /\` inside an assignment return None (a \`$(mkfs.ext4 …)\` is caught only through \`_CMD\`'s \`(\` anchor). The row's own examples \`$(printf mkfs).ext4 /dev/sda\` and \`echo bWtmcw==\|base64 -d\|sh\` still return None. \*\*Gaps this module knows it still has, listed honestly rather than as a complete set:\*\* nesting deeper than \`_MAX_SHELL_DEPTH\` (depth-3 \`sh -c "sh -c 'sh -c …'"\` returns None, pinned by test); a quote at a word BOUNDARY (\`'mkfs.ext4' /dev/sda\`) rather than inside a word; \`powershell -EncodedCommand\` base64; a \`cmd /c\` payload split across several argv items; an obfuscated shell HEAD in argv form (\`\['s""h','-c',…\]\`), because the de-obfuscation pass runs after payload extraction and its output is not fed back through it; and, since #1177, two in the false-refusal direction — a \`case\` label with a leading \`(\` (\`(shutdown) echo bye ;;\`) is still read as a command, and a \`case\` nested inside another is not modelled: its inner first label IS stripped (\`case $1 in\\na) case $2 in\\nreboot) echo r ;;\\nesac ;;\\nesac\` went power_cycle→None), so the docstring's 'not handled' is about the region boundary, not a guaranteed refusal. \*\*Refusals #1177 cleared in the dangerous direction — closed by this slice (H481, 2026-09-22).\*\* The \`case\`-label strip fires only inside a real command-position \`case WORD in … esac\` region now, so \`(echo case in; reboot); echo esac\` and the \`$IFS\`-spliced \`(echo case in; mkfs.ext4$IFS/dev/sda); echo esac\` / \`wipefs$IFS-a$IFS/dev/sda\` refuse again; and \`_heredoc_feeds_a_shell\` now also sees a body a shell consumes AFTER the redirection through a pipe (\`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| bash\`, \`cat \<\<EOF \| grep … \| sh\`) or via a file a shell later runs (\`cat \<\<'EOF' \>x.sh; sh x.sh\`), so those refuse — and, after the review round, so do \`(sh x.sh)\`, \`{ sh x.sh; }\`, \`if true; then sh x.sh; fi\`, \`cat x.sh \| sh\`, \`sh -c '. x.sh'\`, \`. x.sh\`, \`source x.sh\`, \`tee x.sh \<\<EOF; sh x.sh\`, a quoted \`\> "x.sh"\` target, \`sh /dev/stdin \<\<EOF\`, \`bash -c sh \<\<EOF\` and \`cat \<\<EOF \| eval "$(cat)"\`, each verified to run the body. Pinned in tests/test_local_transport.py, reproduced against /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash first, and screened by a 671,995-case differential fuzz vs origin/main whose every difference is explained by the two changed mechanisms (numbers in the summary; the earlier \`refusals_cleared = 0\` claim is withdrawn). Still open in this direction, listed in \`_detection_variants\` and pinned as misses: a heredoc body written to a file run by a bare path (\`… \>x.sh; ./x.sh\`), by a variable-carried name (\`for f in x.sh; do sh $f; done\`) or by the environment (\`BASH_ENV=x.sh bash -c :\`); a heredoc opened inside a command substitution (\`eval "$(cat \<\<EOF …)"\`, read as quoted text); \`xargs -I{} sh -c '{}'\` (each line becomes the command line); and a \`;\` inside a \`( )\` group of a pipe stage (\`cat \<\<EOF \| (cd /tmp; sh)\`). \*\*Two pre-existing false positives are unchanged\*\* (\`git commit -m "fix;mkfs.ext4 handling"\` → mkfs; \`grep -r "(shutdown" docs/\` → power_cycle), and \`_PATH_PREFIX\` added a third class no approval can lift: a path to a script whose basename starts with a hardline name — \`./mkfs.sh\` (mkfs), \`./reboot.sh\`, \`./scripts/shutdown.sh --dry-run\`, \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` (power_cycle) — all None on 1c5054ea, all refused on 494a13b2 (bare \`reboot.sh\` already refused before; the prefix extends it to every path spelling). This slice left that (c) class unclosed on purpose: any name- or path-only rule that cleared \`./mkfs.sh\` or \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` would also clear a TRUE refusal — \`./mkfs.sh\` is structurally \`mkfs.\<suffix\>\` exactly like the real \`/sbin/mkfs.ext4\`, and \`/opt/app/bin/halt\` is \`\<path\>/\<hardline-name\>\` exactly like a copied \`/tmp/mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` — so it needs approval-context (is the target a workspace script or a real system binary) the pure static floor does not have. \*\*And a measured cost, re-measured after #1177 removed the breadth cap\*\* (\`_MAX_SHELL_EXPANSIONS\` and the \`MAX_ARG_CHARS\` re-expansion guard are gone; every level-1 payload is unwrapped again): on a maximal 64x4000-character argv the pass is roughly unchanged (79.6 ms on the pre-#1177 module vs 82.3 ms after, same machine, best of 5), but a command made of 2,000 nested \`sh -c "sh -c 'echo ok'"\` segments (57k chars) goes from 196 ms to 380 ms — synchronous, on the event loop, before every exec. The docstring's linear-in-input argument holds (each level's payloads are disjoint substrings); an independent re-run on another machine saw 79.0→80.6 ms and 125→151 ms, so the constant grows for nested input but the factor is machine-dependent and no test pins either number. This slice's first pass claimed no measurable cost on that maximal 64x4000-character argv (78.7 → 78.4 ms); the review showed the claim held only for input with no \`case\` word and never exercised the redirect-target branch, which was quadratic. Re-measured after the review round (best of 3, same machine, origin/main → this branch): the maximal 64x4000 argv 62.0 → 58.6 ms; the same argv filled with case/in prose 18,207 → 105 ms (main's own \`\\bcase\\b.\*?\\besac\\b\` was super-linear there too); 4000 chars of \`case x in\` 21.6 → 6.5 ms; eight 4000-char argv items of prose 316 → 14 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<EOF \| sh\` blocks (12 KB) 39.8 → 64.9 ms; 400 \`cat \<\<E \>x\` blocks in 4000 chars 25 → 39 ms (2,471 ms on the first pass); 1,600 such blocks (33.6 KB) 132 → 201 ms (97.5 s on the first pass); 2,000 nested \`sh -c\` segments 146 → 148 ms; 300 real \`case\` statements 57 → 60 ms. The heredoc-with-target shapes cost about 1.5x main, linear, because the statement lines after a heredoc are tokenised once and shared; nothing else pays. Six test items pin wall-clock bounds (0.25–2 s) two orders of magnitude above these; no test pins a ratio. \*\*Adversarial review of the original slice found four more defects, all fixed and each reproduced first\*\* against real /bin/sh, /bin/dash and /bin/bash: (1) \`_shell_c_payloads\` demanded the word after the wrappers BE the shell, so \`env FOO=1 sh -c 'mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda'\` — and \`sudo -u root sh -c …\`, \`nice -n 10 sh -c …\` — skipped the whole \`sh -c\` screening while deleting \`FOO=1 \` made the same string \`hardline_denied:mkfs\`; \`_CMD\` had been widened for that assignment prefix and the unwrapper had not. (2) \`_unquoted_lines\` read a backslash-newline as a statement separator when it is a line CONTINUATION, so \`mk\\\` + newline + \`fs.ext4 /dev/sda\`, which all three shells splice into \`mkfs.ext4\`, was seen as two harmless fragments. (3) every heredoc body line became a command position, so \`cat \> x.yaml \<\<'EOF'\` / \`shutdown: graceful\` / \`EOF\` was refused as \`power_cycle\`; body lines were joined as data in the flat text. \*\*#1177's second review showed that fix was incomplete in three ways, all closed there:\*\* \`segments\` still tokenised every body line, so \`cat \> README.md \<\<'EOF'\` / \`rm -rf / will destroy the box\` / \`EOF\` stayed a \`recursive_root_removal\` refusal; the rule had no exception for a heredoc a SHELL consumes, so \`bash \<\<EOF\` / \`mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda\` / \`EOF\` returned None (a bypass the heredoc work itself opened); and any \`\<\<\` — inside quotes, inside \`$(( ))\`, a \`\<\<\<\` here-string, or with a delimiter that never returns — opened a heredoc and switched the newline screening off for every following line. (4) \`{\` anchored bare, so \`jq '{reboot: .needs_reboot}'\` was a \`power_cycle\` refusal; POSIX requires whitespace after \`{\` for a brace group, and \`{ mkfs.ext4 /dev/sda; }\` is still refused. Each fix is red-proofed on its own. [d1](../agents/core/environments/terminal_contract.py) [d2](../tests/test_local_transport.py) |

| <a id="h487"></a>H487 | Ask a human on whatever surface they are on, carry their reason back, and fail closed on silence | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (a) Add an optional, bounded (≤280 chars) human reason to a reject on every surface: the HUD body, the Telegram reject flow, \`nerva approvals reject --reason\`, and ActionApprovalQueue.decide. Store it on the task or item and pass it verbatim into the model-visible result: the browser step reason, the PendingRequests 'refused' step detail, and the tool result. (b) Coalesce identical pending asks, same agent, tool or kind, and canonical args/fingerprint, onto one leader card, and fan the leader's decision out to every waiter. (c) Persist a distinct 'expired' state when an await times out, so the HUD stops offering it and a late approve cannot flip it. Surface 'expired, unanswered' separately from 'rejected by \<who\>' in the model-visible result. | <a id="h490"></a>H490 | Start with zero customization to prove a bug is not your own setup | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Safe mode now runs in Hermes' whole reduced posture, on top of the owner customizations H275 leaves out, and still relaxes no gate. JARVIS_SAFE_MODE=1 (or \`python serve.py --safe-mode\`) is the one switch; LAYERS (agents/core/safe_mode.py:54) gains four layers after H275's seven. Plugins: none is built (Orchestrator._build_plugins, agents/core/orchestrator.py:1236, called where the plugin manager used to be built, agents/core/orchestrator.py:989), so no integration is reached and no plugin data (WorldView, the Signal Layer, the WorldView knowledge-graph sync) reaches a prompt; the hub's .env credentials still load. The permission gate switches every plugin off as well (apply_load_set, agents/core/plugin_gate.py:647), without touching the shared built-in table, and the plugin toggle answers 409 \`safe_mode\` without rewriting the saved choice (toggle_plugin, agents/core/routers/plugins.py:97). Outbound webhooks: ntfy and the owner-configured webhook channels are not started (agents/web.py:571, agents/web.py:614), a proactive send to an owner destination refuses (send_to_target, agents/core/channels/outbound.py:171), and the inbound webhook receiver, whose hooks start agent runs, answers 503 before it reads its switch (_receiver_refusal, agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:238). Memory: no pre-turn recall (_recall_block, agents/core/orchestrator.py:2973) and no core block (_living_core_memory_block, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3056) enters a prompt, and the H314 memory tool reads as memory switched off, refusing with its off description (agents/core/memory_tool.py:370); the conversation's own history stays. Settings: each setting that loosens an approval or widens a budget (FORCED_SETTINGS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:77: the actuation and tool-loop switches, earned autonomy, ambient, acquisition, the input and output scans, the autonomy caps and interrupt budget, the sandbox timeout and memory, the model pull cap, the guest tool list, cloud fallback and product posture) reads the stricter of the owner's value and its shipped default (stricter, agents/core/safe_mode.py:138), so an owner who tightened one keeps it; a switch is read by truthiness as the runtime reads it; the three loosening settings with no shipped row are dropped (UNSEEDED_SETTINGS, agents/core/safe_mode.py:100). The runtime settings pass through override_settings (agents/core/safe_mode.py:159) before and after the product posture is applied (agents/core/orchestrator.py:1262), and the orchestrator's boot reads of the sandbox and autonomy caps go through safe_mode.get_value (agents/core/safe_mode.py:180, agents/core/orchestrator.py:607). Settings whose default is already the loosest value keep the owner's choice. Nerva runs no owner-configured shell hooks, so there are none to leave out. The mode and the layers it left out are in /api/security/posture (\`safe_mode\`, _safe_mode_status, agents/core/routers/security.py:372) besides /healthz, /readyz, /status and /api/status, in the boot log (agents/web.py:479), and in the HUD banner, which names the new layers (frontend/src/safe-mode-banner.tsx). The kernel, the security, secrets and approval modules, the tool profiles, the capability actions and the egress policy still do not read the flag, and the H275 test pinning the exact list of modules that do now names the six new readers. tests/test_h490_safe_mode_posture.py (56 cases) covers the layer order, every forced and unseeded key being a real setting and its default its own stricter value, the stricter rule per kind (switches by truthiness, numbers, a bool that is not a number, the guest list, the ordered choices), settings served unchanged when off, put back when on and copied, the owner's tighter value kept, nothing reported when nothing moved, an unseeded key alone counting, the boot read, the runtime-settings load, no plugin built but the .env still loaded, the gate refusing every plugin with the shared table untouched, the toggle refusing and keeping the saved choice, a proactive send refused, the receiver's 503 before its switch is read, the lifespan's ntfy and webhook-channel gates, no recall and no core block (nothing cached) and recall switched off not reported, the memory tool read and offer, the posture field, the boot log and the banner labels; the banner has a vitest case. 52 mutants, all killed. | <a id="h495"></a>H495 | Never let a credential reach a log, a transcript, an approval card or an export | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | This is one of the row's seven paths. Hermes applies \`redact.py\` to logs, verbose output, tool output, approval prompts, gateway messages, transcripts and memory; this slice covers LOGS. Still owed, and the rationale calls them the part that matters most: the hash-chained audit DB (a \`cat .env\` from \`terminal_run\` still writes a live credential into an append-only log that by design cannot be edited), the decision-inbox approval card the owner reads on a phone, task payloads, terminal output, and the observability exports — which Hermes scrubs unconditionally with no knob, returning \`\[redaction-unavailable\]\` rather than raw text. Also absent: the second mask mode (a syntactically INVALID \`«redacted:ghp_…»\` sentinel for file reads, so an agent cannot write a masked-but-plausible key back and silently kill the credential), \`code_file=True\` sparing source constants, \`_mask_control_split_tokens()\` for tokens smuggled across ANSI/zero-width characters, and the shared \`.env\` basename list. The two silent-degradation paths named at review are now CLOSED, after the SAST gate forced the question: a handler whose \`addFilter\` fails is counted and published through \`uncovered_handler_count()\` and a warning naming how many handlers are writing unredacted, and a \`loggerDict\` walk that raises warns that coverage fell back to root-only. The one silent swallow left is the filter's own last-ditch blank (\`# nosec B110\`), where a \`logging.Filter\` that raises would take down the caller's log call and the scanner has already failed. \`install_log_redaction()\` remains exported although the \`everywhere\` variant is what production uses. \*\*A later slice's full-suite run surfaced one more thing about this control, recorded here rather than fixed:\*\* the scanner's \`high_entropy_secret\` pattern matches an ordinary filesystem path. Under \`-n auto --dist loadfile\` a warning naming a pytest tmp file arrived as \`truncated: \[REDACTED:high_entropy_secret\].md\`, which is what a real operator would see when a log line names a file with a long random-looking segment — a debuggability cost the module docstring already warns about in general terms, now with a concrete case. Separately, \`setup_logging()\` installs the filter on every handler of every logger and nothing took it back off, so every later test on the same xdist worker ran under a live redactor. The installer is PRODUCTION code, not the tests: \`agents/run.py\` calls \`setup_logging()\` at module level, so importing it installs the filter during pytest COLLECTION — before any fixture runs — and eight test files import it; \`agents/web.py\` calls it from the FastAPI lifespan, so any test that drives the app installs it again; five test files call it directly on top of that. There is no list of leaking files to fix. The cleanup therefore lives in \`tests/conftest.py\` as an autouse fixture that strips unconditionally BEFORE each test as well as after — a snapshot-and-restore fixture, which is what the two neighbouring isolation fixtures do and what this was first written as, treats a collection-time install as pre-existing and preserves it for the life of the worker. That version went green locally and RED in CI on exactly the test it was meant to fix. The first attempt at it matched by \`isinstance\` and silently did nothing: \`agents/\` is on \`sys.path\`, so \`core.security.log_redaction\` and \`agents.core.security.log_redaction\` are two module objects over this one file with two distinct \`SecretRedactionFilter\` classes, and the root handler was carrying the other one. The match is by class name now, and four paired tests pin both halves — including the alternate import path, which is the case the first attempt missed. [d1](../agents/core/security/log_redaction.py) [d2](../agents/core/log.py) [d3](../tests/test_log_redaction.py) |
| <a id="h496"></a>H496 | Keep one identity's credentials out of another identity's turn | skip | Exclus intenționat | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h497"></a>H497 | Let a stranger reach the assistant only after the owner says so | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The row names four hardening gaps; (a) is closed, (c) is closed for the deeplink path, (b) and (d) are open.\*\* (c) \*revocation reaching live adapters\*: \`revoke_deeplinks()\` and \`unpair()\` now act on the one object the Telegram adapter redeems from and the gateway consults on every message, so there is no second copy for a link or an approval to survive in — pinned by \`test_revoking_on_the_shared_store_reaches_the_live_telegram_adapter\`. Slack and Discord have no deeplink path and gate through the same gateway instance. Still outstanding, from the row itself: (b) \*no lockout after N failed attempts\* — the row wants a 1 h lockout after 5 failed approvals, checked BEFORE the pending lookup so a code already in flight cannot be brute-forced past it; there is still only a rate limit and an in-memory guess budget, both of which reset on restart. (d) \*no admin-vs-user command split on channels\*, so any paired sender gets every command; the row wants a split with an always-allowed floor of \`{help, whoami}\`. \*\*Unaddressed from the Hermes column:\*\* pairing-code GENERATION (8 characters from a 32-char unambiguous alphabet via \`secrets.choice\`) and the code's 1 h TTL — the code here never expires; "max 3 pending per platform" (the store bounds 200 globally); "1 request per user per 10 min" (it is 5); \`unauthorized_dm_behavior\` treating inbox-shaped platforms differently from chat-shaped ones; and the ordered authorization union (allow-all flag → pairing approvals → per-platform allowlist → global allowlist → global allow-all → default DENY), which is still an ad-hoc sequence in \`is_allowed\`/\`gate_inbound\`. \*\*Two honest limits of the second slice.\*\* The store itself still has no defence against a second instance — the guarantee is one instance per process by construction, and the premise test says so in words. And an install that had relocated its data root (\`JARVIS_HOME\`) and paired senders through the HUD kept them in \`\<cwd\>/memory_logs/sender_pairing.json\`; the store now opens under the data root, so those senders are held once more until re-approved (the owner's allowlisted ids never lived in that file and are unaffected). \*\*And the limit carried from the first slice.\*\* Stretching is a work factor, not a wall: a four-digit code goes from milliseconds to minutes, an eight-character mixed-alphabet code becomes infeasible, and \*\*nothing enforces a floor on what the owner types\*\* — \`PairingCodeBody\` caps the length at 128 and sets no minimum — while generating the code for them, the row's own prescription, is not implemented. [d1](../agents/core/channels/pairing.py) [d2](../agents/core/channels/telegram.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |

| <a id="h500"></a>H500 | Tell the owner when the software actually installed on their box is known-vulnerable | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h501"></a>H501 | Warn about a dangerous deployment posture before it becomes an incident | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now warns about a dangerous host posture, as Hermes does, on top of a network half that was already stronger. assert_safe_bind (agents/core/boot_guards.py) still refuses an unauthenticated non-loopback bind and prints the exact env vars that fix it, and GET /api/security/posture still reports the secrets backend, skill signing, sandbox isolation and guardrails honestly. agents/core/host_posture.py adds the host half: three read-only checks that never block and never change anything, each answering ok, warn or unknown (it could not tell, never a false green), with a reason, what it saw and the fix. check_root (agents/core/host_posture.py:71) warns when the process runs as root (euid 0) on any host, headless included, or elevated on Windows (IsUserAnAdmin). check_sshd (agents/core/host_posture.py:168) reads /etc/ssh/sshd_config the way sshd does (_SshdScan, agents/core/host_posture.py:120): Include files are read in place, relative paths from /etc/ssh, each pattern's hits in lexical order, at most 16 deep; Keyword value, Keyword=value and trailing comments are accepted and keywords and values are case-insensitive; the first PasswordAuthentication outside a Match block wins, so a drop-in Included at the top overrides the main file; a Match block (to the end of its file, an Include inside it conditional) never sets the global value but a Match block that turns passwords on warns too; an absent directive is the sshd default yes and warns; no sshd_config is ok; an unreadable file is unknown unless the answer was read elsewhere; an unrecognised value is unknown. check_container_storage (agents/core/host_posture.py:241) detects a container (/.dockerenv, /run/.containerenv, or a docker, kubepods, containerd, libpod or lxc cgroup of PID 1, in_container) and then finds the mount the data root (resolved through symlinks) lives on in /proc/self/mountinfo (parse_mountinfo with its octal escapes, mount_of taking the longest containing mount point and the later of two stacked mounts): the container's own root filesystem or a tmpfs warns, a volume or bind mount is ok, no mountinfo or no mount is unknown. run_checks and report (agents/core/host_posture.py:273, :287) run the three independently, a failing check becoming an unknown row. The hub logs each warning once per process at start, after logging is set up, off the event loop (log_startup, agents/web.py lifespan). GET /api/security/posture (admin) gains host: the three findings and a warnings count (agents/core/routers/security.py). python scripts/doctor.py and nerva doctor show them as the advisory rows host_not_root, sshd_no_passwords and container_storage at the end of the report: a warning is warn with the fix and never fails the verdict, a check that could not tell or could not run is skip, never ok (check_host_posture, scripts/doctor.py:239). The module is stdlib only so the doctor can use it on a broken install. The shipped Dockerfile has no USER, so a stock container now shows the root warning; running the image as a non-root user is left to the deploy. Tests: tests/test_h501_host_posture.py (42), with real sshd_config trees in a temporary directory (drop-ins read first and last, lexical order, absolute and quoted Includes, Match blocks in the main and in an included file, a trailing comment naming a file, an Include loop) and real mountinfo text. 55 mutants: 53 killed after five cases were added; the other two were dead code, removed. [d1](../agents/core/host_posture.py) [d2](../agents/core/boot_guards.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/security.py) |
| <a id="h502"></a>H502 | Refuse an MCP server entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume, and never hand one the owner's keys | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The documented per-server \`env\` migration path does not exist as a reachable surface.\*\* \`MCPServerConfig\` has no \`env\` field (\`routers/mcp.py:131-143\`, no \`model_config\`), \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` drops the key (pydantic's default \`extra="ignore"\`), and neither \`to_config\` (\`client.py:798-812\`) nor \`load_from_config\` (\`:814-849\`) writes or reads one — the module docstring says as much at \`client.py:41-45\`. The only remedy an owner can actually reach is the \*\*host-wide\*\* \`JARVIS_MCP_STDIO_ALLOWED_ENV\` (\`:92-98\`, "Host-wide, not per-server"), which is a strictly weaker grant than the per-server one the docs describe: an owner whose forge server needs \`GITHUB_TOKEN\` must allow it for every stdio server on the host. Whether to ship on that or build the per-server surface first is a product decision, written up as \`docs/OWNER_TASKS.md\` §P28.3 with both options. \*\*Related and also owner-level:\*\* \`XAUTHORITY\` and \`DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS\` are both on \`STDIO_ENV_ALLOWLIST\` (\`client.py:169\`), and today one is listed-but-silently-dropped (\`_SECRET_LIKE\` at \`:174-176\` matches the \`AUTH\` in \`XAUTHORITY\`) while the other is listed-and-handed-over — they should be consistent in one direction or the other. \*\*And the honest ceiling, stated wherever the guarantee is:\*\* this is \*\*defence in depth, not a security boundary\*\*, and since #1179 the module states that ceiling by TWO routes rather than one (\`client.py:17-36\`). The child is same-UID with no namespace, so a server that can run a subprocess reads the withheld variables straight out of \`/proc/\<ppid\>/environ\`; and the baseline narrows the \*\*filesystem\*\* not at all — \`cwd\` defaults to the hub's own working directory, so \`open(".env")\` hands back exactly the keys that were withheld, which \`cwd_secret_files()\` now says out loud at connect (WARNING, filenames only, \`client.py:565-571\`) rather than leaving the owner to infer a protection from the withheld count alone. What the baseline buys is that a \*careless\* or \*curious\* server does not get them by accident, and that an owner's \`JARVIS_\*\` settings are not ambient in a third party's process. It does not stop an adversary, and the row says so rather than implying otherwise; closing the filesystem route as well as the \`/proc\` one needs real process isolation (separate UID, namespace or container — \`client.py:33-36\`), which is not shipped. \*\*Unaddressed from the row:\*\* there is no refusal of an MCP entry that is a backdoor wearing an MCP costume — no command allow-listing, no signature or provenance check on the spawned binary (the docstring itself calls the screener "still-unshipped", \`client.py:34\`), and \`POST /api/admin/mcp\` still stores \`req.command\` verbatim after checking only transport, trust tier, tool-filter shape and bounds, and duplicate name (\`routers/mcp.py:174-227\`, \`:184\`); the only shape check is \`_command_argv()\`'s shell-metacharacter/NUL refusal at connect (\`client.py:519-529\`), which is not a screener. #1179 changed none of this — it explicitly chose disclosure over refusal. This slice hardens what the child \*receives\*; the row also asks who the child is allowed to \*be\*. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../agents/core/mcp/worldview_write.py) |
| <a id="h503"></a>H503 | Unpack an archive from another machine without letting it write outside the destination | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | archive_safe is the only module in Nerva that reads an archive. Backup verify and restore unpack through extract_tar_path (agents/core/archive_safe.py:573; agents/core/backup.py:598, agents/core/backup.py:667). Marketplace install_from_zip unpacks through extract_zip_bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:637; agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:816). The old backup._safe_extract and the marketplace _safe_targets+extractall pass are gone. An AST scan (tests/test_archive_safe.py:702, with probes at tests/test_archive_safe.py:688) fails when any other product module imports tarfile or zipfile outside a write-only allowlist, opens an archive for reading or calls an extract-family method. normalize_member (agents/core/archive_safe.py:122) folds backslash to '/' first. It then refuses empty, NUL, POSIX-absolute, Windows-absolute, UNC and drive-lettered names, and any '..' or empty part. Only regular files and directories are written. Symlink, hardlink, device, fifo and sparse tar members raise (agents/core/archive_safe.py:504), and so do symlink, device, fifo, socket and encrypted zip entries (agents/core/archive_safe.py:596). _Planner (agents/core/archive_safe.py:182) validates every member before the first write: name, type, in-archive conflicts and duplicates, member count and declared bytes (agents/core/archive_safe.py:257). It also checks the destination's existing content (agents/core/archive_safe.py:286): a link on the way to a member is refused, and so is a directory where a file goes (tests/test_archive_safe.py:365, tests/test_backup.py:471). The pre-scan reads the stream to its end (agents/core/archive_safe.py:522), so a bad gzip CRC, trailing data or a corrupt header part-way through is a refusal, not a shortened tree (tests/test_archive_safe.py:482, tests/test_archive_safe.py:517). Bytes are counted again while streaming (agents/core/archive_safe.py:389). The byte budget is clamped to free space plus the blocks of the single-link files the archive replaces (agents/core/archive_safe.py:314), so an in-place --force restore fits (tests/test_backup.py:443). PAX and GNU long-name header payloads are capped before tarfile reads them, 1 MiB each and 256 MiB in total (agents/core/archive_safe.py:461, agents/core/archive_safe.py:483; tests/test_backup.py:515). A link or hardlinked name at a member's path is unlinked and the file is created O_EXCL\|O_NOFOLLOW, never written through (agents/core/archive_safe.py:373; tests/test_archive_safe.py:323, tests/test_archive_safe.py:349). Backups take their caps from JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_MEMBERS and JARVIS_BACKUP_MAX_BYTES (agents/core/backup.py:86); skill packages use SKILL_PACKAGE_LIMITS (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:42). On the producing side, create_backup writes both the encrypted and the plaintext branch through atomic_write, a sibling temp file that is fsynced and then os.replace'd (agents/core/archive_safe.py:654; agents/core/backup.py:415, agents/core/backup.py:420). Archives are GNU-format tar (agents/core/backup.py:474). The export walk skips symlinks (agents/core/archive_safe.py:715; agents/core/backup.py:401). publish_skill uses it on the staged copy it signs (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:392); the copy itself is built from the bytes of one source snapshot, which refuses a linked folder, a link or non-regular member and a file that changed while read (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:375), which has its own test (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:219). Every other name, hardlinked ones included, is archived as its own regular member (agents/core/backup.py:434, agents/core/backup.py:458; tests/test_backup.py:388). verify and restore compare the manifest's file_count with what the archive held (agents/core/backup.py:625; tests/test_backup.py:430). install_from_zip stages inside skills_dir, runs the signature gate there, and only then renames the tree into place (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:858, agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:880). A non-zip upload is a 400 refusal (tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:194). Staging left by a killed install is swept once it is an hour old (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:773; tests/test_marketplace_governance_hf12_12.py:203). Restore stays an operator CLI action. It prints 'partial restore' when a failure came after the first write (agents/core/backup.py:716) and 'nothing restored' only for a pre-scan refusal (agents/core/backup.py:722). tests/test_backup.py:606 pins that no product module, script or subprocess caller reaches it. The pre-scan also refuses a component longer than the destination's NAME_MAX or a path past PATH_MAX, which the existing-path walk cannot see under a new directory (agents/core/archive_safe.py:251; tests/test_archive_safe.py:726), and a write the destination refuses mid-restore (permissions, a full disk) is a named ArchiveRejected marked partial, never a traceback (agents/core/archive_safe.py:445; tests/test_archive_safe.py:738). A restore leaves the archive's manifest in the target; create_backup never archives a top-level backup_manifest.json from the data root (agents/core/backup.py:482), so a backup of a restored root carries one manifest and verifies, restores and serves the forget purge's verified pre-snapshot (tests/test_backup.py:636, tests/test_backup.py:675). A backup of a restored root made before this change carries that name twice; verify and restore accept exactly that one repeat, last wins as tarfile resolved it, and count the dropped copy against the manifest's file_count (agents/core/backup.py:599, agents/core/backup.py:642; tests/test_backup.py:651). The repeat is bounded: the dropped copy still counts against the member and byte caps, and a third copy or any other duplicate is refused (agents/core/archive_safe.py:220, agents/core/archive_safe.py:223; tests/test_archive_safe.py:768, tests/test_archive_safe.py:775, tests/test_archive_safe.py:790). Known limit: backups made on main from a data root holding a symlink or hardlink fail verify and restore by design. [d1](../agents/core/archive_safe.py) [d2](../agents/core/backup.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/marketplace.py) |
| <a id="h504"></a>H504 | Fail loudly when TLS trust is misconfigured instead of silently not verifying | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva now fails loudly when TLS trust is misconfigured, and verification is never quietly off, as Hermes requires. agents/core/tls_trust.py is the one place that decides what outbound clients verify against; agents/core/http_client.py re-exports its tls_verify for the plugin clients (:180). (1) Boot validation: validate_ca_environment (agents/core/tls_trust.py:170) checks every CA variable that is set, JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE, SSL_CERT_FILE, REQUESTS_CA_BUNDLE and CURL_CA_BUNDLE as files and SSL_CERT_DIR as directories, and certifi itself. Each file must exist, be a file, load into an SSLContext and hold at least one certificate: any certificate, so a pinned self-signed server certificate counts (_check_file, :145; _count_certs through cert_store_stats, :137). certifi must also be at least 1 KB and importable. Each problem is reported with the variable, its value and what is wrong. It also carries a copy-pasteable repair for this host's shell (_repair, :107): export X="$(python -m certifi)" or unset X on POSIX, the PowerShell form on Windows, a path to the root for JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE, a certifi reinstall. When H273's provenance says a .env file set the value, the problem names that file (_set_in, :123). enforce_ca_environment (:217) raises SystemExit with every problem at once. The web lifespan runs it right after enforce_boot_posture (agents/web.py:467), before the data home is scaffolded or anything dials out, so a broken SSL_CERT_FILE is no longer an unnamed FileNotFoundError on the first model call. tls_verify's warnings now name the variable that actually held the bad value (SSL_CERT_FILE when that was the source). (2) Model egress on the anchor: llm_async_client (agents/core/llm/egress.py:95-98) gives every model backend's client verify_for(backend, base_url), so the Anthropic, Gemini, OpenRouter, OpenAI Responses, xAI, LM Studio, Ollama and VLM clients all verify against certifi plus JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE (or SSL_CERT_FILE). That is always an explicit SSLContext (trust_context, :94), so a client that keeps trust_env for the owner's proxy never has httpx read SSL_CERT_FILE itself; a caller's own verify or test transport is left alone. (3) Per-target switch: verify_for (:252) returns False only for a provider id or a host named exactly (case-insensitive, trailing dot ignored) in JARVIS_TLS_INSECURE_TARGETS (insecure_targets, :237). Every client built that way logs a WARNING naming its URL, and the start logs the list once. Under JARVIS_HARDENED=1 the list is ignored with an ERROR, and a bad or missing CA path never yields False. docs/FLAGS.md and .env.example document both variables. Local probes that build a plain httpx client (router.py health checks, ollama_control, lmstudio_control, model_setup, local_model_inventory) talk plain HTTP to loopback, and the boot check covers the variables they would read. Tests: tests/test_h504_tls_trust.py (45) uses real certificates made by cryptography (a CA, a leaf, a pinned self-signed leaf, a CRL-only PEM). It also runs end to end against a real HTTPS server on 127.0.0.1 whose certificate only the test CA signs: refused without the owner's root, accepted with JARVIS_CA_BUNDLE or SSL_CERT_FILE, accepted only for a listed target, and refused again when hardened. [d1](../agents/core/tls_trust.py) [d2](../agents/core/http_client.py) [d3](../agents/core/llm/egress.py) |
| <a id="h505"></a>H505 | Keep the agent's file tools out of credential stores and system files | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h506"></a>H506 | Treat files that steer future runs as a distinct always-ask class, on the way in and on the way out | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The read half now covers two of the three Nerva loaders and none of the external ones — the row stays partial.\*\* H506 requires the same file class to be scanned at context-load time with a positive match replaced by \`\[BLOCKED: … Content not loaded.\]\`. What holds: SOUL.md / SOUL.local.md through \`Agent._scan_soul_body\` (H387's evidence, not this row's — per-line quarantine over the union of raw and normalised copies, cross-line wraps, whole-body drop above the ratio; the U+200B and U+0007 spellings after 'now' are pinned in this row's own test file), and HEARTBEAT.md / HEARTBEAT.local.md through \`scan_heartbeat_config\`, entry refused rather than line-stubbed. \*\*Precisely what is not covered, stated rather than smoothed over:\*\* the Markdown body after the front-matter is discarded by \`_parse_heartbeat\` and reaches no model, log or scheduler, so it is not scanned — \`test_the_prose_body_is_not_loaded_and_therefore_not_scanned\` is the tripwire a loader that starts reading it must flip; no heartbeat text is model input today — the checklist strings are keyword-routed to skills with constant arguments and echoed into a logged run summary — so the scan guards the mapping the loader hands to the agent, not a live prompt path; the keyword scanner is literal after normalisation, so a Cyrillic homoglyph ('Іgnore'), a combining overlay, markdown emphasis inside a phrase ('Ignore _all_ previous') and a phrase split across two checklist items still scan clean — a confusables fold is a separate \`quarantine.py\` slice, and the homoglyph is pinned as a tripwire in this row's test file; and the SOUL cross-line path (\`_wrapped_injection_hits\`) builds its own normalised copy inside \`agent.py\`, so a blank-instead-of-space spelling that ALSO wraps across lines is not covered there (agent.py is untouched here; it is H387's evidence, being re-evaluated by a sibling stream). \*\*Adjacent, pre-existing, NOT fixed:\*\* the orchestrator's \`load_from_config\` overwrites every shipped HEARTBEAT.md cron+checklist with the agents.yaml interval (all twelve shipped agents carry one), so at runtime \`Agent.run_heartbeat\` finds no checklist and returns None for the shipped roster — the loader semantics this row describes are what the code does, not what the shipped agents experience (recorded as a separate task); \`load_all\` keys the config by the front-matter's own \`agent\` field, so a clean \`HEARTBEAT.local.md\` in one agent's directory that says \`agent: jarvis\` replaces Jarvis's schedule (a refused file cannot — the verdict is keyed by directory); and \`enabled: false\` is parsed and never honoured. Still open on the read side: AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md / GEMINI.md / \`.cursorrules\` / \`\*.mdc\` are read by the external harnesses rather than by Nerva, so 'the same file class on the way in' is not met for them. The write-side deviations stand as before: the row's \*"fails closed on a config read error"\* clause has no analogue because the roster is a hard-coded frozenset; \`allow_session=False\` holds only incidentally through the gated toolrpc \`autonomy_level="ask"\` intake, with nothing pinning it; and the class covers \`file_write\`/\`file_delete\` only — the terminal contract, the skill importer and the desktop operator are outside it. Unverified, stated as such: whether the Telegram decision card and the HUD decision inbox render the write-side notice verbatim, and whether any HUD renders the \`blocked\` list (the API carries it; \`frontend/src/gap.tsx\` and \`agents/web/static/systems.js\` read \`heartbeats\` only). \`_mutate\`'s floor remains lifted by a caller-supplied \`approved=True\` by design. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../agents/core/tool_rpc.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_tools_instruction_floor.py) |
| <a id="h507"></a>H507 | Warn the model when the code it just wrote contains a known-dangerous pattern | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Code the agent writes is checked against a table of known-dangerous patterns, and the findings go back to the model and the owner. Nothing is ever refused. agents/core/code_guidance.py holds 25 rules (RULES, agents/core/code_guidance.py:59), each scoped to the file types it applies to. They cover unsafe deserialization (pickle/dill/joblib/marshal loads; yaml.load without SafeLoader, and yaml.unsafe_load; torch.load without weights_only=True); command and code injection (os.system/popen, shell=True, Python eval/exec, JS eval and new Function, child_process.exec and execSync, a Go exec.Command of a shell); SQL built from an f-string or \`%\`; tempfile.mktemp; XSS sinks (innerHTML/outerHTML assignment, document.write, dangerouslySetInnerHTML); and crypto and TLS footguns (ECB mode, crypto.createCipher without an IV, verify=False, unverified SSL contexts, CERT_NONE, rejectUnauthorized: false, NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED=0, InsecureSkipVerify: true, md5/sha1 over a password). They also cover XXE parser flags and a remote \`\<script\>\` without integrity=. A separate check (agents/core/code_guidance.py:117) flags \`${{ github.event.\* }}\` inside a GitHub Actions \`run:\` step, on the run line or in its \`\|\`/\`\>\` block, but not in \`if:\`, \`env:\` or \`with:\`. The guards keep the usual false positives quiet: \`model.eval()\` and \`obj.eval()\`, \`yaml.load(..., Loader=SafeLoader)\`, \`torch.load(..., weights_only=True)\`, \`regex.exec()\`, an \`===\` comparison with innerHTML, \`hashlib.md5(data)\`, a script with integrity=, whole-line comments and non-code files. scan (agents/core/code_guidance.py:163) returns \`{rule, line, message}\`, at most 20 findings over the first 1 MB. The findings go to three places. On the file_write approval card, FileTools.classify_mutation (agents/core/file_tools.py:1157) adds \`code_warnings\` (rule@line, at most 200 characters), \`code_warning_count\` and a title notice; it never sets a class, so H506's approval binding is untouched, and an instruction-file notice and the warning share the title. On the write result (FileTools.write_file, agents/core/file_tools.py:956), the model reads \`code_warnings\` with a \`code_guidance\` note that they are warnings, not refusals; a refused write carries none, and a failing scan never fails a write. For a marketplace install (agents/core/skills/marketplace.py:169; install and install-zip answer \`code_warnings\` when there are any, agents/core/routers/skills.py:462) and a generated skill (agents/core/skills/loader.py:595), scan_tree (agents/core/code_guidance.py:220) walks the folder, skipping symlinks and bounding files and findings; it logs a warning and keeps the list. The owner's switch is \`security.code_guidance\` (agents/core/settings_db.py:209, on by default; only an explicit off turns it off, and an unreadable store keeps it on). tests/test_h507_code_guidance.py (83 cases) covers every rule firing (40 cases), 26 guard cases, the cross-language scoping, the finding shape, the table size, the workflow rule with a blank line in a block and every non-run key, the bounds, the labels, the switch, the real ToolRPC card and approved execution, a clean and a non-code write, the shared instruction notice, a failing scan, a refused write, scan_tree (symlink, bounds, switch), generation, install with the route, and the setting row. There are 46 mutants: 44 killed and 2 equivalent (skipping non-code files before reading them is only a saving; a reset already done at the start of generation). [d1](../agents/core/code_guidance.py) [d2](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d3](../agents/core/skills/marketplace.py) |
| <a id="h508"></a>H508 | Catch content-level attacks in a command that pattern matching cannot see | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h509"></a>H509 | Sign in to a networked Nerva from a browser | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h510"></a>H510 | Stop a web page or a rebound DNS name from driving the local API | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | The Host guard exempts _PROBE_PATHS (agents/web.py:766, applied at :823-824), so /healthz, /readyz and /metrics answer a rebound name: live-verified, \`Host: evil.test\` returns 200 on /healthz and /metrics and reaches the route on /readyz, while every real API route returns 400. Because DNS rebinding makes the browser treat those responses as same-origin, an attacker page still reads the full Prometheus body (route templates, request counters, latency buckets) and the /readyz body naming the configured LLM backend and the agent/channel counts. The code names this residual itself at agents/web.py:799-801 ("a rebound page can therefore still read the golden-signal counters"). Close it by narrowing the exemption rather than removing it — the operability reason for it is sound. Suggested shape: keep /healthz and /readyz exempt but strip /readyz's detail to a bare ready/not-ready when the Host is not accepted, and require an accepted Host for /metrics unless the request is unambiguously non-browser (no Origin and no Sec-Fetch-Site, or an explicit JARVIS_METRICS_ALLOW_ANY_HOST opt-in for a monitor that scrapes by an unlisted name). Needs a test alongside test_healthz_from_any_host_is_exempt asserting that a refused Host cannot read the counter body. [d1](../agents/core/host_policy.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/proxy_trust.py) |
| <a id="h511"></a>H511 | Prove every route's required credential rather than maintaining a list by hand | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h512"></a>H512 | See who authenticated, who failed, and when | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Add auth event types: admin/user auth success, auth failure with reason (missing, invalid, network-disabled), token issued, token rotated, token revoked. Emit them from _admin_guard and _user_guard: failures always; successes deduplicated per token hash and client so the chain is not flooded. Also emit from TokenStore issue/rotate/revoke_all, which covers the CLI. Each event records scope, client host, reason and a token-hash prefix. A fixed redacted-field set (token, x-admin-token, x-user-token, authorization, cookie, code, state, ticket) is dropped before serialisation. Writes are best-effort: an audit failure must never change the allow/401/403 decision. The rotation route should switch to the new type. Tests must cover all of this. [d1](../agents/core/security/audit.py) [d2](../agents/core/security/types.py) [d3](../agents/web.py) |

| <a id="h515"></a>H515 | Generate an image from a prompt (and edit an existing image) | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | The real ComfyUI path does not yet use the existing LLM unload/restore orchestrator. Frozen media.generate/file.write action-kind requirements need explicit architecture adjudication; current local tool.rpc and cloud plugin.egress mediation are verified but are not silently equated to that requirement. Local reference blending is pixel composition and upscale is bicubic resampling. The accepted adaptation requires one cloud seam, not eight-provider fan-out. Ambiguous provider attempts never retry automatically; catalog opt-out is respected. No live ComfyUI, billed provider or native-device validation is claimed. This acknowledgment foundation is not a VRAM swap: canonical aliases, production backend/controller identity, atomic per-generation leases, owner control coordination and authoritative hardware snapshots remain unresolved. Existing coarse headroom and no-victim overcommit behavior remain. ComfyUI unknown submissions need reconciliation before any future exclusive lease can safely release. | <a id="h518"></a>H518 | Browse, search and export everything the assistant has generated | keep | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The unified gallery now scans retained generated and attachment metadata through bounded keyset pages. Search and kind filters apply during each scan, including MIME matches; zero-match pages retain continuation to older records. Cursor fields, ordering and filter/source binding are checked before catalog or header I/O. Each request inspects at most 200 candidate headers and retains the existing authenticated path/byte validation. The HUD resets and cancels stale searches, reports retained versus loaded/scanned counts separately, and exports an explicit selection of at most 200 files/128 MiB. Independent review and desktop/responsive-browser tests cover older-only matches, empty-page continuation, deletion boundaries, source flags, malformed cursors and exact export selection. Shared attachment previews now abort on card unmount and reject late fetch/body continuations; owned object URLs are revoked even before a pending React state update commits. Reassessment against the frozen keep/superior row confirms the bounded retained-catalog browse/search/export contract. Retention and unavailable-byte handling match the original scope; deleted/evicted history restoration, native controls and unlimited ZIPs are not acceptance requirements. Timestamp/key traversal excludes newer appends without promising exact membership against backdated inserts. The current local generator catalogs produced assets; legacy cloud proposals do not execute a cloud image backend. Future approved cloud completion must persist catalog output as part of H515, not be inferred from generic text-task completion. No live-provider or universal producer-provenance proof is claimed. The implemented cloud-image producer now writes canonical validated output and an idempotent opt-in catalog record before successful completion; independent signed-worker/mock-provider/blob/ZIP checks cover the previously conditional writeback obligation. The legacy generic text task is not treated as an image producer. Durable sending/partial/unknown states, current recipient/content checks and process locks prevent automatic replay, stale-held duplication and late-ack continuation. Synchronous local I/O is not preemptible, but no subsequent dispatch occurs after expiry. This is code equivalence with mocked HTTP; no live Telegram, new-provider/native-codec or arbitrary-path guarantee. The complete integrated backend suite collected 11,428 tests with 11,404 passes, 23 skips and one expected failure; no unexpected failures. | <a id="h525"></a>H525 | Refuse to speak in a cloned or persona voice without recorded owner consent | keep | Echivalent | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h526"></a>H526 | Make a spoken reply sound like speech rather than read-out markdown | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A spoken reply now sounds like speech rather than read-out markdown, as Hermes requires, at every text-to-speech entry point. agents/core/voice/speech_text.py is the one normaliser (for_speech, agents/core/voice/speech_text.py:143). It drops \<think\>/\<thinking\>/\<reasoning\> blocks, an unclosed one to the end, and everything before a closing tag that never opened. It drops fenced code (\`\`\` or ~~~, an unclosed fence to the end) and HTML tags. \[text\](url) reads as its text, !\[alt\](url) as its alt, and a bare or \<autolinked\> URL as "link"; the URL no longer eats a closing parenthesis or the sentence's full stop, so "doc (https://…)." is "doc (link).". Headings, list items (-, \*, +, •, 1., 1), task boxes), block quotes and table rows lose their markers and end with a full stop (_line, :115). Table cells read with commas; separator rows and horizontal rules are dropped. Bold, italic, strikethrough and inline-code markers go and their words stay (_inline, :104), with word guards so 2\*3\*4 and my_var_name survive. Symbols are said in the reply's language (_symbols, :95, WORDS, :79): & as and/și, % as percent/la sută, -\> → =\> as to/spre, and °, °C, °F as degrees/grade (Celsius, Fahrenheit). The language comes from the request, else a ro-RO-… voice, else the engine default (speech_lang, :85). Emoji go: pictographs, flags, keycaps, skin tones, ZWJ sequences, variation selectors and tag flags (_EMOJI, :61). Fish \[emotion\] tags are kept intact. Text with no letter or digit left is "" (_SAYABLE, :76), and the result is a fixed point. Wiring: TTSEngine.speak normalises before any backend, Fish included, and synthesises nothing when nothing is left (agents/core/voice/tts.py:150-158). An emotion-tags-only reply is also nothing for the other backends (:182). speak_stream normalises the whole reply before splitting sentences (:222), so a code or reasoning block spanning sentences is dropped whole. Through TTSEngine.speak this covers the wake-word VoicePipeline, VoiceChannel.send and chat voice notes: speakable() is for_speech plus its cap, and SpokenReply passes the chat's language (agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py:68). POST /tts answers 204 with X-Nerva-Speech: nothing_to_say instead of synthesising (agents/core/routers/voice.py:65), and /tts/stream frames carry the spoken sentences. The HUD twin is frontend/src/speech-text.ts (speechText, frontend/src/speech-text.ts:105). Both implementations are pinned by the same cases (frontend/src/test/speech-text-cases.json, 47 cases, read by both suites). SpeechStreamFilter (:129) drops a fenced block or reasoning block whose markers arrive split across token deltas, holding back a tail that could still become a marker (including a \<reasoning …\> tag split inside its attributes). An unclosed block at the end is never spoken. frontend/src/voice.ts uses it on the live sentence stream (:281, :332, :343). It normalises the whole reply for /tts/stream, /tts and the browser's own speechSynthesis voice (:204), and every streamed sentence (:302). A 204 is silence, never an empty clip or the fallback voice (:244, :314). Mobile sends the reply to /tts, so the hub normalises it; ttsFetchBase64 reads 204 as '' and speak() finishes at once (mobile/src/api/client.ts:2402, mobile/src/audio/tts.ts:23). Tests: tests/test_h526_speech_text.py (Python, every shared case plus the fixed-point property), frontend/src/test/speech-text.test.ts and voice-speech-text.test.tsx (a live push-to-talk turn with a fence split across deltas), and mobile/src/api/__tests__/tts.test.ts. 126 mutants: 124 caught after nine cases were added; the other two were redundant code, removed. [d1](../agents/core/voice/speech_text.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/tts.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/spoken_reply.py) |
