| <a id="h529"></a>H529 | Transcribe a voice message or dictated speech | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Un singur backend. Hermes are opt (faster-whisper local, \`local_command\`, Groq, OpenAI, Mistral Voxtral cu diarizare și timestamp-uri pe cuvânt, xAI, ElevenLabs Scribe, DeepInfra) plus provideri pe comandă de shell, un ABC \`TranscriptionProvider\`, selecție explicită strictă cu auto-detect doar la o instalare neconfigurată vreodată, un plafon pe lungimea promptului Whisper, trunchierea liniștii înainte de upload ca să scadă octeții trimiși, un picker și \`POST /api/audio/transcribe\`. Nimic din astea nu e livrat. Nu din lene: clauza de guvernanță a rândului spune că orice STT în cloud trece prin \`plugin.egress\` și trimite vorbirea înregistrată a owner-ului în afara cutiei — e aceeași clasă de dezvăluire ca TTS-ul în cloud și trebuie clasificat, nu pus ca implicit de config. Cache-ul motorului stă tot în \`routers/voice.py\`, care e casa greșită pentru el; canalul îl importă în loc să-l dubleze, iar mutarea lui e o schimbare separată. NEVERIFICAT pe servicii reale: niciun model Whisper încărcat cu adevărat. [d1](../agents/core/voice/stt.py) [d2](../agents/core/voice/hallucination.py) [d3](../agents/core/channels/inbound_voice.py) |
| <a id="h530"></a>H530 | Not turn a silent recording into a message | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Whisper nu tace când nu aude nimic — inventează. Pe liniște întoarce o propoziție fluentă din datele de antrenament: generice de subtitrare („Subtitles by the Amara.org community", „Subtitrarea: ..."), formule de încheiere („Thanks for watching!", „Mulțumesc pentru vizionare!"). Pentru o casetă de dictare e o enervare; aici e un control de siguranță, exact cum spune clauza de guvernanță a rândului: un mesaj vocal devine o tură \*inbound\*, o tură inbound poate cere lucruri, deci un transcript halucinat e o instrucțiune pe care n-a autorizat-o nimeni, intrată într-un sistem guvernat. \`hallucination.py\` o oprește. Designul e dictat de asimetria erorilor: a pierde un mesaj real e inacceptabil (cineva zice „mulțumesc" și dispare fără răspuns), a lăsa unul să treacă costă o tură ignorabilă. De aici două niveluri, nu o listă: frazele distinctive (\`CANNED\`) cad pe text; cele pe care oamenii chiar le spun (\`AMBIGUOUS\`) cad doar când durata \*decodată\* arată că înregistrarea era prea scurtă ca să le conțină — iar un apelant care nu știe durata le păstrează. Potrivirea e pe transcriptul întreg, niciodată pe substring; regula pentru generice cere marker ȘI vocabular exclusiv de generic, fiindcă prima variantă (doar markerul) mânca „am văzut pe amara.org un film bun". Filtrul rulează în \`STTEngine.transcribe\` și întoarce \`\[silence\]\` — santinela care exista deja și pe care fiecare apelant o tratează ca „nu s-a spus nimic" — deci ruta de dictare din browser o primește gratuit, nu doar calea nouă de pe Telegram. CORECTARE la auditul din 07.09: verdictul „missing" era pe jumătate greșit. A doua cerință a rândului — auto-stop pe liniște (VAD), ca utilizatorul să nu apese o tastă — EXISTĂ de mult în \`frontend/src/voice.ts\`: \`recordUtterance\` eșantionează RMS la 60 ms și oprește după \`SILENCE_MS\`, cu plafon dur și abandon când nimeni nu vorbește. Auditul a dat un \`rg -i 'vad'\` peste \`frontend/src/\` și a raportat „nimic relevant"; linia 111 spune „VAD" în litere. Ce lipsea cu adevărat era dovada: nimic nu o testa, fiindcă harness-ul comun folosește un MediaRecorder care se oprește singur și scurtcircuitează intervalul. \`voice-vad.test.tsx\` folosește unul care NU se oprește niciodată singur — dacă o tură se termină acolo, doar detectorul de liniște a putut-o termina — și acoperă fereastra de liniște, plafonul, abandonul și faptul că vorbirea continuă resetează ceasul. | <a id="h545"></a>H545 | Let the editor hand the agent MCP servers that exist only for that session | copy | Lipsă | Reevaluat | Every accepted part is missing: (1) an ACP session entry point (session/new, load, resume, fork) that accepts an mcpServers array; this depends on H542. (2) A session-scoped MCP registry layered over the global MCPManager, so that a server registered for one session is invisible to every other session and is torn down when the session ends. (3) Off-loop registration (asyncio.to_thread) with a bounded discovery join (default 1.5 s), so a dead server cannot block session creation. (4) A scheduled late refresh that re-reads the session's tool list after slow discovery finishes. (5) Governance: session-supplied servers pass through the same outbound MCP authorization gate as configured servers (H554, still partial), and their trust tier is capped at no wider than a configured server's. | <a id="h551"></a>H551 | Choose which of a server's tools the model may see | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Filtrele allow/deny pe nume și glob (\`tools_allow\`/\`tools_deny\`, potrivite cu \`fnmatchcase\` pe numele original al uneltei) restrâng atât listarea, cât și apelarea uneltelor MCP; deny prevalează (\`tool_visible\`, agents/core/mcp/client.py:603-610). \`_list_tools\` nu oferă o unealtă ascunsă (:596-601), iar \`MCPServer.call_tool\` răspunde \`tool_filtered\` înainte să plece vreun frame (:616-617), deci scoaterea unei unelte din filtru o revocă imediat, și la apel, nu doar la înregistrare. Un filtru malformat îngustează, nu lărgește: un allow care nu e listă devine \`\[\]\` (\`_clean_tool_patterns\`, :124-135). Limitele (64 de pattern-uri / 128 de caractere, :120-121) sunt fail-closed. Ruta de add (\`admin_mcp_add\`, agents/core/routers/mcp.py:200-209) refuză cu 400 \`{"error": "invalid_tool_filter", "field": ...}\` un \`tools_allow\` sau \`tools_deny\` peste limite, în loc să-l taie (\`tool_patterns_over_bounds\`, client.py:138-154), la fel ca pe o valoare care nu e listă sau pe o intrare goală. Un deny care nu poate fi păstrat (peste 64 de pattern-uri, un pattern peste 128 de caractere, sau care nu e listă) venit dintr-un config editat manual prin \`load_from_config\` îngustează serverul la nimic (\`tools_allow = \[\]\`) și loghează un avertisment (:407-418), pentru că un deny tăiat ar face din nou vizibilă unealta pe care o numea. Filtrele fac round-trip prin \`to_config\`/\`load_from_config\` (:808-809, :852-853). Teste (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py, 13/13 verzi): listare și deny prevalează (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:47-57), unealtă filtrată refuzată la apel pe nume (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:60-70), filtru malformat → nimic și round-trip de config (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:73-84), ruta primește filtre și refuză formele malformate (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:98-109), ruta refuză 129 de caractere / 65 de intrări pe ambele câmpuri (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:121-129) și acceptă un filtru exact la limită (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:132-138), un deny imposibil de păstrat ascunde toate uneltele cu avertisment (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:141-147), iar un config editat manual cu deny peste limite se încarcă fail-closed fără să atingă serverul vecin (tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py:150-159). Resursele/prompts sunt urmărite separat în H552. [d1](../agents/core/mcp/client.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/mcp.py) [d3](../tests/test_mcp_tool_filters.py) |

| <a id="h557"></a>H557 | Make a third-party tool schema safe and unambiguous for every model provider | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | No provider-agnostic repair of a third-party MCP server's JSON Schema exists (re-verified at HEAD 2026-09-22, line numbers re-mapped 2026-09-23 after the fail-closed tool-filter bounds change, which touches none of this path; #1179 changed nothing on this path either — its client.py hunks are the stdio \`cwd_secret_files\` disclosure at :17-36, :58, :211-242 and :560-571; the only change since the 2026-09-07 stamping that bears on this row is #1152 adding a fifth unrepaired provider path, below). (1) \`agents/core/mcp/client.py:584-601\` \`_list_tools\` still stores \`inputSchema\` verbatim (\`:592\`) into \`MCPTool.input_schema\` (\`:357\`); add a \`_normalize_mcp_input_schema\` applied at registration. (2) Two of Hermes' five repairs are absent on every path, verified by running the code: draft-07 \`definitions\`/\`#/definitions/...\` are never promoted to \`$defs\`/\`#/$defs/...\` (and \`gemini_schema\` silently reduces a \`$ref\` property to \`{}\`, deleting the shape), and a Pydantic nullable union \`anyOf:\[{...},{"type":"null"}\], default:null\` is never collapsed to its non-null branch (\`gemini_schema\` yields \`anyOf:\[{"type":"string"},{}\]\` with \`default:null\` retained on the Gemini path; the other four paths ship the raw nullable union untouched). (3) The three repairs that do exist (\`required\` pruned to declared names \`:424-428\`, missing \`type\` coerced \`:421\`, empty-OBJECT avoided \`:447-449\`) live only in \`agents/core/llm/tool_dialects.py:369-451\` and therefore apply to Gemini alone; the other four serialization paths pass \`input_schema\` through unrepaired: \`anthropic_tools\` (tool_dialects.py:147-151), \`ToolSpec.as_openai\` (tool_protocol.py:19-27; used by base.py:556, the Ollama path base.py:811 and openrouter.py:95), the OpenAI Responses payload (responses.py:72-81) and the xAI Responses payload (xai.py:79-80, whose \`_payload\` overrides responses.py's, added by #1152 on 2026-09-15). (4) No name sanitization or provider-legal prefixing: \`MCPTool.qualified_name\` (client.py:367-370) is \`server/tool\`, whose \`/\` is outside the \`\[A-Za-z0-9_-\]\` function-name charset both Anthropic and OpenAI enforce, and nothing replaces characters outside \`\[A-Za-z0-9_\]\`; \`grep -rn "mcp__" agents/\` returns nothing. (5) The row's security property — a third-party tool shadowing a built-in governed tool name — is unaddressed: \`_resolve\` (client.py:748-777) disambiguates only among \`self.servers\` (:768), never against the agent tool registry. (6) No test covers any of this; \`grep -rln "gemini_schema" tests/\` returns only tests/test_cloud_tool_turns.py, and neither test that constructs \`MCPTool\` (tests/test_mcp_hardening.py, tests/test_mcp_trust.py) asserts on \`input_schema\`. All of the above is currently unreachable by a model because no MCP-client -\> tool-registry bridge exists (\`MCPManager.get_tools()\`, client.py:742-746, has zero callers repo-wide), so the normalizer plus its tests should land inside whichever PR first offers MCP client tools to the model, not as a standalone pure function — and that normalizer must sit at registration, ahead of all five dialect serializers, not inside \`gemini_schema\`. | <a id="h575"></a>H575 | Read the product's own per-feature manual (published docs site) | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h576"></a>H576 | Read a PDF / Office document / notebook as text from inside a tool call | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | Formats stop at .pdf and .docx: no .ipynb, .xlsx, .doc, .ppt, .odt, .rtf or .epub, and the text comes back plain rather than converted to Markdown. There is no size refusal for documents (Hermes refuses over 50 MB): the whole file is parsed again on every page request, and max_bytes bounds only the text returned. There is no scanned-page coverage warning naming the page ranges that yielded no text, which the row calls the clever half. Paging is by byte offset, not a line offset/limit window. A missing parser is named (\`parser_missing\`) rather than installed lazily. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../tests/test_file_search.py) [d3](../tests/test_file_read_documents.py) |

| <a id="h579"></a>H579 | Pull a file or slice of a file into a message inline (@ context references) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The owner's own chat expands @file:path and @file:path#L10-40 inline. agents/core/context_refs.py holds the rules. expand (agents/core/context_refs.py:129) finds every reference that starts a word (agents/core/context_refs.py:42), including a quoted path, #L7 for one line and #L2-L3, and not trailing punctuation. It keeps the message as typed and appends each file's text under "--- Attached Context ---" in a fence labelled as attached file content, not instructions. Each path is resolved through the same FileScope as file_read (_attach_one, agents/core/context_refs.py:78), so outside the file roots, .. traversal, a symlink escape and a secret-looking path such as .env are refused. A missing file, a directory, a binary file (a NUL in its first 8 KB), a bad line range, a range past the end and an unreadable file each become a one-line warning in the same section instead of failing the message. With no file roots configured, the message says nothing was attached. The limits are 8 references per message, 64 KB per reference and 128 KB in all, counted in bytes. A cut reference says so, and a repeated one is attached once. A line range reads only the lines it needs; invalid UTF-8 is read with replacement characters. The expansion runs on /chat and /chat/stream (agents/web.py:1232, agents/web.py:1392), off the event loop, so both the HUD and nerva chat get it. It never runs for a channel message, as Hermes' gateway does not expand either. A message that attached anything marks its turn: the route binds the marker (agents/web.py:1242, and inside the stream task at agents/web.py:1307). handle_input and handle_input_stream then call _taint_attached_context (agents/core/orchestrator.py:332, :1860, :2049), which raises the turn's action origin to recall:untrusted. An action planned from attached content therefore escalates GRANT to QUEUE at the kernel, while an inbound turn keeps its more specific origin. complete (agents/core/context_refs.py:190) lists in-scope completions for what follows @file:, with directories ending in /. It shows dot files only when asked and never secret paths or links out of the roots, is bounded to 50 entries, and returns nothing for a prefix over 1,024 characters. GET /api/context-refs (agents/core/routers/commands.py:63, user-guarded, read-only) serves it and answers 503 no_file_roots without roots. The HUD composer (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:222, :271, frontend/src/context-refs.tsx) lists completions above the input while an @file: reference is being typed; Tab or a click takes one. tests/test_h579_context_refs.py (44 cases) covers the bounds, parsing, whole files, line ranges (single, L-form, past the end), every refusal and warning, a symlink, punctuation, word starts, dedupe, the byte cut, the shared budget, the reference limit, an unreadable file, invalid UTF-8, no roots, an explicit scope and the marker. It also covers completion (prefixes, dot files, secrets, roots, a long prefix, a file or missing folder, the bound), both routes, the completion route, the turn taint on both paths, and an inbound turn. frontend/src/test/context-refs.test.tsx has 3 cases. There are 53 mutants: 49 killed and 4 equivalent. Two of them bound memory only (reading no more than the budget); the other two are a fast path and a context reset that the request's own context already scopes. [d1](../agents/core/context_refs.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |

| <a id="h583"></a>H583 | Steer which upstream provider serves an OpenRouter request (provider routing) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | OpenRouter provider routing ships, and the owner's changes reach the live backend on the next request. Six llm rows are seeded right after compatible_provider (agents/core/settings_db.py:161-166): openrouter_sort, openrouter_only, openrouter_ignore, openrouter_order, openrouter_require_parameters, and openrouter_data_collection, seeded deny. The HUD PUT /api/admin/settings/llm and \`nerva config set\` both validate through settings_db.validate_category, and the three slug lists share one slug check (agents/core/settings_db.py:770 -\> agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py:97). build_provider_block (agents/core/llm/provider_routing.py:110) turns the knobs into OpenRouter's \`provider\` object. It lower-cases and de-duplicates slugs, caps each list at 32, sends require_parameters only as a literal true, omits empty knobs, and raises on an invalid value instead of dropping it. HybridRouter.detect hands OpenRouterBackend a reader over the rows, not a snapshot (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:395). Before every text or tool-turn request the backend re-reads and re-validates them off the event loop (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:94, called at agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:163 and agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:201) and puts the object in the body (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:124). A HUD change, or a \`nerva config set\` run from another process against the same settings DB, therefore governs the next request, so the CLI's '(a running hub picks it up within 30 s)' holds for these rows. The object goes only to the openrouter profile, never to openai-compatible (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:70). Fail-closed limits: a stored value that decodes but does not validate refuses to build the backend at detect (agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py:398, logged). After detect it refuses that request: '\[OpenRouter error\]', nothing sent, and an error log naming llm.openrouter_\* (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:107-111). The next request flows once the row is fixed. Known limit: a row that cannot be read or decoded (a sqlite error, or malformed JSON written around the API) is swallowed by settings_db.get_value into that knob's seeded default with no log line. Such a request keeps data_collection deny but loses only/ignore/order/sort/require_parameters. A 400/404 to a request that carried the object logs OpenRouter's own message (log-safe, 200 chars), the object sent and the settings to loosen (agents/core/llm/openrouter.py:128), so the seeded deny does not fail opaquely after an upgrade. Coverage is tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py (30 tests). It checks the exact body on the tool-turn and text paths (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:78, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:93), and on the wire through the real egress client with a MockTransport injected through its public transport kwarg (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:135). It checks that no object is sent with nothing set or for openai-compatible (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:102, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:114), and that bad values are refused (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:174, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:180). It covers the rows, the deny default, write validation and the CLI entry point (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:192, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:206, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:223), and router wiring with a stubbed read (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:274, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:287, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:300). Three router tests run on a real temporary settings DB: rows -\> detect -\> body (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:346); a change after detect reaching the next request, tightening and loosening (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:358); and a tampered row after detect refusing the request, then healing (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:384). Two more cover the policy-404 hint and its absence without an object or on a 401 (tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:406, tests/test_openrouter_provider_routing.py:424). Nerva has no Nous Portal provider, so the knobs apply to OpenRouter only. | <a id="h586"></a>H586 | Paste or attach a screenshot into the conversation (vision & image paste) | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Both halves of the accepted adaptation are delivered: the HUD composer and the terminal. useComposerImages (frontend/src/composer-images.tsx) takes image paste and drag-drop (frontend/src/composer-images.tsx:42-43) plus a file chooser. It accepts at most eight PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP images of up to 4 MiB each, and the shared InputBar wires it in (frontend/src/cockpit.tsx:240). App.submit sends an image draft to runVision (frontend/src/app.tsx:397, frontend/src/app.tsx:372). runVision posts to the user-guarded POST /api/vlm/composer/describe (agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:216, registered at agents/web.py:1522). The route checks data-URI signatures and bounds raster decoding (validate_raster, agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:53). It binds the request to the reviewed destination/model revision and refuses a non-local VLM unless the owner has acknowledged it (agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:237). It then calls VLMBackend.generate_vision_checked (agents/core/llm/vlm.py:357). Images reach only the configured VLM, never the text chat model. The taint governance holds through isolation rather than a taint flag. The vision answer is a client-only transcript entry, and ordinary turns post only {message, agent} to /chat/stream (frontend/src/app.tsx:323), so no tool plan can be derived from image content. tests/test_composer_vision.py (27 passed) covers validation, auth, binding, the remote acknowledgement and error shapes. Intake and the vision turn are covered by frontend/src/test/composer-images.test.tsx, frontend/src/test/composer-vision-turn.test.tsx and frontend/e2e/composer-images.spec.ts, (the two vitest files, 10 cases, pass; the Playwright spec was not run here). The terminal: \`nerva chat --image PATH\` (repeatable, up to eight) and \`--clipboard-image\` (agents/cli/nerva.py:300) turn the chat verb into the same vision turn (_vision_turn, agents/cli/nerva.py:2223), dispatched before any /chat body is built (agents/cli/nerva.py:1875). Each file is opened once without blocking, must be a regular file, is read up to 4 MiB and sent by the type its signature says, never its name (_image_mime, agents/cli/nerva.py:1966; _read_image, agents/cli/nerva.py:1976). The clipboard is read by the platform's own reader with a fixed argv, no shell, its output read no further than the bound against a 10 s deadline, in a session of its own whose whole process group is killed at the deadline, past the bound or on an interrupt, so a descendant holding the pipe cannot stretch it (_run_reader, agents/cli/nerva.py:2076): wl-paste on Wayland, xclip on X11, pngpaste or else osascript on macOS, Windows PowerShell from the system directory, else the first powershell.exe or pwsh.exe in an absolute PATH entry that is not the current directory (never shutil.which, which puts the current directory back on Windows), on Windows, and through interop under WSL (the /mnt/c default, else the PATH's powershell.exe) (_clipboard_command, agents/cli/nerva.py:2051; _clipboard_image, agents/cli/nerva.py:2161). The verb reads GET /api/vlm/composer/status, sends that destination and binding with the images, so a model changed in between is refused (409), and a destination off the hub's machine is used only when --remote-vision names it, the terminal's form of the HUD's per-destination acknowledgement (agents/cli/nerva.py:2286). The describe call waits up to 240 s (the hub gives the model 180 s), and a timeout after the status answered is a failed turn, not a missing hub; the reply is checked as the HUD checks it (ok, a string answer of at most 128 KiB); a status without its destination, binding or local sends nothing. The --remote-vision address is compared as the hub names it (scheme and host in any case, the default port, a trailing slash); one with a user name, query or fragment is refused as a look-alike, and --remote-vision without an image is refused. A body the route refuses is answered with its first reason as {error, reason}, never FastAPI's default 422 that echoed the images back (_BoundedValidationRoute, agents/core/routers/composer_vision.py:22), and the CLI prints it, with the field a validation reason names; hub text is filtered before it reaches the terminal. --agent, --session and --reasoning are refused with images: the turn goes to the vision model only, never to /chat, an agent or a session. -z, --json and --usage-file keep their meaning. tests/test_nerva_chat_image.py (30 cases) covers the route taken, the real route answering the CLI, a model changed in between, each raster kind, the remote acknowledgement, the refusals before any request, the hub's answers and the clipboard readers; its 20 mutants are all caught. The first adversarial review (0 major, 6 minor, 9 nits) found the 30 s timeout reported as no hub, the clipboard buffered whole, the route's refusal without its reason, PowerShell resolved from the current directory, 16 surviving mutants of its own, and macOS/WSL gaps; all are fixed, and tests/test_h586b_image_review.py (50 cases) pins them, its 35 mutants all caught. Test manual: CHT-115, CHT-116. The second review (0 major, 4 minor, 8 nits) found an oversized clipboard image reported as empty, the deadline stretched by a descendant, the Windows fallback still reaching the current directory through shutil.which, and 8 real survivors; all are fixed, tests/test_h586c_image_review.py (23 cases, real processes for the deadline, the group kill, the exit code and the bound) pins them, and its 16 mutants are all caught but one equivalent. Known limits: the receipt's model stays null for an image turn, and the route's own 403 (no acknowledgement) is told apart from an auth 403 by its message, a case the binding already makes unreachable. | <a id="h594"></a>H594 | Have the agent pick up a project's conventions automatically (context files) | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva reads a project's convention files into the turn, as Hermes reads its context files and subdirectory hints. agents/core/project_context.py walks from the nearest git root (never above the file root) down to the working directory, the first JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS root, and reads AGENTS.md, CLAUDE.md, .cursorrules and .cursor/rules/\*.mdc in each directory in that order (CONVENTION_NAMES, candidates). A directory that file_read, file_list, file_search or a local terminal_run touches is noted for the session (note_tool_path, bounded to 8 directories and 256 sessions); its chain is read on every later turn, and the tool's own result carries the files the turn had not seen yet, marked tainted so the loop fences it (attach, attach_terminal; agents/core/file_tools.py _with_project_context; agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py _rpc_terminal_run). A project SOUL.md, HEARTBEAT.md or IDENTITY.md is never read, not even through a symlink (HUB_FILES, _admit). Every file is resolved through FileScope (outside the roots, symlink escape and secret paths refused), must be a regular file (a FIFO is never opened), and is bounded to 20 KB, with 40 KB and 12 files a turn; a cut says how much was shown and a file past the budget is named (load_file, collect, render). Each file is scanned with detect_injection_normalized and a flagged one becomes a \[BLOCKED ...\] line with none of its text. The caveated block sits after the core-memory block in _build_agent_turn_text; _begin_project_context builds it off the event loop at turn start in both turn paths and marks the turn recall-tainted when any file was given, so an action planned from it escalates GRANT to QUEUE. It is off with llm.project_context_files and in safe mode (layer project_context, named on the HUD banner). tests/test_h594_project_context.py: 47 cases; 51 mutants, all killed. [d1](../agents/core/project_context.py) [d2](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d3](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) |
| <a id="h595"></a>H595 | Let the model write a script that calls tools without a round trip per call (execute_code) | keep | Parțial | Reevaluat | Generic SSH/Modal provisioning remains unsupported; the validated Docker transport accepts local Unix endpoints. Deliberate native-fd bypass output is not a redirected Python stream. Stream spills are capped at 5,000,000 bytes per stream: later bytes are counted but not kept, so only the inline tail shows a runaway stream's end. Hermes project\|strict mode remains intentionally represented by Nerva tool profiles (agent x surface x principal), not copied as a separate switch. [d1](../agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py) [d2](../agents/core/memory/session_search.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) |

No OAuth credential type exists, and a 401 has no refresh-then-rotate step. Keys resolve only from os.environ in from_env; VaultResolver (agents/core/secrets_vault.py:16) is never consulted for LLM keys. The owner also cannot see partial degradation. AuthProfilesPanel shows one healthy/failing tag per provider, derived from the pool-level healthy count, so a pool with some keys cooling still reads green. The API returns per-profile cooldown, but the panel does not render it. [d1](../agents/core/llm/auth_rotation.py) [d2](../agents/core/llm/hybrid_router.py) [d3](../agents/core/llm/anthropic.py) |

## delta

| ID | Capabilitate | Decizie inițială | Stare cod | Bază | Observație / restanță |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| <a id="h656"></a>H656 | A group chat between several agents that keeps running when the client that opened it is closed | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h657"></a>H657 | A surviving host continues a room whose owner-host died, and fences the old host if it comes back | skip | Exclus intenționat | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h658"></a>H658 | A member of a shared session that lives on a different machine, running under a capability the target machine issued and can revoke | update | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h659"></a>H659 | A retried external request returns the original run instead of starting a second one | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The three external intakes now honour an Idempotency-Key, so a retried request returns the original run instead of starting a second one. agents/core/idempotency.py holds the rules. parse_key (agents/core/idempotency.py:67) accepts 1-255 visible ASCII characters and refuses anything else with HTTP 400 invalid_idempotency_key. check_header (agents/core/idempotency.py:257) runs first on every route, before the body is read or the caller is checked. IdempotencyStore (agents/core/idempotency.py:92) keeps a SQLite row whose primary key is (scope, key), in the data folder (idempotency.db). reserve (agents/core/idempotency.py:131) runs inside BEGIN IMMEDIATE, so two workers or processes on the same file cannot both win (8 racing threads get exactly one reservation), and a reservation survives a restart. A row holds only the fingerprint (SHA-256 of method, path and body, each length-prefixed; agents/core/idempotency.py:76) and the public reference of what the request started, capped at 2 KB. It never holds the body, a reply or a credential. A retry with the same fingerprint returns that reference with Idempotent-Replayed: true, and nothing runs again. The same key with a different request gets 409 idempotency_key_reused. A retry while the first attempt is still running gets 409 idempotency_in_progress with Retry-After. A first attempt that raised or answered 5xx releases the key, so its retry runs. An attempt abandoned by a dead process is taken over after 600 s. Rows expire after 24 h. When the store cannot be written, a keyed request is refused with 503 idempotency_unavailable rather than run without its guarantee. durable (agents/core/idempotency.py:113) says whether the store is a file rather than :memory:. The scope is the authenticated caller, and the key is reserved only after authentication and before anything is queued or run, so a duplicate never becomes a second approval item, kernel task or audit row. POST /api/actions/request (agents/core/routers/actions.py:63) reserves in the owner's action scope after the body is validated. A replay returns the queued action, or its id if the queue no longer holds it. POST /api/a2a/task (agents/core/routers/a2a.py:72-73) reserves in the scope a2a:\<peer\> only after the peer's signature verifies. A forged retry is refused with 401 and never reads the peer's receipt, and a refused task releases its key. The stored receipt is id, status and accepted. POST /api/webhooks/{id} (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:551) reserves in the scope webhook:\<id\> after the token or signature, the receiver switch and the enabled check, and before the delivery is counted, run or sent. _run_delivery (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:581) is the previous delivery body, unchanged. _public_outcome (agents/core/routers/webhooks.py:570) keeps only ok, target, skipped and error; a replay answers those with the original status code, never the reply, steps or delivery. tests/test_h659_idempotency.py (70 cases) covers the key grammar, the fingerprint, and every store state: TTL, stale take-over, restart, two workers, a thread race, an in-memory store, the reference cap, an unreadable reference, and rollback on a file and in memory. It also covers the begin/claim mapping and all three routes: replay, conflict, no key, a malformed key checked first, an invalid body or unauthenticated caller not burning a key, a failed attempt released, scopes per hook and per peer, a skipped delivery replayed, and a forged A2A retry. There are 57 mutants, all killed. [d1](../agents/core/idempotency.py) [d2](../agents/core/routers/actions.py) [d3](../agents/core/routers/a2a.py) |
| <a id="h660"></a>H660 | Code the agent runs keeps its variables, imports and loaded data between calls | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | Generic SSH, Modal and remote TLS provisioning remain unsupported and require an explicitly isolated governed transport design. Docker selection currently supports local Unix endpoints. Correlation tokens/nonces reject stale frames but cannot attest output against Python introspection inside the same worker; authorization is enforced by the host broker. Spills cover redirected Python stdout/stderr up to 5,000,000 bytes per stream; deliberate native-fd bypasses and the part of a runaway stream past that ceiling are not kept on disk. Production deployment evidence is separate from these real-container tests. [d1](../agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py) [d2](../agents/core/memory/session_search.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py) |
| <a id="h661"></a>H661 | Oversized tool output is paged from a file instead of being thrown away | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Oversized output is paged back from its spill file instead of being re-run. execute_code stdout/stderr are spooled to disk as they arrive (agents/core/code_tools.py:480, agents/core/tool_result_store.py:431) under \<data root\>/workspace/tool_results. Each stream is capped at DEFAULT_MAX_STREAM_BYTES = 5,000,000 (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:130): StreamSpill.write stops writing at the cap but keeps counting (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:600), close appends one '\[... spill capped at N bytes: M more bytes ...\]' marker (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:642), and the result carries \<stream\>_spill_capped and \<stream\>_kept_bytes (agents/core/code_tools.py:274-275). Head and tail stay inline with omitted-byte accounting (agents/core/environments/output_limits.py:42, tests/test_environment_output_limits.py:38). A whole result the loop spills (agents/core/agent_runtime.py:1404) keeps both ends in its preview (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:705-706, tests/test_tool_loop_result_spill.py:139), and file_read itself is never spilled (tests/test_tool_loop_result_spill.py:287). Every notice carries the exact call from page_recipe (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:145): file_read(path="\<resolved path\>", offset=0, max_bytes=N). The same arguments are returned as \<stream\>_read_with (agents/core/code_tools.py:272) and read_with (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:736), and stream recipes propose pages of at most 8,000 bytes (agents/core/code_tools.py:226). file_read takes an integer offset from 0 to MAX_OFFSET = 2\*\*53-1 (agents/core/file_tools.py:123, agents/core/file_tools.py:1511). Anything else is refused as bad_offset, both in the preflight and in a direct call (agents/core/file_tools.py:1430, agents/core/file_tools.py:740). An offset at or past the end is an empty final page, answered without seeking (agents/core/file_tools.py:758). A page that stops early returns next_offset (agents/core/file_tools.py:1285) and never ends inside a UTF-8 character (agents/core/file_tools.py:1237). A .pdf/.docx pages through its extracted text (agents/core/file_tools.py:1289). The named call is one file_read answers in every live setup, including the three the review found broken. The recipe path is resolved (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:207). A spill is never named like a secret (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:190). file_read alone gets a read-only door for exact spill-file names in the coordinator's spill directory (agents/core/file_tools.py:648, agents/core/file_tools.py:685, agents/core/file_tools.py:1202), wired at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:819. So JARVIS_FILE_ROOTS pointing elsewhere, a symlinked JARVIS_HOME or a bridged tool with a secret-looking name no longer strands the file (tests/test_spill_paging.py:507, tests/test_spill_paging.py:535); the door's limits are pinned by tests/test_spill_paging.py:414. The recipe is named only where that call is answered. ToolResultStore.readable(path) (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:361) asks the coordinator probe (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:823). The probe requires four things: file_read registered, allowed by the job toolset, in this turn's profile offer (noted at agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:977), and able to open this very path (agents/core/autonomy_coordinator.py:835, agents/core/file_tools.py:713). execute_code also requires file_read in its own run's offer (agents/core/code_tools.py:462, agents/core/code_tools.py:559). Otherwise the notice says the file is kept for the owner and cannot be paged from this turn (tests/test_spill_paging.py:545, tests/test_spill_paging.py:589, tests/test_code_tools.py:866), and concurrent turns each see their own offer (tests/test_spill_paging.py:627). A failed spill just omits the path (agents/core/code_tools.py:258-259, tests/test_code_tools.py:698). Retention is unchanged (agents/core/tool_result_store.py:490, tests/test_tool_result_store.py:214, tests/test_tool_result_store.py:353). End to end, a real AgentToolRuntime follows read_with and then next_offset through the real file_read and rebuilds the spilled result byte for byte, while the producing tool runs once (tests/test_spill_paging.py:719). execute_code stdout does the same on the one-shot and session paths (tests/test_code_tools.py:824), and a runaway stream stops at its ceiling with one marker (tests/test_code_tools.py:877, tests/test_spill_paging.py:233). Adaptations: offsets are bytes, not Hermes' lines. The 5,000,000-byte cap covers streamed spills, which is Hermes' execute_code scope; whole-result JSON spills stay bounded by the 256 MB retention sweep. A spill stays third-party when it comes back: every file directly in a spill directory counts as one (agents/core/file_tools.py:699), a file_read page of it and a file_search whose matches reach it declare tainted (agents/core/file_tools.py:765, agents/core/file_tools.py:917), and the loop fences the result as DATA and raises the turn's recall taint, in the turn that spilled it or any later one (tests/test_spill_paging.py:761, tests/test_spill_paging.py:812, tests/test_spill_paging.py:834, tests/test_spill_paging.py:856). A trusted tool's spill counts too, because a spill's file name cannot say reliably which tool wrote it. [d1](../agents/core/file_tools.py) [d2](../agents/core/tool_result_store.py) [d3](../agents/core/code_tools.py) |

| <a id="h666"></a>H666 | The agent's own task list nests subtasks under a parent | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built in #1207 and fixed after one adversarial review (review-H666: no major; five minors and eight nits, all fixed). Hermes nests the agent's subtasks with one optional parent on each todo item (the id of another item) instead of a nested array, so merge-by-id keeps working, and draws the list through one defensive tree builder. Nerva now does both, for the todo tool's items and for mission plan steps. The tree builder: - agents/core/todo_tree.py tree() (agents/core/todo_tree.py:48) returns (item, depth) pairs in depth-first order, children in list order under their parent, with every item exactly once. It is stdlib only, because the CLI imports it. - Depth is capped at MAX_DEPTH = 4 (agents/core/todo_tree.py:30); a deeper item is drawn at depth 4, under its parent. - An item whose parent is missing, itself, or not an id at all is drawn at depth 0 in its place (agents/core/todo_tree.py:65). ref() accepts only text or whole numbers as ids; a bool, a fractional float, a list or a dict is no id, and a whole-valued float is its number, as JSON's 1.0 is 1 to the HUD (agents/core/todo_tree.py:33). When two items share an id, the first holds it. Anything that is not a list is an empty tree. - Cycle members, and anything under them, are reached from no root, so they are appended flat at depth 0 in list order (agents/core/todo_tree.py:78). A getter that raises counts as no parent. The walk uses an explicit stack, so a 5,000-item chain costs no recursion. - The HUD's twin is frontend/src/todo-tree.ts todoTree() (frontend/src/todo-tree.ts:28), with the same rules. Both suites read one shared file of 47 cases, frontend/src/test/todo-tree-cases.json (hand-written shapes plus seeded random ones over text, number, whole and fractional float, bool, null, list and dict ids and parents), and assert the same drawn order and depths. The review's fuzz had found the twins differing on whole-valued floats; that is fixed and pinned. The todo tool: - An item may carry parent: the id of the item it sits under. It is an allowed item field (agents/core/todo_tool.py:84) and is in the schema as a string of at most MAX_ID (agents/core/todo_tool.py:102); the description tells the model how to nest and un-nest. - _clean_parent (agents/core/todo_tool.py:197) cleans it like an id. Empty or whitespace-only text means no parent, as Hermes clears it. Anything else that is not an id is refused as todo_bad_parent, with a sentence the model can act on, and changes nothing. - A parent need not name an item in the list: a dangling, self or cyclic parent is stored and drawn at the top, never refused and never lost. - On a merge, parent null means not sent (as for content and status), and "" clears it. A changed parent counts as a write, so the plan cap applies. A parent moved by an untrusted turn taints the item, as a status does. Re-sending the same parent changes nothing: no taint, and the plan's labels stay (agents/core/todo_tool.py:400). - model_items answers the parent only when it is set (agents/core/todo_tool.py:227), so a flat list answers exactly as before and plan_bytes counts a parent's bytes. Mission plan steps: - MissionStore.create (agents/core/autonomy/missions.py:203) gives every step a nullable parent. _plan_step (agents/core/autonomy/missions.py:390) reads each entry. - A title, or any other value, is a top-level step with its own text, as before; a mapping without title or parent is still stringified. - {title, parent?} may sit under an earlier step: parent is that step's index, and only an earlier one. A plan is therefore a forest by construction. - Anything else is refused as bad_plan_step: a self, later, out-of-range, negative, bool, text or float parent, a missing or blank title, or an unknown key. - POST /api/missions (agents/core/routers/missions.py:50) accepts such a plan. A bad step answers 400 with one fixed sentence and code bad_plan_step (agents/core/routers/missions.py:73); no request data reaches the body. - The build-queue plan kept out-of-range and self parents on mission steps and let the tree degrade them. The store refuses them instead: a mission plan is written once, by its creator, so a bad parent there is a caller's mistake worth a 400. The tree still degrades any step the store did not check, such as a row written before this change, which has no parent key. A legacy entry {title, \<other key\>} is now refused too; nothing in the repo sends one. - finish_step budget charging and the completion gates are unchanged. A child step is charged like any step, closing a child does not close its parent, and exhausting the budget still auto-fails the mission. Rendering: - nerva todo draws the tree through todo_tree, indented two spaces a level (agents/cli/nerva.py:1556). - The Decision Inbox's plans in flight (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx) orders items through todoTree and indents each row 14 px a level, with data-depth (frontend/src/panels/plans.tsx:78). ITEMS_SHOWN counts rows in tree order. - MissionCanvasPanel draws plan steps through todoTree, the tree keyed by each step's idx (frontend/src/panels/mission-canvas.tsx:248). The finish controls name the step's own idx, not its drawn position: the test clicks done in a nested row drawn third and asserts it posts that step's idx. Tests: - tests/test_h666_subtasks.py (39 cases): - the tree: DFS order, a child listed before its parent, the depth cap, dangling, self and cyclic parents, non-id parents, duplicate ids, a getter that raises, and a long chain; - todo parents: storage, merge, clearing, cleaning, refusal, the cap, labels, taint, the schema and the tool answer; - mission step parents in the store and through POST /api/missions, with the refusal cases, budget charging on a child step, and nerva todo indentation. - Vitest: frontend/src/test/todo-tree.test.ts (9, including the shared cases), plus one case each in the plans-in-flight and mission-canvas suites (indentation, dangling or cyclic parents kept in view, and the finish control's idx). - 43 mutants of the new guards were all killed (four survivors of the first run were killed by added cases). One unreachable guard, a placed-twice check in the walk, was removed. The review's 51 mutants left three survivors: two (a boolean read as an id in the TS twin, a finish control addressing its drawn position) are now killed, and the third (the tree keyed by position) is equivalent, since a step's idx is its position. Six mutants of the fix round's guards were all killed. [d1](../agents/core/todo_tree.py) [d2](../agents/core/todo_tool.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy/missions.py) |
| <a id="h667"></a>H667 | Never assume /tmp is real storage; prune only the cache you own | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Code execution no longer keeps its files on the system temp root. Where a Sandbox built without an explicit work_dir makes its run directory, first match wins: JARVIS_EXEC_TEMP_DIR in the process environment, then the owner's security.sandbox_temp_dir setting, then the managed cache \<data root\>/cache/exec (resolve_root, agents/core/exec_cache.py:149). A value that is not an absolute path, or cannot be read as one (a relative path, ~nosuchuser, a NUL byte), is skipped with a warning (_as_root, agents/core/exec_cache.py:117), and the setting refuses such a value on write, as it refuses an age outside 1-8760 hours (agents/core/settings_db.py:734); a choice that names the managed cache, however spelled (through a link or a '..'), is the managed cache, compared by identity (_same_dir, agents/core/exec_cache.py:138). Each run directory is an absolute private mkdtemp (mode 0700, named nerva-sandbox-\*) under a root made 0700 when this user owns it; a root that cannot be used gives the system temp directory for that run, with a warning, unmanaged and never pruned (new_work_dir, agents/core/exec_cache.py:177). The Sandbox takes it at construction (agents/core/sandbox.py:85); the orchestrator's one production sandbox (agents/core/orchestrator.py:610) passes no work_dir, so it gets the managed cache. A run directory removed under a live sandbox, or there without its lock or wider than 0700, is made again 0700 and its lock taken again before the next run, and the tool-RPC runtime asks for that before it makes its mailbox inside the directory (agents/core/tool_rpc_runtime.py:381) (ensure_work_dir, agents/core/sandbox.py:100). Docker bind-mounts it with --mount, CSV-quoted, so a ':', ',', '"' or line break in the data root is data (_bind_mount, agents/core/sandbox.py:29). A managed run directory's lock lives outside it, in \<root\>/.locks/\<name\>.lock, where code run in the directory cannot remove, replace or forge it: an flock held for the sandbox's lifetime, recording host, pid, whether the flock was taken, the kernel's boot id and the pid's start time, written under a private temporary name and renamed into place so it is never seen empty and a link planted at its name is never written through, in a .locks directory that is private and not a link, and released (closed, then removed, as Windows needs) when the sandbox is gone (release, agents/core/exec_cache.py:312; hold, agents/core/exec_cache.py:257). An hourly job (schedule_exec_cache_prune, agents/core/scheduler_service.py:229; run_exec_cache_prune, agents/core/scheduler_service.py:595) prunes the managed cache whatever root is chosen now, off the event loop, never raising into the scheduler (prune, agents/core/exec_cache.py:499). Each run directory is aged as one group: the newest date anywhere inside it, links never followed, dates the whole directory (_newest, agents/core/exec_cache.py:327), a file being dated by the older of its mtime and ctime so a date set in the future never keeps it forever (_date, agents/core/exec_cache.py:323). A directory is removed only when that is older than security.sandbox_temp_max_age_hours (72 by default, never under an hour; max_age_hours, agents/core/exec_cache.py:596), it is not the hub's own live sandbox, and its lock is not held: on the kernel that wrote it (same boot id), a record whose writer took the flock is held exactly while the flock is, so a crashed sandbox whose pid came back (the hub is pid 1 in a container) is free; a record written without a flock is held while its pid is alive with the same start time, asked of the process table on Windows (OpenProcess and GetExitCodeProcess, never os.kill, whose signal 0 is a console Ctrl+C there) (_pid_alive, agents/core/exec_cache.py:390); only a busy flock reads as held, and any other flock error (a filesystem that refuses flock) reads the record; a record from before a reboot of this host, or from before the flag existed, is free when the probe gets the flock; a pid is read only in the pid namespace that numbered it; another host's record, another namespace's, a record whose flock the probe could not test and one with a pid no hold writes are kept until the directory is ten times the age limit old; a lock that cannot be judged is kept and logged and the sweep goes on (_held_safely); a lock that cannot be opened reads as held, and anything but a regular file is no lock (_held, agents/core/exec_cache.py:407). It is claimed by a rename before it is deleted, so a directory that cannot be claimed is kept whole; one swapped for a link after the checks is only unlinked and not counted; an unfinished claim is finished by the next sweep, and a lock whose directory is gone is removed (another host's once the lock is ten times the limit old). Only nerva-sandbox-\* entries are considered, in a managed root that is a real directory (not a link or a Windows junction) owned by this user and not writable by others; otherwise the sweep is reported failed to the scheduler, as is an unreadable root (_root_problem, agents/core/exec_cache.py:477). A root the owner chose is never bulk-deleted. Backups leave out the cache and, directly under a root the owner chose inside the data root, the run directories exactly as mkdtemp names them; an owner folder that merely starts with the prefix is kept (agents/core/backup.py:405), and \`nerva config set security.sandbox_temp_dir\` says it needs a restart. tests/test_exec_cache.py (60 cases) covers the resolution order and the refused values, the relative data root, the sandbox's own directory, its mode and its lock outside it, the owner record without flock, another host, locks that are not files, the probe's descriptors, a sandbox that is gone, a directory removed under a live sandbox (host and Docker paths), group aging by the newest date and by the directory itself, a future date, the age setting and its floor, the live list, the owner-root exclusion, foreign entries and links, a root that is a link or writable by others, a directory swapped for a link, a claim that cannot be made or was left unfinished, orphan locks, an unreadable root, the hourly job (the owner's age, off the loop, never raising, the managed cache while a root is chosen), its registration, the backup and the restart note; tests/test_h667d_exec_cache_review.py (30 cases) covers the third review: refused flocks (ENOLCK, EOPNOTSUPP, EINVAL) and a busy one, owner folders named like the prefix and nested run files in backups, another kernel's record under a free flock, a reboot, a failed exit-code query, a cache that does not exist yet, an owner-root directory made again, a failed replace, a root we do not own, a record from before the flag, another pid namespace, odd pids and a lock that cannot be judged, staged records, an interrupt during hold, a first lock taken later, reparse tags and the label; tests/test_h667c_exec_cache_review.py (55 cases) covers the second review: Windows liveness through a faked process table (no such process, access denied, exited, a reused pid) and a dead sandbox's directory pruned there, the release order, a free flock against a live pid (this process, pid 1, the parent), a renamed host on the same kernel, a flock still held, a record without a flock (alive, reused), another host's record and orphan lock ageing out, the record's fields, the tool-RPC path's re-creation, a directory there without its lock, the managed cache through a link or a '..', control characters, the mount's quoting, the planted link, the private .locks, the backup, a real tree aged with the production date and the record written before the lock has its name. Test manual: PNB-167, PNB-168. The first adversarial review (0 major, 8 minor, 9 nits: a bad setting that stopped the hub's start, a relative data root, backups including the cache, an unreadable cache reported ok, a later choice stranding the cache, lock and mtime games from inside a run directory, no flock on network filesystems, test gaps) is answered in full; its 31 follow-up mutants are all caught but three equivalent ones. The second review (one major: on Windows os.kill(pid, 0) is a console Ctrl+C that never says dead, and an open lock cannot be removed, so the cache was never pruned there; four minors: the record overrode a working flock, the tool-RPC path made the run directory again without its mode and lock, the managed cache spelled another way took no lock, twelve mutant survivors; seven nits) is answered in full; its 36 mutants, all caught but two equivalent ones (a .locks mkdir mode the chmod repairs, samefile behind resolve), five after their cases were added (a probe that took no flock, the start tick's field, a wide .locks, the mailbox made after the repair, a file named like a run directory). The third review (no major; three minors: a filesystem that refuses flock read as busy, backups dropping any folder with the prefix, eight real survivors; eight nits) is answered in full; this round's 18 mutants were all caught but three equivalent ones, three after their cases were added (an odd pid ageing out, another host's pid never read, an owner folder under the chosen root). Not taken: the collision-safe task-id directory naming, which the rationale made conditional on persistent per-task sandboxes, which do not exist (mkdtemp names are unique). TMPDIR/TMP/TEMP still pass through to child processes (agents/core/environments/__init__.py:24-26); the sandbox's own files no longer depend on them. Known limits: Windows liveness is exercised here through a faked process table (the Windows lane runs after merge); on a filesystem whose flock is local to each host (SMB, NFS with local_lock), another host's record is kept by age alone; the settings label and PNB-167 say that JARVIS_EXEC_TEMP_DIR makes a root the owner keeps (never pruned) and that a pruned cache moves with the data root; a live sandbox's own directory is exempt as a whole, not aged file by file; each Sandbox keeps its directory until the prune (there is no close()). [d1](../agents/core/sandbox.py) [d2](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) [d3](../agents/core/environments/__init__.py) |
| <a id="h668"></a>H668 | /plan — turn the current request into a saved implementation plan without implementing anything | copy | Parțial | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h669"></a>H669 | /btw — answer a side question about the running conversation without disturbing it | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h670"></a>H670 | The default identity prompt is a behavior contract, and it lives in one file | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | The shared identity band exists. agents/_identity/IDENTITY.md is one behaviour contract: the reply-sizing rule (match length to the weight of the ask), earned depth, the named prohibitions (no filler, no restating the request, no re-summarizing, no narrating visible tool calls), the anti-sycophancy line ('agree because it is right, not because the owner said it') and 'say when you do not know'. Its reply rules govern the agent's own replies to the owner: text drafted in someone else's voice (an email, a post, a message written as the owner) keeps that voice, greetings and sign-offs included, and a persona's warmth or curiosity lives inside the answer (review-H670 m-5); its honesty rules hold for everything an agent writes, drafted text included (review-H670b m-1). Its leading maintainer note (stripped before the model sees it, _strip_maintainer_note, agents/core/agent.py:453) says never to add 'be targeted and efficient in your exploration', and why. The repo-root SOUL.md mirrors the text, so agents working on the repo read the contract the product ships; a test keeps the two equal. identity_path (agents/core/agent.py:438) resolves it as soul_path_for does a persona: the data home's souls/IDENTITY.local.md, then a gitignored agents/_identity/IDENTITY.local.md, then the shipped file. read_identity (agents/core/agent.py:481) runs it through the same H387 helpers as a SOUL (the injection scan on the uncapped body, then the cap), announces a flagged or truncated contract (its blocked and quarantined stubs name the contract, not a persona), caps it at its own 4,000 characters (IDENTITY_MAX_CHARS, never above the persona's cap, so the two bands stay within the persona cap plus 4,000), and reads it once per settled file signature for the whole process. An override that cannot be read (not UTF-8, a directory, no permission) is reported at ERROR and the shipped contract used instead, cached against the shipped file's signature so an edit of it is seen; a contract that cannot be located or read at all leaves the agent without it; none of these stops an agent from being built. Agent.system_prompt (agents/core/agent.py:572) is the contract, then the agent's persona (either may be empty; an agent without a SOUL still gets the contract). Every place a system prompt is built uses it: Agent.process (agents/core/agent.py:1008) and the synthesis path in agent.py, and the orchestrator's two sites (agents/core/orchestrator.py:2212, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3863) through _system_prompt_of (agents/core/orchestrator.py:451). At a compaction boundary refresh_soul (agents/core/agent.py:730) re-reads the contract as it re-reads the persona (_refresh_identity, agents/core/agent.py:599): a few stats when unchanged; when the file that resolves is absent, vanishes before the read or cannot be read, the contract in force is kept, reported at the boundary as failed-open and warned once per episode (an override absent for an instant resolves to the shipped file for that boundary, as a persona's does); and a moved contract reported as rebuilt, so an edit reaches running agents without a restart. The contract in force at start is committed to the prompt VC (SoulVersionStore) as _identity (_record_identity_version, agents/core/orchestrator.py:433), a no-op when unchanged or empty, so its history is recorded and diffable through /api/admin/prompts/_identity/... beside the personas; what is served is the file (a rollback in the VC does not change it), and an edit picked up at a boundary is recorded at the next start. The prompt-size report counts it as a shared component every call pays (identity_component, agents/core/prompt_size.py:136), and the packaged build ships it through its agents/\*/\*.md glob (overrides, \*.local.md, never ship). IDENTITY.md and its .local overlay are in the H506 instruction-file class (INSTRUCTION_BASE_NAMES, agents/core/file_tools.py:259), so a file write to them asks as an instruction file; a test derives the class from every name the contract's and a persona's loaders can resolve. The data home's README lists souls/IDENTITY.local.md. Persona SOULs and Persona.prompt_block (agents/core/cognition/persona.py:142) stay the character layer on top. tests/test_identity_contract.py (18 cases) covers every rule's presence, the banned line's absence and the kept warning, the mirror, the scan verdict, every active agent's system prompt starting with the contract (and exactly contract then persona), an agent without a persona, the orchestrator's helper and all four sites, both overrides and their order, the scan and the cap on an override, an edit reaching a running agent at the boundary, the one-stat unchanged path, a failed re-read keeping the last-good contract, the version record (once, and a failing store never stopping a start) and one read shared across agents; tests/test_h670b_identity_review.py (21 cases) covers the first review: the instruction-file class, a contract absent or vanishing at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 or unreadable override, the contract's own cap, no empty version, the start-time record, the contract's scope, a mid-file comment, the prompt-size component, the ERROR on a flagged contract, the stubs' wording and CHT-117; tests/test_h670c_identity_review.py (14 cases) covers the second review: honesty unscoped, a shipped contract that stops being UTF-8, a vanish before the real read, the hub's start recording the contract, one warning per episode and a second one, a reader that raises, the fallback following a shipped edit, a directory override, a permission error at load and at a boundary, the fallback rules once when both bands are blocked, and the docs. The first review (one major: the contract was outside the instruction-file class; seven minors: a missing contract emptied at a boundary, a non-UTF-8 override stopped every agent, the two bands exceeded the cap, the VC's rollback and A/B claim, the contract's unscoped rules against drafting personas and trait directives, six untested survivors, CHT-117 naming a retired agent; six nits) is answered; its six real survivors are pinned. The feature's 17 mutants were caught but one equivalent; the first review round's 15 were caught, one only against a stand-in (the vanish case, now driven through the real read). The second review (no major; three minors: the scope lifted the honesty rules from drafted text, a shipped contract that stopped being UTF-8 emptied the contract at a boundary, three real survivors; eight nits) is answered; its 31 mutants' three real survivors are pinned, and this round's 10 were all caught, two after their cases were added (a reader that raises, a second warning episode). Known limits: the prompt-size report measures the shipped contract, not an override in force (as it measures shipped personas); PromptRefresh.text is still the persona alone; the contract's scan verdict is reported only in the log (a persona's also reaches GET /api/agents/{id}/soul); the data home's README is written once, so an existing install does not get its new row. Test manual: CHT-117; docs/ARCHITECTURE.md names the band. [d1](../agents/core/agent.py) [d2](../agents/core/orchestrator.py) [d3](../agents/core/cognition/persona.py) |
| <a id="h671"></a>H671 | A long-lived conversation knows what day it is now | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Durable session birth is resolved through an immutable, generation-checked continuation root. Explicit owner-created child sessions carry bounded retained context and root birth through multiple generations, text/stream/process entrypoints and restart without switching the shared default. SQLite atomically commits child identity, seed, lineage and initial clock; idempotent request receipts resolve retries. Corrupt newer snapshots, reused identities and failed recovery stop dispatch or writes instead of silently replacing context. Accepted history/tool compaction alone advances the durable rebuild clock through compare-and-swap; request snapshots preserve same-day prefix bytes, reject late inherited commits, and add the trusted current-date line only when rebuild and root-birth days differ. Production rendering resolves explicit TZ or existing local IANA discovery and uses historical/current DST rules with an explicit UTC offset. Unknown birth is omitted; invalid or unavailable zone discovery preserves truthful offset-only rendering rather than inventing an IANA name. Independent actual-route, Agent, restart, concurrency, cancellation, failed-resume and observe-only regressions support the full frozen clock contract. This is code-equivalence, not a live-provider claim; ordinary subsequent JSON persistence remains best effort and turn leases are process-local. H672 hangs the persona re-read on the same commit: \`_history_for_prompt\`'s \`publish()\` calls \`_refresh_souls_at_boundary\` after the clock CAS and only when the compressor reported a compression, so a refused \`commit_clock\` migrates nothing and an accepted one is followed by the refresh in that order (\`test_a_refused_clock_commit_migrates_nothing\`, \`test_an_accepted_clock_commit_is_followed_by_the_refresh_in_that_order\`); the re-read neither reads nor writes the clock, so the same-day prefix bytes and the current-date line are untouched by it. #1188's edit to \`orchestrator.py\` is the pairing store's registry path, nothing on this row. [d1](../agents/core/agent.py) [d2](../agents/core/checkpoint.py) [d3](../agents/core/context_compressor.py) |
| <a id="h672"></a>H672 | A never-ending session picks up config, skill and tool changes at the compaction boundary | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | \*\*The boundaries are opt-in, so on a shipped default nothing migrates\*\*: the conversation-level commit exists only with \`memory.context_compression\` on (default off) and the in-turn fold only with \`llm.tool_loop_enabled\` on (default off) — a default install still needs a restart for a persona edit, exactly as before. \*\*Profile revocations are not enforced on the execution path\*\*: \`ToolRPCServer.handle\` checks the job toolset, registration and the gate, never the tool profile, and the executor's gate map is rebuilt only at a fold — so in a loop that never folds (small results, the common shape) a tool the profile withdrew mid-turn stays offered \*and\* executes until the loop ends; pre-existing, unchanged by this slice, pinned as a limit by \`test_between_folds_a_profile_revocation_is_not_enforced_by_the_executor_either\`. \*\*The persona migration is process-wide\*\*: \`orchestrator.agents\` is every agent and \`agent.soul\` is the one dict every session's system prompt reads, so the session that commits migrates the persona for all sessions (the boundary log line says so); refreshing only the routed agents would need the route at \`_history_for_prompt\`, which it does not have. \*\*On the staged shared-route path the turn's budget is computed against the persona in force before the commit\*\* (\`_shared_route_plan\` → \`build()\` measures \`agent.soul\` while planning; the refresh runs in \`accept()\` → \`publish()\`), so a persona that grew is sent that turn with a budget computed for the old size — bounded by \`_soul_max_chars\`, self-correcting next turn; the H363 cached prefix and the H673 usage anchor under-count for one turn after a rebuild the same way. The probe trusts the kernel's signature; what the builder reads outside the file (the scan patterns) is code and needs a restart. The prompt half covers the SOUL only: Nerva's plugin and skills sections live in the user prompt and were already per-turn, so there is no plugin section in the system prompt to fail open — the fail-open is exercised on the SOUL builder. The in-turn boundary does not rebuild \`messages\[0\]\`: a persona change reaches the \*next\* turn, not the loop in flight. "A newly configured MCP server appears without a restart" is only as true as the MCP manager's registration into ToolRPC is hot, which this slice did not verify. The lineage row stays at \`nerva.context-compaction.v1\`; the boundary note is a log line plus the runtime event, not a field on the row. \`refresh_tools\` compares names, so a tool whose schema changed under the same name is adopted only because the fresh list is taken whenever the set moved, never on its own. The stale comment at \`agents/core/file_tools.py\` saying \`_load_soul\` runs no injection scan predates H387 and is left for a hygiene commit (it would drift five unrelated rows). Nine ledger rows cite \`agent.py\`, \`agent_runtime.py\` or \`orchestrator.py\` and were drifted by this branch (and by #1188 on main: its registry path in \`orchestrator.py\`, and the pairing kwarg, refusal branch and lifespan hand-over in \`telegram.py\` and \`web.py\`, which H146, H449, H364 and H671 also cite and were read against — the pairing store is not their subject); each was re-read against the diff on its cited paths before its hashes moved. Six keep their text and take fresh stamps — H146, H298, H363, H364, H449, H679: the in-turn re-resolution runs through \`_profiled\`, which still applies the job toolset on both sides of the profile, and the result-spill, cache, reasoning and job-pin mechanism those rows cite is untouched. Three are rewritten — H387: the ERROR line now comes from \`_announce_soul_verdict\` behind the \`_read_soul\` builder, for boot and boundary alike; H671 and H673: \`publish()\` re-reads the personas after the clock CAS, and the staged-route budget limit above is now on H673's own row. The 16 other needs_review rows on main (H060, H061, H068, H071, H104, H108, H130, H135, H139, H398, H477, H510, H523, H683 from #1188's edits to \`orchestrator.py\`, \`web.py\` and \`telegram.py\`; H288 and H684 from #1187's \`heartbeat.py\` and \`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md\`) are main's own drift, not this branch's, and are left untouched here; #1187's five-line comment in \`orchestrator.py\` re-drifted the nine rows above once more, and they were re-read against it and re-stamped. [d1](../agents/core/session_refresh.py) [d2](../agents/core/agent.py) [d3](../agents/core/agent_runtime.py) |
| <a id="h673"></a>H673 | Context pressure is measured from the provider's own number, not re-estimated | copy | Parțial | Reevaluat | A model with no num_ctx parameter and no operator configuration remains unresolved (server environment/running context is not probed). Fixed image estimates remain incomplete. Gemini usage-only final frames, absent terminal-local counts, unknown terminal semantics and ambiguous multi-candidate streams deliberately remain unreported. Synthesis/compression/background model calls are deliberately outside conversational usage attribution. Cache discounts/write premiums and undocumented vendor-specific usage mappings are not modeled by the new totals-only adapters. No live provider or billing proof. Responses accounting is limited to supported API-key GPT-4.1 and exact xAI Grok4.5/4.6 dialects, not Codex subscription usage or general OpenAI reasoning-model replay. Cache counts do not establish live discounts, prices or physical context occupancy. Actual specialist planning applies to the existing opt-in shared-compression path; disabled/unmanaged callers retain their existing behavior. Unknown capacity remains a conservative planning estimate, not verified provider metadata. | <a id="h674"></a>H674 | Compression cannot stall the user: bounded holds, inactivity deadlines and derived thresholds | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | A compaction summary no longer holds the owner's turn past a short bound, and a silent summarizer is cut. Hermes keeps two clocks: how long the summary may make the user wait, and how long the summarizer may go quiet. Nerva awaited the strict-local summarizer inline with neither. Its only limit was the httpx read timeout (120 s for Ollama), a total-duration wait on a non-streaming call, and nothing told the owner. (1) Bounded hold. ContextCompressor takes a gate (agents/core/context_compressor.py:325, used at :591). The orchestrator builds it per turn (_summary_gate, agents/core/orchestrator.py:3574, passed at :3741) around SummaryHold (agents/core/compaction_hold.py:136). SummaryHold.wait (:146) waits at most memory.compression_max_turn_hold_seconds for the LLM summary (settings_db.py:246; default 10 s, 0 = never wait, clamped 0..120 by hold_seconds, :63). One summary per session is written at a time: a turn that finds one still in flight neither waits on it again nor starts a second. Past the hold the turn goes on without the summary, and compress marks the result summary_deferred (:616). compact (:519) then sends the turns verbatim, their old images dropped, when they still fit the window under the owner's memory.compression_max_tokens; otherwise it sends the deterministic digest. The summary keeps being written. When it lands it seeds this session's iterative-merge prior (_ctx_summary_cache), but only if no newer turn has replaced the prior this turn started from, and never through the compaction-clock CAS: publish commits only what the prompt was built from, and never writes the digest over the pending summary (orchestrator.py:3796). A turn cancelled while it waits (Stop, a client gone) still leaves its summary for the next turn. A summary that fails later seeds nothing and is logged by name. aclose stops what is still being written within the task budget (orchestrator.py:4481; SummaryHold.aclose, :185). (2) The owner is told. record_turn_notice (agents/core/turn_notices.py:35; a per-turn sink collected like turn_approvals, one notice per code) carries DEFERRED_NOTICE (compaction_hold.py:38) out as notices on /chat (ChatResponse.notices, agents/web.py:1101, TurnNotice :1084) and on every /chat/stream end event, the failed one included (:1356, :1362). The HUD shows each notice as a system line under the reply (frontend/src/turn-notices.ts, app.tsx:339). The hub log says the summary was deferred and, later, that it landed. (3) Inactivity deadline. The summarizer now streams (stream_summary, compaction_hold.py:88, called at orchestrator.py:3566). It is cut after memory.compression_summary_idle_seconds with nothing received (settings_db.py:247; default 60 s, up to 600, no "off": idle_seconds, :71). Any chunk counts as life: a visible token, and a reasoning chunk that shows no text, through the new on_activity callback on the Ollama and LM Studio streams (agents/core/llm/base.py:625, :874). A slow stream that keeps talking is never cut. A cut stream is cancelled and given CLOSE_GRACE (1 s) to close its HTTP response. A backend with no stream is awaited whole. A degraded reply ("⚠️ …", "\[backend error …\]") or an empty one is now a failure, and so the digest is used. Before, the non-streaming generate could hand an error text over as the summary. Tests: tests/test_h674_compaction_hold.py (52) covers both settings, the idle deadline (silent, slow-but-talking, token-only, thinking, degraded, no stream), the hold (in time, at the bound, single flight, zero, cancelled wait, late failure, failure within the hold, aclose), the compressor's deferral (digest, verbatim when it fits, owner's budget, covered with a prior), and the orchestrator: the turn going on at the hold, the late summary seeding the next turn, a newer prior never overwritten, no clock commit for the deferred summary, the idle setting reaching the summarizer, aclose. It also covers the notice on /chat and every stream end event, the collector unbound after /chat, and on_activity on both local streams. frontend src/test/turn-notices.test.ts covers the HUD. 72 mutants: 70 caught after nine survivors got cases. Two were equivalent. m23 (busy ignoring done()) is equivalent because the done callback always pops the flight first. The other was a redundant typeof check in the HUD helper, which was removed. The phase-2, H20.3 and compaction-policy suites pass unchanged. The AUD-7 and H677 end-event and reply pins gain the notices key.
