| <a id="h677"></a>H677 | Boot warm-up before accepting work, and every shutdown wait has a short explicit budget | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Boot now waits for the local model before it accepts work, and every shutdown wait has a short explicit budget. (1) Warm-up gate. load_agents still starts LLMRouter.warm_up as a background task (agents/core/orchestrator.py:928). The web lifespan now waits for it before orch.start_channels(): gate_warmup (agents/core/lifecycle_budget.py:102, called at agents/web.py:627). The wait is bounded by system.startup_warmup_timeout_seconds (settings_db.py:361; default 20 s, 0 = do not wait, clamped to 0..300 by warmup_timeout, lifecycle_budget.py:37; a bad value keeps the default). The HTTP app is not served before the lifespan yields, so /chat waits too. On expiry the gate logs the setting's name, opens anyway, and the warm-up finishes in the background; the gate never cancels it. WARMUP (WarmupState, :51, :99) holds off / warming / ready / cold (warm_up answered False: nothing local to warm) / failed (raised or cancelled), the gate_expired flag, and the seconds it took (frozen when it ends). GET /api/status (routers/status.py:120) and /readyz (routers/ops.py:143) report it; it is never a reason to be not-ready. A turn served while it is still warming is marked: ChatResponse.warming (web.py:1098, :1250), every end event of the stream, the failed turn's included (:1356, :1362), and a log line for channel turns (orchestrator.py:1597). (2) Shutdown budgets. bounded (lifecycle_budget.py:128) runs one step for at most its budget, logs an overrun or an error by name and moves on; a step that ignores cancellation is abandoned, never awaited. wait_task (:147) cancels a background task and waits at most its budget. ChannelManager.stop_all bounds each channel's stop at 3 s (channels/manager.py:126). _cancel_task bounds each cancelled task at 2 s (orchestrator.py:1396). stop_channels bounds the Oracle watcher and plugin close_all (:1416, :1419). aclose bounds the checkpoint flush, each context-cache task, the warm-up task, the LLM router, the Ollama client, MCP, the context cache, the plugin HTTP clients and each channel's aclose (:4470-4519). So one wedged step costs its budget, not the service manager's stop budget. The budgets are per step; there is no total deadline, so several wedged steps still add up. (3) Per-chat lanes. ChatLanes (agents/core/channels/chat_lanes.py:24) runs a chat's turns in order and different chats side by side, at most 8 turns at once. A failed turn never stalls its lane. drain (:66) gives the lanes a bounded time at stop, cancels what is left and names how many it abandoned. Telegram's poll loop creates the lanes (channels/telegram.py:420) and hands each batched turn (_deliver_turn, :543), each button tap (:454) and each observed group line (:500) to its chat's lane through _in_chat (:548). Work with no chat waits for every lane first (settle), so H117's order holds: what a chat said before a tap is handled first. The loop goes straight back to reading, so one slow answer, or a 180 s lease wait, no longer holds other chats. stop() and the loop's exit drain the lanes within 2 s (LANE_DRAIN_BUDGET, :53). The voice-reply mark (H071 "voice for voice") is taken when a note is read, while the chat's previous turn may still be running; the note's turn now carries it into its lane (_voice_pending, :236, :712; _deliver_turn; _run_turn, :561) and sets it only when it runs, so the earlier, typed, reply is never the one spoken. Tests: tests/test_h677_boot_and_shutdown.py (51) covers the setting, every gate phase, the lifespan order, status/readyz, the /chat and stream marks, the channel-turn log, bounded and wait_task, a hung channel stop, a stubborn task, a hung aclose step, a source pin that no teardown await is bare, lane order, concurrency, failures, bound, drain and settle, Telegram reading the next update while a chat is answered, and a voice note read while its chat is busy keeping its own spoken reply. The H117 batching and Telegram suites pass unchanged. 84 mutants, all caught after cases were added (a cancelled or None-returning warm-up, the frozen duration, a zero budget, cold vs failed, an abandoned step's cancel and late error, lane order after an earlier turn ends, drain cancelling, the poll loop's own lanes, a tap in another chat, cache-task cleanup, the warm-up cancelled at close, the budgets nesting); one was equivalent (asyncio.shield around asyncio.wait, which never cancels) and the shield was removed. Building the lanes exposed a regression they would have introduced (the voice mark taken by the busy chat's earlier reply), fixed red-first. | <a id="h683"></a>H683 | Chat replies stream, can be edited or deleted, and acknowledge receipt at ingest | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | (1) No edit or delete in the base contract: ChannelAdapter (agents/core/channels/base.py:14) still declares only abstract start/stop/send plus receive, and no adapter implements a delete operation (no delete_message or deleteMessage anywhere in agents/), so a sealed streamed preview cannot be removed and there is no base method that fails loudly for a channel that cannot edit; the fallback to a whole reply is silent. (2) Slack and Discord do not stream: both descriptors declare supports_edit=True (agents/core/channels/slack.py:102, agents/core/channels/discord.py:32) but neither adapter implements begin_stream or an edit call (Slack's send posts via chat_postMessage only, slack.py:333), and _begin_channel_draft refuses both because a streamed draft would publish tokens before the channel.reply approval; streaming there needs an approval contract that covers output not yet generated. (3) No acknowledgement at ingest: Telegram sends a typing chat action only while reading an inbound attachment (telegram.py:699) and a record_voice action before a spoken reply, so an ordinary text message gets no receipt signal until the first streamed token or the whole reply; Slack and Discord send none (the Slack Socket Mode envelope ack is a transport ack to Slack, not a signal the sender sees). | <a id="h687"></a>H687 | A recurring instruction runs once immediately, then on its cadence | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Built on this branch and reworked after two reviews. A recurring instruction now runs once when it is created, then on its cadence, with Hermes' split kept: - Hermes' /loop is an interval. LoopManager.set() starts it with next_due_at=now (hermes_cli/loops.py:466), and the reply says the first wakeup fires now. - A Hermes cron job waits for its first computed run. Nerva applies the /loop behaviour to its cron-style jobs where the schedule is an interval. Before this change, a new Nerva job always waited for its first slot, and every confirmation named only the schedule. Every creation surface goes through JobRunner.arm (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1295): - POST /api/jobs (agents/core/routers/jobs.py:109); - the /remind slash command (agents/core/commands.py:275); - \`nerva jobs create\`, which posts to that route and prints the hub's confirmation (agents/cli/nerva.py:1404); - the HUD arm and JobCreateDialog notes (armNote, frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:37, used at frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:152 and frontend/src/panels/jobs.tsx:309). JobRunner.create is a thin wrapper around it (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1293). first_run_policy (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:543) decides what runs before its first slot. Only an agent-instruction job does (ask, brief or task, or a script or monitor source), and only on an interval cadence. is_interval_cron (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:517) reads what the cron fires on, not how it is written: every hour with one minute or the minutes of a \`\*/N\`, or one minute in the hours of a \`\*/N\`, with no weekday, day or month restriction. So '0 0-23/2' and '0 0,12' count as '0 \*/2' and '0 \*/12', while two times of day ('0 9,21') and '\*/5 \*/2' (only in even hours) are calendar schedules. These wait for their first slot: - a calendar schedule (a 07:00 morning brief armed in the evening, a weekday job armed on Saturday); - a reminder; - a repeat-limited job, whose early run would spend one of its few attempts (critic note 15); - anything during quiet hours, whose output would be held and delivered after the next fresh run's; - anything whose first slot is near (first_run_margin, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:538): nearer than two minutes or than a quarter of the cadence (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:485-486), read from the real cron in the scheduler's timezone (slot_timing, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1329). "every 12 hours" armed 45 minutes before its noon slot waits for it instead of running twice within the hour. first_run_decision (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:580) applies it at creation. A paused or disabled job never fires, and the create call's \`first_run\` (StrictBool, agents/core/routers/jobs.py:34; \`--first-run/--no-first-run\`, agents/cli/nerva.py:210) overrides the policy either way. It is a creation-time choice, never a stored option: validate_options refuses it, so PATCH cannot store it and no rollback trips on it. The first run is a dispatch request drained by _fire_once (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1442) under the shared job gate (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1320; ORIGINS and the column at agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:12 and agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:40). Its origin is 'first_run' when the policy chose it and 'first_run_asked' when the create call asked for it. - The request is durable, so the drain checks a policy first run against first_run_policy again when it comes to run (first_run_due, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1345; agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:214). By then the job may have been edited into a calendar schedule, a reminder or a repeat-limited job, quiet hours may have begun, or a scheduler that was down may have left its first slot near. The request is then cancelled and names the reason. An asked first run is not second-guessed. - The drain runs both with force=False (agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:223), so _fire_once applies the pause check (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1455), the emergency stop (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1461), option and action validation, the repeat reservation (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1480; for scripts, agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py:123-124) and the quiet-hours hold on delivery (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1701; for scripts, agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py:433-435). - Only the owner's own ▶ now runs a paused job. When ▶ now folds into a queued first run, it makes that request manual (agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:86). - Pausing cancels a not-yet-claimed first run of either origin (cancel_queued, agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py:107, called at agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1414). - An edit that changes what runs (action or options) queues the first run again for the edited job, keeping its origin, instead of letting the claim's identity check drop it (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1393; _requeue_first_run, agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:1397). A rename or a new schedule leaves it queued. Either way a policy first run meets the policy again at the drain. If the queue refuses the new request, the edit is still saved and says why in the log. If the enqueue is refused at creation (the queue is at capacity, or the database is locked), the answer is still 201 with first_run null and 'first run not queued (…)', because the job is armed and a retry would duplicate it. POST /api/jobs returns the receipt as first_run, or null, plus one confirmation built by arm_confirmation (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:604): - 'first run now, then \<schedule\> (\<cron\>)', or 'first run queued, then …' with a note that nothing fires while the scheduler is not running (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:614); - '\<schedule\> (\<cron\>), on its cadence', with the quiet-hours or near-slot reason when that was the cause. /remind, the CLI and the HUD print it. Tests: - tests/test_job_first_run.py, 74 cases (41 before the second review). 28 of them fail on ccdd7161, with its fixture pointed at that commit's slot helper: - an interval ask job is queued and drained into one run before any slot (tests/test_job_first_run.py:109); - a calendar schedule waits (tests/test_job_first_run.py:123); - the emergency stop (tests/test_job_first_run.py:133); - pause cancels the first run, and one that slips past the pause is still skipped (tests/test_job_first_run.py:143, tests/test_job_first_run.py:155), an asked one too (tests/test_job_first_run.py:451); - ▶ now keeps its force (tests/test_job_first_run.py:164); - edits: one to the action runs the edited job (tests/test_job_first_run.py:176), one the policy would not fire drops it (tests/test_job_first_run.py:387), an asked one survives (tests/test_job_first_run.py:439), a rename leaves it alone (tests/test_job_first_run.py:473), and a refused re-queue still saves (tests/test_job_first_run.py:483); - at the drain: quiet hours that began (tests/test_job_first_run.py:402), a slot now near (tests/test_job_first_run.py:416), and an asked first run that runs anyway (tests/test_job_first_run.py:429); - first_run is a creation choice, never stored (tests/test_job_first_run.py:210); - a refused enqueue and a locked database (tests/test_job_first_run.py:229, tests/test_job_first_run.py:499); - quiet hours, the near slot, a long cadence near its slot and no scheduler (tests/test_job_first_run.py:242, tests/test_job_first_run.py:251, tests/test_job_first_run.py:462, tests/test_job_first_run.py:261); - an interval script job submits its first run (tests/test_job_first_run.py:273); - the interval table, the decision table and the slot timing on the real cron (tests/test_job_first_run.py:306, tests/test_job_first_run.py:339, tests/test_job_first_run.py:520); - both CLI flags (tests/test_job_first_run.py:353, tests/test_job_first_run.py:363). - tests/test_nerva_cli.py:1236 and tests/test_slash_commands.py:317. - frontend/src/test/jobs-first-run.test.ts (armNote). - tests/test_jobs_routes.py: a new reminder still sends nothing until /run, and the script job's /run is again a fresh manual request. Every guard added in the second review was mutated out one at a time, and a test failed each time. 656 job, dispatch, command, CLI and schedule tests pass. One-shot run_at jobs (H450) never take a first run, even when one is asked for: first_run_policy and first_run_decision answer "one-shot" (agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py:563, :589). The H450 row says they must stay out of the first run. A known pre-existing gap outside this row: parse_schedule drops a window or day qualifier after an interval ("every 30 minutes between 9 and 17" becomes '\*/30 \* \* \* \*'), so such a job runs all day and counts as an interval. [d1](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs.py) [d2](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs_dispatch.py) [d3](../agents/core/autonomy/jobs_scripts.py) |
| <a id="h688"></a>H688 | Config keys whose NAME contains a dot can be addressed | skip | Exclus intenționat | Audit 07.09 | Preluat din auditul 07.09; nereevaluat în această livrare.  |
| <a id="h689"></a>H689 | One stable identity for this install, and per-profile isolation of its runtime artifacts | copy | Echivalent | Reevaluat | Nerva has a durable install identity, one hub per data root and isolated profiles, as Hermes does. agents/core/install_identity.py keeps one 32-hex id in \<data root\>/install_id (install_id). It is minted once under an in-process threading lock and a cross-process file lock (fcntl.flock, or msvcrt.locking on Windows, on install_id.lock). It is written atomically: mkstemp, fsync, os.replace, then a directory fsync. It is read back before it is cached and returned. A file holding anything but an id, one that cannot be read, or a write that fails answers None, never a fresh value, and the file is left as it is. The consumers use it. agents/core/first_action.py stores it as the activation clock's install_id; mark_installed no longer re-mints over an unreadable or foreign record, and infer_install_at_boot and record_first_action never write over one. agents/core/node_mesh.py issues node grants as node:\<id\>@\<install\> and keeps hub_id on the record. agents/core/channels/pairing.py stamps each deeplink with hub, and redeem_deeplink refuses another install's (other_install). agents/core/satellite_hub.py gives SatellitePairing a validated hub_id, and SatelliteHub refuses a pairing pinned to another install (other_install, read lazily through _own_hub_id). serve.py stops on a bad JARVIS_PROFILE (paths.profile_error). It holds \<data root\>/hub.lock for the life of the process (acquire_hub_lock), so a second hub on the same root stops with HubAlreadyRunning naming the holder's pid, and it prints the data root and install id. agents/core/paths.py gives JARVIS_PROFILE a meaning. A valid name (profile_name: lowercase letters, digits, - or _, up to 32; "default" is the default root) makes data_root \<root\>/profiles/\<name\>. Settings, memory, the install id and lock, and the credentials follow it; agents/core/secrets.py resolves DEFAULT_STORE under the root the process starts with. It is classified in env_provenance READ_BEFORE_LOAD and FROM_PROCESS_ONLY. Tests: tests/test_h689_install_identity.py (43), including racing processes that agree on one id and a real second-process lock refusal; tests/test_first_run_first_action.py pins the no-re-mint rule; 32 mutants, 30 killed, 2 fixed. [d1](../agents/core/install_identity.py) [d2](../agents/core/paths.py) [d3](../agents/core/first_action.py) |
| <a id="h690"></a>H690 | OS permission grants survive an interpreter upgrade (macOS TCC anchoring) | copy | Lipsă | Audit 07.09 | Reevaluare pe cod; cerințele și lipsurile sunt în rândul original.  |
| <a id="h691"></a>H691 | Reverse-proxy trust is a validated, bounded allowlist rather than an on/off switch | update | Echivalent | Reevaluat | JARVIS_TRUSTED_PROXIES is the only allowlist that decides whose forwarding headers count: X-Forwarded-For / X-Real-IP and X-Forwarded-Proto alike. Parsing and refusals: parse_trusted_proxies (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:134) reads each entry with ip_network(strict=False) (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:160), so a bare address becomes its own /32 or /128. It refuses '\*', 0.0.0.0/0 and ::/0 by name (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:107), any prefix length 0 (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:165), unreadable entries and more than 64 entries, naming the variable and the position, never the value. A malformed list refuses boot through assert_parseable_trusted_proxies (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:411), whose message tells the operator to list the proxy's own address or a bounded CIDR range with no wildcard; it runs in enforce_boot_posture (agents/core/boot_guards.py:350), which the lifespan calls after the .env files load (agents/web.py:456-463, H273), and assert_front_door runs it over a caller's own mapping (agents/core/boot_guards.py:318). That is stricter than Hermes, which drops a bad entry and carries on. At request time a malformed list fails closed and is said once (agents/web.py:166). The default trusts nothing, not even loopback; the legacy JARVIS_TRUSTED_PROXY flag means loopback only (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:206). One trust set: uvicorn's own proxy-header layer used to rewrite the client address and scheme from loopback, or from whatever FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS named, before the app looked. serve.server_config now builds uvicorn with proxy_headers=False (serve.py:99) and records that choice (serve.py:101). docker-compose.yml passes --no-proxy-headers (docker-compose.yml:13); the Dockerfile, the systemd unit, start.sh, START.bat and the packaged build already start through serve.py. The app reads X-Forwarded-Proto itself, only from a listed peer and only when there is exactly one http/https/ws/wss value (forwarded_proto, agents/core/proxy_trust.py:389, applied by _ForwardedProtoMiddleware, agents/web.py:200, registered at agents/web.py:678), so a TLS proxy still yields https:// base URLs for the MCP resource and the widget snippet. The scheme is set on the scope in place, as uvicorn's layer did, so the route the router records reaches the golden-signals middleware and a forwarded request keeps its per-route metrics label (agents/web.py:231, tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:582). assert_server_proxy_layer_within_trust (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:570) runs in the same boot pass (agents/core/boot_guards.py:353) and in assert_front_door (agents/core/boot_guards.py:319). It refuses FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS, UVICORN_FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS, or --forwarded-allow-ips on a uvicorn command line when an entry is '\*', a /0, not an address, or a peer outside JARVIS_TRUSTED_PROXIES plus uvicorn's loopback default. Gates: the localhost auth bypass (_real_client_host, agents/web.py:176) and the HF-2 rate-limit key (_client_ip, agents/web.py:359) resolve the client through forwarded_client, which trusts only a listed peer and walks the chain right to left. The HF-2 localhost exemption now follows the localhost gate's origin (agents/web.py:778), so an unlisted same-box proxy, or a listed one whose chain vouches for nothing, is not waved through. Logging: web.py's import-time read is silent (agents/web.py:158). The lifespan calls announce_trusted_proxies after setup_logging and the .env load (agents/web.py:519). When anything is trusted it logs one INFO line naming the source and the canonical networks (_log_trust_set, agents/core/proxy_trust.py:253), together with the wide-entry and legacy warnings that belong with it, which are repeated when the set was resolved before logging existed (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:294). Nothing is logged for the default. It also names uvicorn's layer whenever that layer believes anyone (agents/core/proxy_trust.py:528): on a raw uvicorn start without --no-proxy-headers, or under a launcher it cannot identify. Tests: tests/test_trusted_proxies.py covers parsing and refusal (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:58, tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:74), the LAN-peer loopback spoof (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:183), the INFO line (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:295), the lifespan wiring run through the real lifespan (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:434), the silent import (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:416), and the served config with uvicorn's ProxyHeadersMiddleware in front, where a LAN peer sends X-Forwarded-For 127.0.0.1 under FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS='\*' (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:506). It also covers the throttle exemption (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:534), X-Forwarded-Proto believed only from a listed peer (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:562), and the uvicorn-allowlist refusals (tests/test_trusted_proxies.py:624). tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py pins the new guard in the boot pass and in the mapping check (tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py:148, tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py:200), and that the lifespan loads the .env files before the boot pass (tests/test_o26_f6_boot_guards.py:218). tests/test_rate_limit_hf2.py and tests/test_admin_guard_hf7.py cover the two gates. Operator documentation is in docs/FLAGS.md. Known limits, which the row does not require: a raw python -m uvicorn agents.web:app without --no-proxy-headers (the CI openapi, soak and smoke jobs, and dev use) still lets uvicorn believe loopback's forwarding headers, which is Hermes's own default. On that raw start uvicorn's loopback walk can also rewrite a loopback peer into a listed proxy's address, after which Nerva's walk trusts the rest of the chain; using it needs the raw start, a list naming a non-loopback proxy and a same-box unlisted proxy that passes the client's X-Forwarded-For through verbatim. The guard caps that layer at the Nerva list plus loopback, and the boot names it at INFO. A launcher that embeds uvicorn some other way passes its allowlist out of the app's sight. The RFC 7239 Forwarded header is not parsed. Behaviour change: a same-box proxy that is not listed no longer gets per-client throttle keys or https:// base URLs through uvicorn's implicit loopback trust until it is listed (127.0.0.1/32). [d1](../agents/core/proxy_trust.py) [d2](../agents/web.py) [d3](../agents/core/boot_guards.py) |

| <a id="h696"></a>H696 | A small default skill bundle with a large on-demand catalog, re-pinned to the current release | update | Parțial | Reevaluat | 1. Re-pin to upstream's current release (v2026.8.31 or later). Read the bytes, record sha256, execute nothing, and keep the fail-before-network posture. 2. Pin format. The pin must accept the optional-skills/ root, which agents/core/skills/importer.py:113 currently rejects. Each entry must digest-verify and import the skill's reference files as well as SKILL.md, because the consolidated github skill and the pdf and AgentMail skills keep their per-workflow detail in references. 3. Catalog content. The re-pinned catalog must expose the consolidated github skill and grill-me, and drop the merged/deleted skills. 4. Move importer constants, tests and the doc-consistency check to the new tag.
